# Material 3 Expressive polish research

Started 2026-11-09. This folder records the official source, the package path, and the result of each review. A component is complete only after its native API, geometry, color, motion, interaction, accessibility, and host integration have been checked.

## AndroidX release audit — 2026-11-11

- The [official Compose Material 3 release table](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/compose-material3) still lists 1.4.0 as stable and 1.5.0-rc01 (2026-11-04) as the latest release candidate. `android/build.gradle` already pins `material3:1.5.0-rc01`, so no newer native Material 3 artifact is available to adopt today. The RC includes fixes for `ToggleButton` pressed-shape tokens and slider/range-slider precision and overlap; these remain owned by the actual Compose components in the package. This version audit is current as of the date above and must be repeated before publication.
- The [official `carouselParallaxScrollEffect` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/carouselParallaxScrollEffect.composable) explicitly prescribes a `LazyRow` or `LazyColumn` for variable-size items; dedicated carousel composables require uniform item size. The package's multi-aspect path already uses that official modifier with `CarouselParallaxScrollEffectState` on a lazy list, while hero, multi-browse and uncontained routes use the real dedicated carousel composables. This is native Material 3 behavior, though it is not a dedicated multi-aspect composable. Device parallax, clipping and scroll checks remain pending.
- The same [official multi-aspect API sample](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/carouselParallaxScrollEffect.composable) sets 8 dp item spacing and 16 dp content padding on its `LazyRow`. The package uses those values for multi-aspect items. The newer [`CarouselDefaults` source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/%2B/e1eb0a46d5b53164d02603d0125bf45331c1e7fc%5E2..e1eb0a46d5b53164d02603d0125bf45331c1e7fc/) defines `ItemSpacing` and `ContentPadding` as zero; those tokens are used by the package's dedicated carousel composables but would erase the sample's intentional multi-aspect gaps if substituted into the lazy list. The public `AdaptiveCarousel` documentation already distinguishes the two defaults. This audit required no code or build change.

## Vertical slider

- The official [Compose `VerticalSlider` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/VerticalSlider.composable) is present in the pinned Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 but the package exposed only a horizontal slider. `AdaptiveSlider.vertical` now reserves caller-supplied height and at least 48 dp width, forwards `topToBottom` (true by default) to the real state-based Compose control, and reuses the existing normalized range, tick, colors and start/change/end event bridge. Its thumb uses the official `SliderDefaults.Thumb(isVertical = true)` overload so the native vertical shape and size token remain intact; optional labels remain anchored by Compose `Label`. Other platforms orient their existing Flutter/Cupertino slider because no UIKit vertical slider is exposed by this package.
- Four new bridge tests first failed because the named constructor was absent. The final five vertical tests and thirteen existing horizontal tests pass, including host geometry, direction, range callbacks, disabled state and fallback. A one-worker offline Kotlin compile passed with a 768 MiB heap after a process/memory preflight; targeted Dart analysis, formatting and diff checks pass. Physical vertical drag direction, focus, TalkBack range and label placement still need device checks.

## Expressive filter chips

- Official [`FilterChip`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FilterChip.composable) and [`ElevatedFilterChip`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ElevatedFilterChip.composable) expose `ChipShapes`; [`FilterChipDefaults.shapes()`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FilterChipDefaults) supplies normal, selected and pressed shapes. The package now exposes a standalone `AdaptiveFilterChip` that delegates those states, ripple, color, focus and selection semantics to the real Compose control. Its text and icon are serializable; the selected checkmark is decorative because the chip itself carries the selected state. Flutter's Material `FilterChip` remains the other-platform route.
- Official `FilterChip` and `ElevatedFilterChip` also expose a `trailingIcon` slot. `AdaptiveFilterChip` now forwards an optional serializable icon to that real Compose slot in both variants. Its Flutter fallback uses bounded, ellipsized label text beside the trailing icon; a narrow-width test verifies no overflow. Six bridge/fallback tests, full Dart analysis and capped Kotlin compilation pass. Native long-label/font-scale geometry and device checks for pressed/selected morph, touch, keyboard and TalkBack remain pending.
- Official [`InputChip`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/InputChip.composable) exposes a separate `ChipShapes` overload and gives an avatar precedence over a leading icon. The [Compose chip guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/chip) demonstrates dismissal by pressing the whole chip and a trailing close icon in its native slot. `AdaptiveInputChip` now supports that action alongside controlled selection as mutually exclusive modes. It uses `InputChipDefaults.shapes()`, native avatar/icon sizes, optional serializable image bytes, and Compose's default tonal colors, border, padding and elevation. The avatar slot stays allocated during asynchronous decoding and its bitmap uses circular crop, avoiding a width jump. `dismissLabel` defaults to the app's localized Material delete tooltip. Flutter's disabled fallback keeps the delete icon visible but inactive using `InputChip.isEnabled`. Seven focused bridge/fallback tests, full Flutter analysis and capped Kotlin compilation pass. Image decoding, actual selected/pressed motion, touch and TalkBack remain device checks.
- Flutter 3.47.9 `InputChip`'s M3 default sets `showCheckmark: true`, whereas the official Compose `InputChip` relies on its explicit leading/avatar slots and does not add one automatically. The other-platform fallback now sets `showCheckmark: false`; the selected, dismissible fallback test checks this property. This is a visual parity adjustment, not a claim of identical Flutter/Compose animation.
- Official [`AssistChip`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AssistChip.composable) and [`SuggestionChip`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SuggestionChip.composable) use a single `shape` parameter, unlike filter/input chips' `ChipShapes`. Their elevated variants are separate Compose components. `AdaptiveAssistChip` and `AdaptiveSuggestionChip` now route actions to the real flat/elevated controls with native icon sizes, default colors, borders, elevation, padding, ripple and focus. A narrow Flutter fallback initially overflowed with a trailing icon; its focused test failed, then passed after constraining the text with ellipsis. Five focused tests, full Flutter analysis and capped Kotlin compilation pass. Actual device geometry, ripple, hover, focus and TalkBack are still pending; the native API does not promise shape morph on press for these types.

## Motion and accessibility

- Native views use an ordinary Android `ComposeView` and its window recomposer. [AndroidX's window recomposer](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/c26796f468659c82dd22f29aa0eb05301ad65fd7/compose/ui/ui/src/androidMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/platform/WindowRecomposer.android.kt) observes the system animator duration scale and supplies it as `MotionDurationScale`; a zero scale finishes Compose motion on the next frame. The package's `MaterialExpressiveTheme(MotionScheme.expressive())` chooses the official springs, while the system scale governs their playback. The Flutter wrapper no longer disables all descendant tickers when `MediaQuery.disableAnimations` is true: [Flutter's `AnimationController.animationBehavior`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/animation/AnimationController/animationBehavior.html) already shortens ordinary transitions, while a disabled [`TickerMode`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/TickerMode-class.html) can freeze them. Verify both engines on a device with Android's Remove animations setting and with a nondefault animator scale before closing the motion gate.
- Rechecked the pinned Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 `MotionScheme.kt`: [`MotionScheme.expressive()`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/MotionScheme) returns a singleton, so passing it through every package-owned `M3EAndroidTheme` does not allocate a new spring scheme on each recomposition. A native animation scan found only the app-bar/search snap specs from Compose defaults, shape rotations from `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.defaultSpatialSpec()`, progress specs from the indicator defaults, the official [loading-indicator example spring](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LoadingIndicator.composable), and the caller's optional carousel tap duration. The remaining Flutter-host scroll/route motion and device frame pacing still need separate review; this source audit does not prove runtime visual behavior.

## Theme color precedence

- Official sources: [Compose Material 3 color guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/designsystems/material3), [Compose Material 3 API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/package-summary).
- Package paths: `lib/src/widgets/android/android_m3e_platform_view.dart`, `lib/src/theme/adaptive_material_expressive_theme.dart`, and `android/src/main/kotlin/com/ledexsoft/adaptive_liquid_glass/M3EAndroidContent.kt`.
- Confirmed mismatch: the Dart bridge always serialized every Flutter `ColorScheme` role. Native code first obtained the Android 12+ wallpaper palette when `dynamicColoring` was true, then `withAppColorScheme` overwrote every role with the Flutter palette. The app bar also supplied Flutter surface/on-surface colors unconditionally.
- The official 1.5 API provides `expressiveLightColorScheme()` and recommends `darkColorScheme()` for dark mode; the current fallback follows that guidance. The fix belongs in palette precedence at the Flutter/Compose boundary, not in a new dark-scheme function.
- Current change: the bridge sends the full host `ColorScheme` when `dynamicColoring` is false. The app bar uses native theme colors unless `tintColor` is explicitly supplied. On API 24–30 with dynamic coloring requested, a complete expressive fallback is generated from the host primary seed. The wrapper's documented `dynamicColoring: false` switch still selects the full host color scheme on every supported Android version.
- The earlier API 24–30 fallback paired a custom primary with black/white `onPrimary` chosen at luminance 0.5. That threshold could choose white for a midtone such as `#e57373` (2.99:1), whereas black gives 7.03:1. The direct native fallback now chooses the higher-contrast foreground at the exact black/white crossover near 0.179 luminance. The full expressive scheme bridge below supersedes this pair for package-created views; the local correction still protects direct native calls without a serialized scheme. Sources: [Material 3 color roles](https://m3.material.io/styles/color/the-color-system), [Compose M3 color schemes](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/designsystems/material3), and [WCAG contrast calculation](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG21/Understanding/contrast-minimum).
- The pinned Compose 1.5.0-rc01 `expressiveLightColorScheme()` has no seed argument: its source calls `lightColorScheme()` with four expressive container text overrides. Flutter 3.47.9 contains Material Color Utilities' `SchemeExpressive` through `ColorScheme.fromSeed(dynamicSchemeVariant: DynamicSchemeVariant.expressive)`, and [Material Color Utilities](https://github.com/material-foundation/material-color-utilities/blob/main/dev_guide/creating_color_scheme.md) documents deriving a complete scheme from one source. The bridge now computes that complete light/dark fallback scheme from the host primary, serializes its color roles and applies it in native Compose only when the requested Android wallpaper scheme is unavailable below API 31. The Android 12+ palette remains native wallpaper-derived; `dynamicColoring: false` retains the app's exact host scheme. A focused bridge test failed on the missing fallback map and passed after implementation, alongside the explicit-scheme case. The generated scheme and serialized role map are cached per platform view to avoid recalculation and redundant channel updates on rebuild. The actual Compose palette and contrast in light/dark modes still need device review.
- A bridge-key audit found 48 shared roles with no missing Compose role. Flutter also sends `shadow`, which Compose's `ColorScheme` does not expose. The expressive scheme can rotate the primary hue away from the seed by design; callers needing their exact brand scheme use `dynamicColoring: false`. Flutter child content in hybrid surfaces still uses the host `ThemeData`, so native/Flutter color pairing on pre-Android-12 devices needs visual review for custom themes.
- Legacy color-role fidelity: Flutter's [`surfaceVariant`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ColorScheme/surfaceVariant.html), `background`, and `onBackground` are deprecated but remain separately configurable; Compose's [`ColorScheme`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ColorScheme) still exposes all three. The bridge had replaced them with `surfaceContainerHighest`, `surface`, and `onSurface`, discarding explicit app values. It now sends each Flutter role to its matching Compose role. A focused regression failed on the variant before the change; the expanded three-role regression, all six palette bridge tests, targeted analysis, and Dart formatting pass afterward. Dynamic wallpaper colors on Android 12+ still come from Compose; device palette comparison remains open.
- Alert-dialog semantic action colors had a separate contrast gap: `success` and `warning` used fixed green/orange text, while the actual Compose [`AlertDialogDefaults.containerColor`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AlertDialogDefaults) comes from the theme's `surfaceContainerHigh` token. The orange has only 3.11:1 contrast on white, and the green has 3.63:1 on a representative near-black surface. The native route now keeps each semantic hue when it already meets 4.5:1 and otherwise adjusts it toward black or white against the current dialog container. Standard and destructive actions still use Compose's theme roles. [Material color roles](https://m3.material.io/styles/color/the-color-system) and [WCAG text contrast](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG21/Understanding/contrast-minimum) informed this local override. Wallpaper palettes, typography and the perceived hue still need device review.
- An explicit `AdaptiveMaterialExpressiveTheme.seedColor` previously changed only the Flutter `primary` role, while the Android 7–11 bridge generated a complete expressive fallback from that primary. A focused test showed the Flutter and Compose roles diverged. The wrapper now builds a complete `ColorScheme.fromSeed(..., expressive)` when no explicit light/dark theme takes precedence, and passes that same scheme through its inherited scope to native views. The test failed before this correction and passed for primary, on-primary, secondary and surface roles afterward. A second test showed that a selected `lightTheme` was also reduced to its primary on the native fallback; the scope now forwards its full custom `ColorScheme` instead. Explicit `dynamicColoring: false` still sends the effective Flutter scheme. Android 12+ native wallpaper colors intentionally remain independent of a Flutter seed or custom theme; exact brand color use requires opting out of dynamic coloring. Dark theme selection and on-device palette pairing remain to verify.
- `followSystemBrightness: true` previously read the platform brightness but left a fixed parent `ThemeData` untouched when no matching explicit `darkTheme`/`lightTheme` was supplied. The native bridge then received the wrong `isDark` flag and a light fallback under a dark system. A focused widget test failed on `Brightness.light` in that case. The wrapper now derives a full expressive scheme from the explicit seed or host primary whenever effective system brightness differs from the parent theme, and forwards that same scheme to Compose. The five-case palette suite passes; runtime brightness changes and on-device wallpaper/host pairing remain to verify.
- Still to verify: wallpaper and host palettes in light/dark mode on Android 12+, fallback roles on API 24–30, and explicitly tinted app bars on a device. The current wrapper cannot distinguish an explicit host `lightTheme`/`darkTheme` from its inherited theme when dynamic coloring remains enabled; callers who want the host scheme must set `dynamicColoring: false`.
- Additional native routes forced Flutter colors: `AdaptiveCard` passed a Flutter surface-container color to `NativeM3ECardSurface` even when `color` was absent; the default info snackbar passed a fixed dark gray and white. [Compose CardDefaults](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/CardDefaults) exposes theme-aware defaults, and the [M3 snackbar overview](https://m3.material.io/components/snackbar/overview/) includes dynamic-color mappings. Current change: an ordinary filled card with no explicit color or elevation uses native `CardDefaults.cardColors()` and `CardDefaults.cardElevation()`; explicit caller values still win. The Android glass fallback uses native `surfaceContainerHigh` and its existing 1 dp elevation. Neutral snackbars use `SnackbarDefaults` colors, including the separate action color, unless the host `SnackBarThemeData` supplies colors; success/warning/error styles retain their semantic overrides.
- Still to verify on a device: card child text/background contrast with wallpaper colors, the snackbar action color, and light/dark semantic snackbar variants. The card child remains an arbitrary Flutter widget, so Compose cannot automatically recolor its text.
- Card interaction bridge: `AdaptiveCard` overlays an arbitrary Flutter child and handles `onTap` in Flutter over a native Compose `Card` surface. The pinned 1.5.0-rc01 clickable [`Card` overload](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Card.composable) and official [Compose interaction guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/touch-input/user-interactions/handling-interactions) connect paired `PressInteraction.Press` / `Release` / `Cancel` to the real card ripple and pressed elevation through `MutableInteractionSource`. Flutter now forwards a transient press ID, local touch position, release and cancellation over the existing per-view method channel; Compose converts logical pixels to physical pixels and emits the interactions on the native clickable `Card`. The native view buffers rapid events until its composition collects them. Flutter retains the tap callback and child hit testing; the native card clears its separate semantics action. An early active press is replayed when the platform view appears, while a tap completed before creation is never replayed. Because the native card is behind the Flutter child, opaque child content can cover the native ripple; a device check must establish where it is actually visible. The five focused interaction tests and capped Kotlin compilation pass, but no Android device is attached, so real ripple timing, pressed elevation, accessibility and child taps still require runtime inspection.
- Hover follow-up: Compose's [interaction guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/touch-input/user-interactions/handling-interactions) pairs `HoverInteraction.Enter` with an `Exit` referring to the same enter event. Flutter's `MouseRegion` now forwards entry and exit to the same native card interaction source and supplies a click cursor. The active hover is replayed if the platform view appears after pointer entry and cancelled when `onTap` becomes null or the card is disposed. Focus from Flutter children is still not bridged. The hover bridge test failed before the change and passes afterward; the capped Kotlin compile passes. Native hover visuals and focus order still require a device.
- Keyboard follow-up: the native clickable `Card` had an empty click callback because the arbitrary child and actual `onTap` live in Flutter. Compose's [focus guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/touch-input/focus/change-focus-behavior) permits `focusProperties { canFocus = false }` without disabling pointer interactions, so the native surface no longer creates a dead keyboard target. Flutter's [`FocusableActionDetector`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/FocusableActionDetector-class.html) now owns the keyboard target and dispatches the default [`ActivateIntent`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/ActivateIntent-class.html) on Enter and Space to the existing callback. Paired `FocusInteraction.Focus/Unfocus` events reach the same native `MutableInteractionSource` for official focus feedback. A focused widget test failed before this change and now passes for focus, both activation keys and blur; all 13 card tests and capped Kotlin compilation pass. Android hardware keyboard and TalkBack traversal still need runtime inspection, especially when the Flutter child contains its own controls.
- Card shape review: the ordinary Android card previously sent a fixed 12 dp radius even when the caller supplied no shape. The [Compose `Card` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Card.composable) defaults to `CardDefaults.shape`, and AndroidX's [filled-card token](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/268d841a45644cadf438fc335c793869728449ec/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/tokens/FilledCardTokens.kt) points to the theme's medium corner. The bridge now leaves the native shape at that default unless the caller sets a radius; the Flutter child still uses a 12 dp clipping radius, matching the default medium shape but not a custom native theme shape. The glass fallback retains its 16 dp radius.
- Explicit asymmetric or elliptical card corners formerly sent only the top-left horizontal radius, so Compose drew all four corners alike while Flutter clipped the child with the caller's full shape. The bridge now sends all eight resolved physical radius values. The existing native `Card` draws a Compose [`GenericShape`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/shape/GenericShape) built from a [`RoundRect`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/geometry/RoundRect) for these explicit overrides; a uniform circular radius still uses `RoundedCornerShape`, and the absent override still uses `CardDefaults.shape`. Radii are scaled when their sums exceed the card bounds, matching Flutter's rounded-rectangle normalization. The RTL bridge test checks directional corners after resolution.
- A caller-supplied `RoundedRectangleBorder.side` was dropped by the Android bridge. The official [Compose Material 3 `Card`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Card.composable) accepts a `BorderStroke`; the bridge now forwards the side's width and color into that native parameter when `borderOnForeground` is false. Flutter's [Card contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/Card/borderOnForeground.html) places the border over its child by default. For that case, the native Compose `Card` remains the surface and a pointer-transparent Flutter `ShapeDecoration` draws only the caller's explicit border after the child clip. This avoids drawing the border twice and leaves the native color, elevation, and shape in place. A device visual check is still needed for edge-to-edge child content and rounded clipping.
- Snackbar behavior gap: the package opened `NativeM3ESnackbar` inside a full-screen Compose `Dialog`, hosted by a Flutter `PageRoute`. The [M3 snackbar guidance](https://m3.material.io/components/snackbar/overview/) says snackbars should not interrupt the user's experience. Current change: a one-pixel `OverlayEntry` supplies the native window token without a Flutter route, while a nonfocusable Compose [`Popup`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/window/Popup.composable) positions the real `Snackbar` at the bottom of the window. This is intended to receive taps only inside the popup bounds. The original Flutter theme, brightness, directionality and dynamic-color choice are preserved in the overlay host.
- Current motion change: the popup now contains the official [`SnackbarHost`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SnackbarHost.composable) and `SnackbarHostState`. AndroidX's [source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/c4cb9c1561c1d4315d2a5836b3ed88d1bd039514/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SnackbarHost.kt) uses FastEffects opacity and FastSpatial scale motion from the active motion scheme, plus a polite live region and dismiss semantics. The package waits for the host's exit item to leave composition before removing the popup. The existing caller-specified duration is adjusted through Compose's accessibility manager.
- Flutter's [snackbar accessibility contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/SnackBar-class.html) keeps a snackbar with an action from timing out when accessible navigation is active. The native bridge's manual finite timer previously ignored that state. It now forwards `MediaQuery.accessibleNavigation` and keeps an actionable snackbar until the user chooses its action or the official Compose dismiss action. The dismiss button is enabled through `SnackbarHostState.showSnackbar(withDismissAction = true)` only for this persistent case, as the pinned AndroidX source recommends for `SnackbarDuration.Indefinite`. A focused bridge test failed before the property was forwarded; nine focused snackbar tests and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation pass. TalkBack runtime behavior remains unverified.
- Current queue change: calls from the same root Flutter overlay are serialized, so the next one waits until the previous native host has finished its exit animation. A pending snackbar is skipped if its source context was unmounted before its turn.
- The native popup formerly used only the system bottom inset, so a snackbar could cover `Scaffold.bottomNavigationBar` or a FAB. [Flutter's floating snackbar contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ScaffoldMessengerState/showSnackBar.html) places it above both, and Compose's [snackbar guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/snackbar) hosts it in `Scaffold`. Since this package's visible snackbar is a separate native popup, Flutter tracks its actual [`ScaffoldGeometry`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ScaffoldGeometry-class.html) during paint and forwards the height of the higher bottom obstacle. Reading that geometry in the tap callback is invalid by [Flutter's paint-phase contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/Scaffold/geometryOf.html); the first attempt fell back to zero, and a test reproduced the overlap. A one-pixel, pointer-transparent painter measures it before the native popup is shown and continues forwarding changes while it is visible. The first live-height test failed with a one-time sample and passed after the tracker was retained. Flutter's `ScaffoldGeometry.copyWith` retains the previous nonnull bar position when a bar is removed, so the tracker also checks the current `Scaffold` slots; a removal test failed before that guard and passed after it. Eight focused tests pass for bottom bars, FABs, height changes and removal; the shared tooltip overlay's seven tests also pass.
- Still to verify on a device: touch passthrough outside the popup, action taps, native popup motion during live bottom-bar height changes, placement above the IME, TalkBack focus, route changes while visible, and rapid consecutive snackbars. Each invocation still creates its own native host; the Flutter queue coordinates them across calls.

## Tooltips

- Official sources: [Compose tooltip guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tooltip), [`TooltipBox`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TooltipBox.composable), [`TooltipDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TooltipDefaults), and AndroidX's [plain-tooltip implementation](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/bc6f6ab1f5a46fc2091e8becd635c3e11933e4a2/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Tooltip.kt).
- `AdaptiveTooltip` used a Flutter page route plus a full-screen Compose `Dialog` around the real `TooltipBox`. This made a brief plain tooltip modal. It now mounts `TooltipBox`/`PlainTooltip` in the existing one-pixel nonmodal overlay, leaving Compose's popup and motion in control. The default container, text color, type and internal padding now come from `TooltipDefaults` and `PlainTooltip`, instead of generic inverse colors, fixed 14 sp type and an extra 12 × 8 dp inset. Caller overrides still cross the bridge. The spacing parameter now reaches `rememberTooltipPositionProvider`, and the overlay waits for Compose's exit transition before disposal.
- Because the host itself measures one pixel, a regular `Box` could constrain the invisible anchor to one pixel even if Flutter supplied its real bounds. A small Compose `Layout` now measures that anchor at the supplied Flutter size and places it at the supplied screen coordinate while reporting only the host's one-pixel size. This is bridge geometry; `TooltipBox` still renders and positions the actual Material tooltip.
- The Flutter trigger used to open the native tooltip on a normal tap and had no cursor-hover path. The current [Compose tooltip guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tooltip) and the pinned `BasicTooltipBox` source instead use long press on touch and hover with a mouse. A focused test reproduced the unwanted tap popup. The bridge now starts immediately after Flutter's recognized long press, applies `waitDuration` only to hover, cancels pending hover when the cursor exits, and sends a native dismissal request after a shown hover exits. Compose keeps the real `TooltipBox`/`PlainTooltip`, its persistent hover state, and its animated exit. Four focused bridge tests and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation pass.
- The pinned AndroidX `BasicTooltipBox` also shows its tooltip when the anchor receives keyboard focus, dismisses on blur, and handles Escape. Flutter hosts the real focusable child, so a nonfocusable, traversal-skipped `Focus` ancestor observes descendant focus and forwards the same persistent show/dismiss signal to Compose. Hover exit preserves the tooltip while keyboard focus remains, and blur preserves it while the cursor remains. An initial test failed because the bridge never showed a tooltip on focus; after the fix, seven focused tests cover normal button taps, focus, Enter activation, Tab traversal, blur and Escape without adding a focus stop. [Flutter `Focus` contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/Focus-class.html), [descendant `hasFocus` behavior](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/FocusNode/hasFocus.html).
- Still to verify on a device: the popup's screen position when its invisible anchor is offset from the one-pixel host, touch passthrough, hover/long-press timing, keyboard focus and native dismissal timing, accessibility announcement, reduced motion, system insets and custom sizes. The Flutter bridge tests prove route, timer, focus and property behavior, not native popup geometry.
- The [official `TooltipAnchorPosition`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TooltipAnchorPosition) exposes `Above`, `Below`, logical `Start`/`End`, and physical `Left`/`Right`; the package had only the inherited above/below `preferBelow` switch. The optional Android-only `androidPosition` and `androidSpacing` now feed those values to the existing real `TooltipDefaults.rememberTooltipPositionProvider` on `TooltipBox`, while a null position retains the old default. Logical sides remain logical through Compose's layout direction. An explicit native position paired with a Flutter-only font or decoration now reports an error instead of silently using a Flutter tooltip that cannot honor side placement. Two focused regressions failed before the change; all 17 tooltip tests, the full 808-test Flutter suite, full Dart analysis and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation pass. Side placement, edge flipping and RTL still require device inspection.

## Native view ownership

- Official source: [Compose in Views](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/migrate/interoperability-apis/compose-in-views).
- Package path: `android/src/main/kotlin/com/ledexsoft/adaptive_liquid_glass/M3EPlatformView.kt`.
- Finding: each platform view has its own `ComposeView` and owner. Installing that owner on the shared `FlutterView` leaves a reference to a destroyed owner when the platform view is disposed. The owner should be scoped to the `ComposeView` only.
- Current change: removed the shared-root assignment; the existing assignment on the `ComposeView` supplies lifecycle, saved state, ViewModelStore, and back dispatcher ownership.
- Flutter channel lifecycle audit: [AndroidView](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/AndroidView-class.html) creates/releases platform-view resources with its state. The Dart wrapper reused one channel field but did not unregister an earlier channel when a replacement view ID arrived, so the old channel could still deliver events into the current widget and remained registered after widget disposal. `_onPlatformViewCreated` now detaches the previous card interaction controller, clears that channel's handler, and installs the replacement; each incoming handler verifies it still owns the mounted view. A second race let `onPlatformViewCreated` arrive after the wrapper was removed and register a new orphan handler; it now returns before channel creation when unmounted. Both focused tests first reproduced their races and then passed alongside six card-interaction tests. Native owner cleanup still needs repeated creation/disposal on an Android device.
- Property traffic audit: Flutter's [mapEquals](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/foundation/mapEquals.html) compares nested lists/maps only by their own equality operators. The bridge rebuilt carousel `items` and encoded image buffers in nested collections, so a semantically unchanged rebuild sent `setProperties` and the entire image payload again. The bridge now compares only channel-supported maps and lists recursively, first checking identity so stable image buffers are constant-time; changed bytes still cause a native update. A focused test failed on the duplicate send and passed after the change, alongside the lifecycle and card-interaction tests. `AdaptiveCarouselItem.imageBytes` is documented as immutable after supply; use a new buffer to update it. Runtime channel throughput under large images remains a profiling check.
- Still to verify: creation, disposal, repeated opening, and system Back with multiple native views on an Android device.

## Typography

- Official sources: [Material typography guidance](https://m3.material.io/styles/typography/applying-type), [Compose `MaterialExpressiveTheme` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/MaterialExpressiveTheme.composable), [AndroidX theme source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/2c498ed799191b8b273eccae8ed99a5c3fe14ade/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/MaterialTheme.kt).
- AndroidX currently supplies `Typography()` when no explicit typography is given; its source still marks a distinct expressive typography default as a TODO. The real Compose `MaterialExpressiveTheme` accepts a `Typography` parameter. The bridge maps numeric metrics from the active Flutter `TextTheme` into its 15 baseline roles, and an explicit `AdaptiveMaterialExpressiveTheme(typography: ...)` scale supplies all 15 emphasized roles. Each role starts from Compose's default `Typography()`, so absent metrics and native font scaling remain intact. Flutter-declared font assets are resolved through the real Compose `Font` API; per-widget fonts and decorations remain open. A later follow-up below covers variable axes in theme roles.
- Flutter 3.47.9 still bundles `FontManifest.json` with each declared family, face path, optional weight and style. The Android plugin now obtains manifest and face keys through the engine's `FlutterAssets.getAssetFilePathByName`, reads the app's packaged manifest, and builds Compose `FontFamily` with the [official Android asset `Font` constructor](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/text/font/package-summary). The theme's baseline and emphasized role serializers include `fontFamily` and `fontFamilyFallback`; a listed fallback is tried only if the primary is not packaged. Failed or unlisted assets leave Compose's own default family in place. Six focused Flutter typography tests, targeted Dart analysis, capped one-worker Kotlin compilation and `git diff --check` pass. No custom-font device rendering has been observed; see [the font asset plan](flutter-font-assets-plan.md).
- Variable-font follow-up: Flutter's [`TextStyle.fontVariations`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/painting/TextStyle/fontVariations.html) carries OpenType axis values, while Compose's [asset `Font` overload](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/text/font/package-summary) applies `FontVariation.Settings` on API 26+. The bridge now serializes unique axis/value pairs from baseline and emphasized roles and loads declared Flutter font faces with those settings. A 64-entry LRU bounds the resulting font-family cache as animated role axes change. On API 24–25 the existing declared face remains in use without variable axes; generic/system families do not claim axis support. The pinned Compose UI 1.12.0 lacks the current documentation's `Settings.merge`, so the implementation builds one unique settings list: face weight/style are included unless the caller overrides `wght` or `ital`. The serialization test failed before the change and seven typography bridge tests now pass; capped Kotlin compilation passed after the pinned-API correction. Real variable-font rendering and axis bounds remain device/font checks, and per-widget text variations are still unsupported by the generic native text slots.
- OpenType feature follow-up: Flutter's [`FontFeature`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/dart-ui/FontFeature-class.html) identifies a four-character feature tag and value; Compose's [`TextStyle.fontFeatureSettings`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/text/TextStyle) accepts CSS-style feature settings. The bridge now sends common four-character alphanumeric tags with nonnegative values to theme typography roles, supported native `Text` slots, and native text fields. For example, `FontFeature.tabularFigures()` becomes `'tnum' 1` in real Compose text. A text slot with an unsupported tag retains its Flutter renderer; invalid nonempty features in an explicit native theme scale raise a Flutter error instead of being silently dropped. The three focused bridge cases failed before their respective changes and 67 related Flutter tests plus full `flutter analyze --no-pub` passed afterward. A later capped offline Kotlin compile passed after the feature bridge; visual font rendering remains to verify on a device.
- Two bridge tests failed before the change and now pass. The seven focused typography/palette tests, targeted Dart analysis, and capped offline one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. This verifies the property path and native compilation, not font rendering.
- Native modal and nonmodal overlays are hosted outside the caller's inherited theme scope. Both previously lost the caller's emphasized type scale and explicit fallback palette. They now capture the caller's Flutter theme, expressive scale, brightness, and dynamic-color setting; the modal still reads the route's live `MediaQuery` so keyboard insets can update. Both focused overlay tests failed before this propagation, then 11 typography/palette/dialog tests and targeted Dart analysis passed.
- The wrapper initially applied its explicit scale to Flutter and Compose but did not register the same `ThemeExtension`, so `AdaptiveExpressiveTypography.of(context)` threw inside that wrapper. It now installs the scale in `ThemeData.extensions` while preserving unrelated extensions. The focused test failed on the missing extension before the change.
- `AdaptiveApp` installs the wrapper internally. Its Material theme's expressive extension previously remained invisible to that wrapper, so the emphasized Compose roles were absent even when the app opted in. The builder now forwards the active light/dark theme extension. The bridge test failed on the missing role before the change; the focused test and existing `AdaptiveApp` suite passed afterward.
- Device check remains: large font scales and TalkBack should keep labels readable without clipping in each native component.

## Expressive shape catalog and morph

- Official sources: [Material shape principles](https://m3.material.io/styles/shape/overview-principles), [Compose `Shapes` theme API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Shapes), [Compose `MaterialShapes` source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/c8a071114c193cd7b43a05ba1489e72d21f3b833/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/MaterialShapes.kt), and [graphics-shapes `RoundedPolygon.normalized()`](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/graphics/shapes/RoundedPolygon).
- The package exposes all 35 `MaterialShapes` and a generic native `Morph`. AndroidX normalizes these polygons into the 0..1 unit square. The package's drawing code used the transform for a -1..1 polygon, scaling each axis by half and translating by one unit. This placed the polygon in the lower-right part of its host and made it half-sized.
- Current change: scale the normalized path by the full host width and height, following AndroidX's `toShape` transform; cache the `Morph` across draw frames; use `MaterialTheme.colorScheme.primary` when the caller does not provide a color. The catalog itself stays native and unchanged. The Dart wrapper now excludes semantics only for the decorative shape layer, preserving accessibility for an interactive child placed on top.
- The real `MaterialExpressiveTheme` also accepts a `Shapes` scale. The package previously had no bridge for its eight corner roles. `AdaptiveExpressiveShapeScale` now sends optional dp radii for those roles to an actual Compose `Shapes().copy(...)`; omitted roles preserve Compose defaults. `AdaptiveApp`, custom theme wrappers, and native popup overlays carry the same scale. The four focused bridge checks and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed; native shape rendering and component-specific overrides still need device inspection.
- The pinned Compose filled-card token resolves to `Shapes.medium`. The hybrid `AdaptiveCard` still clipped its Flutter child to 12 dp when the new native medium role was customized, while Compose used the requested radius. A focused test failed at 12 versus 24 dp; the Flutter clip now reads the same optional medium role, retaining explicit card-shape precedence. All 12 focused card/shape checks passed afterward.
- Still to verify on a device: shape bounds and morph progression in square and rectangular hosts, clipping, dynamic light/dark color, and frame pacing when a Flutter animation drives progress.

## FABs and extended FAB

- The official [Compose FAB guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/fab) and pinned AndroidX `FloatingActionButton.kt` expose four real standalone sizes: `SmallFloatingActionButton` (40 dp), baseline `FloatingActionButton` (56 dp), expressive `MediumFloatingActionButton` (80 dp), and `LargeFloatingActionButton` (96 dp). The previous package API exposed only `mini`/baseline on the standalone control, and its native Kotlin route had no medium case. `AdaptiveFloatingActionButton.androidSize` now selects all four actual Compose variants, with `mini` preserved as the default when no explicit size is given. A distinct enum avoids changing `AdaptiveFabSize.medium`, which existing FAB menus use for their 56 dp toggle. Medium and large icons follow `FloatingActionButtonDefaults.MediumIconSize` and `LargeIconSize` from the pinned AndroidX source. Flutter cannot reproduce the native 80 dp expressive medium variant for an icon with unsupported paint effects, so that explicit combination reports a clear error rather than silently showing a different size. The focused bridge tests cover medium and large host sizes plus the unsupported-style error; all 13 FAB tests, capped one-worker Kotlin compilation, and full Dart analysis pass. Device appearance and TalkBack remain unverified.
- The official [Compose accessibility API defaults](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults#graphic-elements) say an interactive icon needs a localized text description; the [FAB example](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/fab) supplies one on its `Icon`. The package had set the native FAB `Icon` description to null and relied on `Icon.semanticLabel` or `tooltip` in the parent modifier, but accepted both absent, creating an unnamed button. The Android bridge now rejects missing or whitespace-only names before either the native route or an unsupported-style Flutter fallback is selected. Callers provide `tooltip` or `Icon.semanticLabel`; both are already public and the real Compose FAB and TooltipBox remain unchanged. The two missing-name tests failed first, then all 15 focused FAB tests passed. Existing Android FAB callers without a name must add one. TalkBack speech still needs device verification.
- The current [Compose accessibility guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults#minimum-touch-target-sizes) requires a 48 dp minimum interactive layout. In the pinned AndroidX `FloatingActionButton.kt`, the real `SmallFloatingActionButton` has a 40 dp visual minimum but delegates to clickable `Surface`, whose `Surface.kt` applies `minimumInteractiveComponentSize()` before drawing the surface. The package's Flutter AndroidView was only 40 × 40 dp, leaving no surrounding space for that native reservation and risking a clipped 48 dp target. The small route now reserves a 48 × 48 dp host while the Compose root centers the unchanged official small FAB; larger sizes keep their native 56/80/96 dp hosts and an explicitly oversized icon still enlarges the host. The 48 dp host test failed first at 40 dp and all 16 focused FAB tests pass after the correction. Device edge-hit and visual alignment checks remain.
- Large-text geometry follow-up: Flutter's [TextPainter.textScaler](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/painting/TextPainter/textScaler.html) must use the current `MediaQuery` scale to reserve space for text, while the real AndroidX [ExtendedFloatingActionButton implementation](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/dd7d2935e6a5ab12f6957a813926eea1ca38188e/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButton.kt) measures its `Text` slot and applies native expansion motion. The Flutter host previously measured unscaled label width and held its height at 56 dp even when scaled text grew taller. It now reserves scaled label width and height, keeps the larger host during native collapse, then consumes Compose's measured height to release the extra space. The real `ExtendedFloatingActionButton` and its spatial/effects animation remain in Compose. Two staged widget assertions failed first on unchanging width/height and on retained height after collapse; all five focused FAB tests, capped offline Kotlin compilation and full Dart analysis passed afterward. Device checks remain for font-scale parity, clipping, alignment and native transition timing.
- Official sources: [Compose `ExtendedFloatingActionButton`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ExtendedFloatingActionButton.composable) and [AndroidX implementation](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/c73a560c5c9ba4ed53a4f6dd5df36dfd16a837e7/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButton.kt). The `expanded` overload owns the label's visibility, width and opacity animation; the official sample gives its collapsed state an accessible icon name and tooltip.
- The package previously swapped between separate `ExtendedFloatingActionButton` and `FloatingActionButton` composables. That removed the native expand/collapse transition and left the collapsed icon without an accessible name; the serialized tooltip was unused. The native route now keeps one official `ExtendedFloatingActionButton(expanded = ...)`, supplies the collapsed label/tooltip to the icon, and anchors a real Compose tooltip above a collapsed or explicitly described button.
- Extended FAB icon slot fidelity: the official [`ExtendedFloatingActionButton`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ExtendedFloatingActionButton.composable) keeps the `icon` and `text` slots in one native component while `expanded` animates text visibility. The pinned 1.5.0-rc01 source uses 16 dp start padding, 12 dp icon/text spacing, and 20 dp end padding. The bridge previously discarded explicit Flutter icon size, color and semantic label; its fixed width estimate could also clip a large custom icon. It now forwards those values to the real Compose `Icon`, grows the Flutter host estimate from the pinned slot spacing when required, and adjusts collapsed width/height for a large icon without replacing Compose's animation. Unsupported Flutter icon effects retain the Flutter FAB with explicit background/foreground colors. The new style/geometry test failed first on the missing native size; all three focused tests, capped Kotlin compilation and full Flutter analysis pass. Device visual and TalkBack verification remain pending.
- The ordinary native FAB also used its public `tooltip` only as a content description. The [official FAB reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButton.composable) wraps the control in `TooltipBox`/`PlainTooltip` so hover and long press can show the action label. The package now follows that pattern for a supplied nonblank tooltip across its small, regular and large Compose FAB variants, retaining the original accessibility description.
- A normal FAB without `tooltip` also discarded the `semanticLabel` on a supplied Flutter `Icon` while serializing the icon for Compose, leaving the native button unnamed. The bridge now forwards that label separately; Compose uses it before the tooltip as the button's accessibility description. A custom child without either label still needs the caller to provide a tooltip because its arbitrary Flutter semantics cannot be reconstructed in Compose.
- FAB icon content parity: [Compose FABs](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SmallFloatingActionButton.composable) accept arbitrary composable content, and [Compose `Icon`](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/graphics/images/material) defaults to 24 dp and `LocalContentColor` but exposes a size modifier and explicit tint. The package's native FAB discarded an explicit Flutter `Icon.size` or `Icon.color`. It now forwards those values to the real native icon, including the optional AI badge path; absent values retain Compose defaults. Flutter icon font axes and paint effects that cannot cross the bridge use the existing Flutter FAB. The focused size test failed first; all three focused FAB tests, capped Kotlin compilation and full Flutter analysis passed. Native layout and disabled icon tint still need device review.
- Flutter formerly shrank the Android platform view from the estimated expanded width to 56 dp in the same frame as `extended` changed, which would clip Compose's new collapse animation. The bridge now retains the wide host until Compose reports its measured final 56 dp width, then releases the extra Flutter layout width. Expansion reserves its estimated width before updating the native state. This host coordination does not implement the animation; Compose owns the actual FAB transition.
- The [Compose FAB APIs](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButton.composable) do not expose an `enabled` parameter. Previously the package only suppressed the callback, so a nullable `onPressed` still left a clickable, focusable FAB with ripple. Ordinary and extended FABs now keep the official composable but consume disabled pointer input on the initial pass, remove focus and click semantics, and announce the disabled state. The disabled appearance uses native [`ButtonDefaults.buttonColors()`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ButtonDefaults) disabled colors and zero elevation because FAB has no own disabled tokens. Enabled FABs keep their native interaction and motion.
- Still to verify on a device: the measured-width event arrives after native layout, transition is unclipped in both directions, RTL alignment, large-font labels, tooltip positioning, TalkBack naming, disabled pointer/focus behavior, and gesture hit testing during width changes.

## FAB menu

- The pinned AndroidX `FloatingActionButtonMenu.kt` measures its button slot directly, while its real `ToggleFloatingActionButton` starts at 40 dp, uses `Modifier.toggleable` without a surrounding `minimumInteractiveComponentSize()` layout, and interpolates to `FabMenuBaselineTokens.CloseButtonContainerHeight` (56 dp) when expanded. The package's full-screen overlay keeps native pointer dispatch, but its Flutter `Scaffold` marker was only 40 dp. Following [Compose's 48 dp touch-target guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults#minimum-touch-target-sizes), the small menu now reserves a 56 dp marker and a centered 56 dp Compose button-slot wrapper around the unchanged official toggle. This also fits the complete 40-to-56 dp native animation. The marker-size regression failed at 40 dp and again at an intermediate 48 dp reservation before the 56 dp correction. All ten focused menu tests, the full 828-test Flutter suite, full analysis, and the capped offline Android example APK build pass. Actual edge taps, corner alignment, tooltip placement and TalkBack still require a device.
- The [official Compose FAB-menu sample](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButtonMenu.composable) assigns the real toggle a `contentDescription`, `stateDescription`, tooltip and close-menu custom action. `AdaptiveFabMenu` supplied localized defaults, but explicitly empty or whitespace-only overrides replaced them and left the native action or state without useful speech. Android now rejects blank `toggleMenuLabel`, `closeMenuLabel`, `expandedStateLabel` and `collapsedStateLabel` overrides before the native view is mounted. The regression failed for a whitespace-only toggle label before the guard, then all 11 focused menu tests passed. This does not change the Compose FAB, its shape or its motion; a TalkBack check remains necessary.
- The real Compose [`SplitButtonLayout`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SplitButtonLayout.composable) keeps its leading and trailing segments separate. The package provides the leading segment's accessible name through its visible text, and sets the trailing segment's `contentDescription`, `stateDescription` and tooltip from bridge labels. Previously a whitespace-only primary or menu-row label rendered as an unnamed action, while blank optional overrides erased the trailing name or state. Android now rejects those values before creating the native view. Two focused regressions failed on the old bridge; all 14 split-button tests pass after correction. The Compose buttons, dropdown, ripple and shape morph are unchanged; real TalkBack output remains to verify.
- Official sources: [M3 FAB menu overview](https://m3.material.io/components/fab-menu/overview), [Compose `FloatingActionButtonMenu` API and sample](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButtonMenu.composable), [AndroidX toggle FAB source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/2c498ed799191b8b273eccae8ed99a5c3fe14ade/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButtonMenu.kt).
- Package paths: `lib/src/widgets/adaptive_fab_menu.dart` and `android/src/main/kotlin/com/ledexsoft/adaptive_liquid_glass/M3EAndroidContent.kt`.
- Native control: `FloatingActionButtonMenu`, `FloatingActionButtonMenuItem`, and `ToggleFloatingActionButton` in Material 3 Compose 1.5.
- Finding: the default Flutter platform view is limited to the 40/56/96 dp button bounds. The Compose sample places the menu in a screen-size container. The optional full-size Flutter FAB changes `Scaffold`'s FAB geometry, which can misplace floating snackbars.
- Current change: the default Flutter route uses `OverlayPortal` and a `CompositedTransformTarget` marker whose size remains 40/56/96 dp. A full-size `CompositedTransformFollower` carries one package-owned Compose platform view anchored to that marker, so Flutter Scaffold measures a normal FAB while the official M3 menu remains mounted through its entrance and exit. The caller-owned full-size route remains opt-in and rejects unbounded parents.
- Remaining risk: the full-size platform view has not yet been exercised on a device for hit testing, clipping, focus, system Back, or interactions with other overlays. The native M3 menu's exit is now state-driven, but its rendered motion needs observation.
- The official Compose sample exposes a `CustomAccessibilityAction` on the last item to close the menu, because the close FAB precedes the items in traversal order. The package now follows that sample and serializes a localizable `closeMenuLabel`.
- The same [official Compose sample](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButtonMenu.composable) names the toggle tooltip as a pane and returns keyboard focus from the first item to the toggle on Up, numpad Up, or Shift+Tab. The native menu already handled Up and Shift+Tab; it now also handles numpad Up and gives the tooltip the localized toggle/close pane title. Kotlin compilation passed; physical keyboard and TalkBack behavior still require a device.
- The native FAB menu previously defaulted its opening, closing and state descriptions to English even under a localized Flutter `MaterialApp`. Omitted labels now resolve through the host `MaterialLocalizations` before entering the overlay; explicit caller labels keep precedence and the public string getters retain their previous fallback values. The native toggle's tooltip and content description announce the closing action while expanded. A focused widget test uses a custom localization delegate to verify all four strings cross the overlay bridge. TalkBack timing and spoken order still need a device check.
- The current official Compose FAB-menu sample adds `LiveRegionMode.Assertive` to the toggle's `PlainTooltip` semantics as a workaround for tooltip announcements by screen readers. The native toggle now uses that property alongside its localized pane title. The actual menu, toggle, tooltip, and their motion remain AndroidX composables. Kotlin compilation passed; TalkBack announcement timing is still a device check.
- The AndroidX implementation interpolates container size, corner radius, container and icon colors, and icon size using checked progress. The package removed its fixed outer `Modifier.size` and now supplies the official interpolation helpers. Default/semantic colors transition from the selected container role to primary/onPrimary; caller-supplied colors stay fixed. The package's 40 dp small option uses the same Compose helpers with a 40 dp initial size. These transitions still need device observation.
- The pinned [AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3-android/1.5.0-rc01/material3-android-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) staggers item dismissal with a spring and then animates each item's width and alpha. Its item column reports zero height only when all items are no longer visible. The package previously sent `itemPressed` to Flutter immediately after setting `expanded = false`, allowing navigation to dispose the native view before its exit motion finished. The native bridge now records the menu's initially collapsed measured height, stores the selected index, and sends the action only when Compose's measured menu height returns to that collapsed size. Additional taps cannot replace the pending action. A recreated transient menu starts closed so it can measure that baseline before selection. This waits for the real native animation without a guessed delay; no API-level completion callback exists. Capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Device interaction, interrupted transitions, reduced motion and callback timing still require runtime verification.
- Item-enabled API audit: the pinned `FloatingActionButtonMenuItem` exposes `onClick`, text, icon, modifier and colors, but no `enabled` parameter or disabled color tokens. A package item with a null `onPressed` currently still uses that real clickable item and closes the menu without an app callback. Adding a visual-only disabled state around `Surface(onClick)` would leave native keyboard/click semantics inconsistent; a fully disabled item needs an official API or a deliberate public contract change. No imitation is added during this pass.
- Label contract: the official [`FloatingActionButtonMenuItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButtonMenuItem.composable) requires a `text` slot for the visible action label. The package previously allowed an empty or whitespace-only `AdaptiveFabMenuItem.label`, producing a visually blank, hard-to-name native action. It now rejects blank labels before constructing the real Compose menu, on every platform. The focused test failed before this validation; all seven FAB-menu tests pass afterward. This is input validation, not a replacement menu rendering path.
- The [official Compose FAB-menu sample](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButtonMenu.composable) adjusts the text slot width to its longest rendered line for multiline labels. The package's real `FloatingActionButtonMenuItem` now uses that text-slot measurement, with an empty-label fallback, so wrapped labels do not reserve the width of their entire unwrapped string. A capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed after replacing a `Constraints` helper unavailable in the pinned Compose version. Visual wrapping and font-scale behavior still need Android device inspection.
- Bridge race: the native item callback is delayed until the official menu finishes collapsing. If the Flutter app reorders its item list during that interval, a bare index can call a different action. Each Flutter `AdaptiveFabMenuItem` now receives a weakly held, monotonic identifier; Compose retains it with the pending selection and sends it after collapse. Dart finds the same live item by identifier, so a reordered item keeps its action and a removed item cannot trigger a different callback. A focused test failed before the ID was serialized and passed after the bridge change; targeted Dart analysis and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Device animation timing remains open.
- Still to verify: menu item visibility on short and long screens, RTL corners, touch passthrough, keyboard focus, TalkBack traversal, system Back, reduced motion, dismissal motion, and snackbar placement.

## Carousel

- Official sources: [M3 carousel overview](https://m3.material.io/components/carousel/overview), [Compose carousel guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/carousel), [`CarouselItemScope` reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/CarouselItemScope).
- Multi-aspect follow-up: the pinned AndroidX Material 3 `1.5.0-rc01` source contains the public `carouselParallaxScrollEffect` modifier and `CarouselParallaxScrollEffectState(LazyListState)`. Google's [Compose sample](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/9308af0af4c16704f68d2ae0151745a783fd187c/compose/material3/material3/samples/src/main/java/androidx/compose/material3/samples/CarouselSamples.kt) applies them to individually sized `LazyRow` items. `AdaptiveCarouselType.multiAspect` now uses that native path; each `AdaptiveCarouselItem` supplies `mainAxisExtent`. The bridge validates every main-axis size and rejects the shared width and a custom tap duration, which this official lazy-list path cannot honor. The existing dedicated carousel layouts remain on their respective native composables. The multi-aspect variant uses free scrolling; its settled callback reports the first visible item and the item nearest the viewport center. Capped offline Kotlin compilation and focused Flutter tests passed. Parallax, RTL, large text and TalkBack still need device verification.
- Multi-aspect orientation and spacing follow-up: the [official `carouselParallaxScrollEffect` contract](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/Modifier) explicitly accepts `LazyColumn` as well as `LazyRow`. The package now maps `axis: Axis.vertical` plus `type: multiAspect` to a real Compose `LazyColumn` with the same effect state and variable item heights; the dedicated hero, multi-browse and uncontained APIs remain horizontal only. `mainAxisExtent` names the item's size in the chosen scroll axis. The Google sample uses 8 dp between items and 16 dp content padding, while `CarouselDefaults.ItemSpacing` and `ContentPadding` are zero for dedicated carousels; the multi-aspect route now follows the sample without altering those dedicated defaults. The dedicated AndroidX carousels set `Role.Carousel` in their root semantics; both generic multi-aspect list roots now apply the same role. The new vertical bridge test failed on the old horizontal-only rejection, then all 13 focused tests, full Dart analysis and capped Kotlin compilation passed. Device checks remain for vertical parallax, touch, full-width card clipping and TalkBack.
- The package already uses the native centered hero, multi-browse, and uncontained Compose carousels. Their items are measured at a large width and need `CarouselItemScope.maskClip` to follow the moving focal mask. The package's card applied a fixed `clip(RoundedCornerShape)` instead, so the item content did not follow the native reveal/collapse mask.
- Current change: the native card is a `CarouselItemScope` extension and applies the official `maskClip` to its outer modifier. Tapping an item now calls the official `CarouselState.animateScrollToItem`, so a small item can become focal while preserving any caller tap callback. The `positionChanged` callback now waits for `CarouselState.isScrollInProgress` to become false, matching the public "settles" contract instead of reporting a changing focal index during a drag.
- Card interaction audit: the carousel still used the noninteractive Compose `Card` overload with an outer `Modifier.clickable`, so the press was outside the card's `MutableInteractionSource` and its official pressed elevation. The pinned [interactive `Card` overload](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Card.composable) sends clicks through `Surface` with `CardDefaults.cardElevation()` and its interaction source. Each carousel item now calls that overload directly; `CarouselItemScope.maskClip`, the native card content and the existing `CarouselState` tap scroll remain. Device inspection is still needed for ripple clipping and pressed elevation while a small item expands.
- Geometry and motion review: the bridge previously forced 8 dp item spacing, 16 dp horizontal content padding, a 300 dp hero width, and a 28 dp item corner even with no caller override. The [Compose carousel defaults](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/CarouselDefaults) and [centered hero API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/HorizontalCenteredHeroCarousel.composable) now supply the default spacing, padding and unspecified hero width; `MaterialTheme.shapes.extraLarge` supplies the item's default corner. Callers can set the spacing, padding, width and corner explicitly. The public tap `scrollAnimationDuration` now reaches the [native `CarouselState.animateScrollToItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/CarouselState): null keeps its spring, zero jumps, and a positive value uses a native Compose tween for the requested milliseconds. Free and snapped gesture flings retain Compose's own behavior.
- Fling audit: the shared `freeScroll = false` default previously forced `CarouselDefaults.singleAdvanceFlingBehavior` on all three layouts. The official [`HorizontalUncontainedCarousel`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/HorizontalUncontainedCarousel.composable) instead defaults to `CarouselDefaults.noSnapFlingBehavior()`, while hero and multi-browse default to single-item snapping. The native bridge now always uses no-snap for uncontained and keeps the explicit `freeScroll` choice for hero and contained. The Dart property documents this type-specific behavior. The pinned Compose artifact exposes these three horizontal layouts but no official vertical full-screen carousel, so the package still rejects vertical Android carousels rather than drawing a substitute.
- Item-width audit: `HorizontalMultiBrowseCarousel` requires a preferred width and `HorizontalUncontainedCarousel` requires an exact width. The bridge used an unexplained 300 dp fallback for both, which can leave too little room for multiple items on a compact screen. The official [Compose carousel examples](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/carousel) use 186 dp for both variants. The bridge now uses 186 dp when the caller omits `uncontainedItemExtent`; an explicit width retains precedence. The centered hero variant continues to let Compose determine its own preferred width.
- Native geometry input audit: the public bridge previously forwarded zero/negative item width, negative spacing or radius, and infinite padding directly as Compose `Dp` values. It now rejects nonfinite values, requires a positive explicit item width, and requires nonnegative spacing, padding and radius before creating an Android view. This preserves the real `CarouselDefaults` whenever an override is absent.
- Viewport geometry follow-up: the same bridge still accepted infinite or negative `width`/`height` and sent them into its Flutter platform-view host and native viewport payload. Finite nonnegative viewport dimensions are now required before mounting the Android view; zero remains accepted for a temporarily hidden host. The focused geometry test failed with no `FlutterError` on the old path, then all 13 carousel tests passed. No native Compose implementation changed.
- Card color audit: the native carousel item used `surfaceContainerHigh` and `onSurface` even when no colors were supplied. The pinned AndroidX [`CardDefaults.cardColors()`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/CardDefaults) instead uses the filled card token (`surfaceContainerHighest`) and its matching content color. Carousel cards now use those native defaults and pass caller overrides through the official `CardColors`, so text and icons follow the card's content color. Image overlays default to white text over a dark scrim. One-worker offline Kotlin compilation passed.
- Image-content follow-up: an explicit `AdaptiveCarouselItem.contentColor` reached the real native `CardColors` but was ignored by the overlaid image title and supporting text, which always forced white. Those text slots now honor the caller's color; white remains the safe default. The scrim also used to start fully transparent at the first title line, allowing white text to disappear on a bright image. Its top alpha is now 0.6 and its bottom alpha 0.78. It uses black behind a light text override and white behind a dark one, so choosing a dark caller color does not put it on a black scrim. The [Compose carousel guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/carousel) permits brief item text; image composition remains custom content inside the official carousel and clickable `Card`. Visual crop, scrim appearance and custom-color contrast need Android device review.
- Image-decode performance: the carousel decoded every newly composed `imageBytes` synchronously in a `remember` calculation on the UI thread. Android's [Compose performance guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/performance/bestpractices) advises keeping expensive work out of composition, and its [image-performance guidance](https://developer.android.com/agents/skills/jetpack-compose/jetpack-compose-performance/references/graphics-performance) calls out bitmap decoding during scrolling. The carousel now decodes through a composition-owned coroutine on `Dispatchers.IO`, capped at two concurrent decodes; a new byte-array identity resets the image state. Text uses the presence of source bytes to keep its position and scrim stable while decoding. The Flutter host forwards its viewport width and height, and the decoder uses Android's [power-of-two `inSampleSize` procedure](https://developer.android.com/topic/performance/graphics/load-bitmap) against those physical pixel bounds. Direct native calls without those values fall back to the window configuration. The app-bar logo and both popup-menu avatar paths now use the same loader with 32–36 dp targets; their icon/logo slots remain stable during loading, and a failed decode restores the old title or icon fallback. A device trace is still needed to measure frame pacing, image appearance and retained bitmap memory.
- Event lifetime audit: the Flutter bridge already bounds-checks native settled-position events but forwarded every `itemPressed` index. A late event after the item list shrinks could therefore reach an app callback with an invalid index. Press events now use the current list bounds. All five focused carousel tests, including negative and out-of-range press indices, passed.
- Reorder audit: a bounded index could still refer to another card if the app reordered `androidItems` before a native click event crossed the platform channel. Each item now carries a weak identity; the Compose clickable card emits that identity with its press, and Flutter resolves it against the current items or ignores removal. Settled-position events also carry leading/focal identities and are ignored if their old indices no longer describe the current list. The Compose position collector restarts when the item identity sequence changes and guards a focal index removed during shrink. The focused test failed before the identity bridge and all seven carousel tests plus capped Kotlin compilation pass after it; runtime event order remains a device check.
- Duplicate item follow-up: a canonical carousel item reused at two positions had the same native ID in both slots. The second card's click resolved to the first, and a settled-position event could not distinguish those slots. The shared per-occurrence ID allocator now gives each position a distinct stable ID; Flutter resolves click and position events using the current ID sequence. The duplicate test failed before the fix, then all eight carousel tests passed. No native Compose change was needed; device event timing remains to verify.
- The current [Compose carousel guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/carousel) describes four layouts, including vertical full-screen. The pinned Material 3 `1.5.0-rc01` source exposes only three public `Horizontal*Carousel` composables; its vertical pager is internal. The package therefore retains an explicit unsupported error for vertical Android carousels until a public Compose component is available, consistent with the requirement to use real native components.
- Callback geometry: the public [`CarouselState.currentItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/CarouselState) identifies the item closest to the snapped position, not the first visible item. The bridge previously sent that one index as both `focalIndex` and `leadingIndex`. Compose now retains the [layout coordinates](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/layout/LayoutCoordinates.html) of the viewport and composed cards, then samples their intersection after scrolling settles. The card's layout bounds alone are too wide because the native carousel masks each item; the intersection therefore uses the official [`CarouselItemDrawInfo.maskRect`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/CarouselItemDrawInfo) transformed into root coordinates. The smallest visible item index is reported separately from `currentItem`; recycled cards remove their coordinates on disposal. A new bridge test failed on the old single-index event and passes with distinct indices. Native geometry, RTL and lazy-item recycling still require an Android device check.
- The public `heroAlignment` and `isExtended` parameters previously had no effect on any platform. The available [Compose carousel APIs](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/package-summary) expose a centered hero, multi-browse, and uncontained layout without these controls. Nondefault values now fail explicitly rather than appearing to work; the inert `heroAlignment` property is no longer sent over the native bridge. The default centered hero still uses the official composable. A future API may replace these legacy parameters if Android adds a matching official layout.
