The row keeps its 40 dp or caller-specified minimum and uses [`wrapContentHeight(unbounded = true)`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/layout/package-summary) to measure beyond the temporary Flutter platform-view constraint; [`onSizeChanged`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/layout/onSizeChanged.modifier) reports the result to the existing measured-height bridge. A focused test first reproduced the 40 dp clipping after a 64 dp native report, then passed for growth, shrinkage and stable AndroidView state. This follows the official [`SingleChoiceSegmentedButtonRow` guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/segmented-button). Actual large-font geometry and first-frame timing still need a device check.
- Native-event audit: the official [single-choice row](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/segmented-button) keeps selection in caller state; the package likewise forwards Compose's selected index to Flutter. A delayed platform event could previously invoke `onValueChanged` after the control was disabled or its segment list shortened. Flutter now checks the current enabled flag and index range before dispatching. All seven focused native segmented-control tests passed. The Compose row, native check icon and selected-state visuals are unchanged; device selection and accessibility remain to verify.

## Tabs

- Official sources: [Compose tabs guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tabs), [`PrimaryTabRow`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/PrimaryTabRow.composable), [`TabRowDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TabRowDefaults), and the AndroidX [Material 3 tab height token](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/b827e60cec1f802a9fa2dbfbe6a0ddd7576bafca/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Tab.kt).
- The Flutter host reserved 64 dp for a text-only `PrimaryTabRow`, while the native tab height token is 48 dp. This left a 16 dp blank strip below the actual tabs. The host now reserves 48 dp. When `backgroundColor` and `selectedColor` are absent, Compose now uses `TabRowDefaults.primaryContainerColor` and `primaryContentColor` rather than a forced transparent container and generic primary role. Explicit caller colors still take precedence.
- The fixed native row compressed or truncated longer/more numerous tab labels. [Compose's scrollable primary tab row](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/PrimaryScrollableTabRow.composable) is intended for tabs that do not fit. Android now measures the labels at the theme's `titleSmall` size, accounts for the native 90 dp minimum and horizontal label padding, and selects the real `PrimaryScrollableTabRow` only when the fixed row cannot fit. Compose owns its scroll position and indicator motion.
- Still to verify on a device: indicator geometry, long labels, selection animation, RTL, font scaling and the native/Flutter page transition pairing.
- The tab host now begins at the 48 dp text-tab token but follows the measured height of the real `PrimaryTabRow` or `PrimaryScrollableTabRow`. Both native row variants sit inside a height-unbounded Compose measurement and report their actual size to the existing Flutter bridge. This follows the [official tabs guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tabs) and [Compose layout-constraint behavior](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/layouts/constraints-modifiers). A focused test failed on the prior fixed 48 dp host after a 72 dp native report, then passed for growth, shrinkage and stable AndroidView state. Large-font geometry and first-frame layout remain device checks.
- The current [official Compose tab example](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tabs) allows two label lines and uses ellipsis when the text exceeds them. The native bridge had forced one clipped line and selected the scrollable row whenever the unwrapped label widths exceeded the screen. It now measures each label in the fixed tab's available text width with a two-line limit; when they all fit and retain the native 90 dp minimum, `PrimaryTabRow` remains fixed. Otherwise `PrimaryScrollableTabRow` remains the real native overflow path. Both rows render up to two lines with ellipsis, and the measured-height host can grow with that text. Device checks remain for long translated labels, large font scales, indicator placement and RTL.
- Tab/page lifecycle audit: the Compose tab row takes its labels from Flutter while its `PageView` remains Flutter, so the two lists must retain the same count. The package previously accepted mismatched lists and kept an out-of-range `_currentIndex` after a parent shortened them. It now reports a clear Flutter error for mismatched counts, clamps selection when the list shrinks, moves the page controller to that valid page after layout, and ignores stale native selection indices. A new native-event test exposed a separate motion bug: `SchedulerBinding.currentFrameTimeStamp` throws when Compose's platform-channel callback begins a Flutter scroll outside a Flutter frame. The velocity bridge now uses its own monotonic `Stopwatch`, valid in both frame and platform-event contexts. The eight focused native/general tab tests failed on the old clock and pass after the fix. Native row indicator and Flutter page-spring synchronization still need device observation.
- Follow-up source audit: the [official Compose tab guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tabs) uses a two-line text tab inside `PrimaryTabRow`, matching the package's current text slot. AndroidX's [primary navigation tab tokens](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/268d841a45644cadf438fc335c793869728449ec/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/tokens/PrimaryNavigationTabTokens.kt) use a 48 dp text-only container, primary active label color and on-surface-variant inactive label color; the current native bridge uses those same defaults and switches to the real `PrimaryScrollableTabRow` when its measured two-line labels cannot fit. The [AndroidX release page](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/compose-material3) still lists 1.5.0-rc01 as the latest release candidate on 2026-11-10. No tab implementation change or build was needed in this pass; device indicator, text-scale, RTL and Flutter-page motion checks remain.

## Selection controls

- Official sources: Compose [`SwitchDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SwitchDefaults), [`CheckboxDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/CheckboxDefaults) and [`RadioButtonDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/RadioButtonDefaults).
- The native switch, checkbox and radio all overrode their checked colors with generic `primary`/`onPrimary` roles even when callers did not request custom colors. Absent overrides now retain the real Compose theme tokens for enabled, disabled and checked states. Checkbox and radio color factories accept `Color.Unspecified` as a fallback; the pinned switch factory does not, so the switch must start with `SwitchDefaults.colors()` and apply optional colors through `SwitchColors.copy`. Explicit `activeColor`, `thumbColor` and `checkColor` still reach the native color factories.
- The Flutter radio bridge marked a selected, non-toggleable radio as lacking a callback. Because the native dispatcher combines `enabled` with `hasCallback`, this made the selected option disabled and changed its appearance and accessibility state. It now forwards callback availability independently of selection. Native radio remains selected and enabled until the app actually passes `onChanged: null`.
- Still to verify on a device: keyboard traversal, TalkBack selected/disabled announcements, touch ripples, high-contrast and dynamic wallpaper palettes, and tri-state checkbox transitions.
- Radio callback audit: Flutter 3.47.9 specifies that tapping an already selected radio does not invoke `onChanged` unless `toggleable` is true. The native Compose `RadioButton` still receives clicks when selected, so the bridge now suppresses only the redundant event while retaining the real native control and its interaction feedback. A selected toggleable radio still emits `false` so Dart passes `null` to the group callback. Device ripple, keyboard and TalkBack checks remain.
- The official [Compose switch guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/switch) and pinned AndroidX `Switch.kt` offer a real `thumbContent` slot sized by `SwitchDefaults.IconSize` (16 dp), with `SwitchColors` providing its checked/unchecked and disabled tints. `AdaptiveSwitch` and `AdaptiveSwitchListTile` now expose optional Android-only checked and unchecked native thumb icons. The bridge selects the icon for the current state and lets the Compose `Switch` draw, color and animate its own thumb; absent icons leave the native default intact. Android device transition, contrast and accessibility checks remain.
- The exact pinned `SwitchDefaults.colors` overload stores its arguments directly, unlike the checkbox and radio factories that resolve `Color.Unspecified` against defaults. The standalone native switch formerly passed unspecified values for an absent `activeColor` or `thumbColor`, potentially drawing its checked track or thumb without a usable color. It now copies the actual theme defaults and overrides only supplied colors. A supplied `thumbColor` applies to both enabled positions; disabled colors stay with Material's native tokens. Capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed, while real checked/unchecked paint still needs device verification.
- Checkbox source audit against the pinned AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 `Checkbox.kt`: the package uses real `Checkbox`/`TriStateCheckbox`, native 48 dp touch layout, and native color tokens when no override is supplied. The tri-state callback cycles off → on → indeterminate → off, matching Flutter 3.47.9. `AdaptiveCheckbox` now enforces its documented `tristate || value != null` precondition in debug builds, as Flutter's `Checkbox` does; previously the Android route silently rendered an invalid null value as indeterminate even with `tristate: false`. Device focus, hover, ripple and state announcements remain open.
- Focus/hover color contract: Flutter's [`Checkbox.focusColor`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/Checkbox/focusColor.html) and equivalent Radio colors customize their Material state overlay. Official Compose [`Checkbox`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Checkbox.composable) and [`RadioButton`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/RadioButton.composable) expose token colors and `interactionSource`, but no separate `focusColor`/`hoverColor` parameters. The native bridge previously accepted the public Flutter overrides and silently dropped them. With either override, the Android adapter now keeps the existing Flutter control so the requested colors remain active; default cases still use the real Compose checkbox/radio with native motion. Two focused tests failed first on an unexpected Android platform view; all eight focused selection/radio tests and targeted Dart analysis pass. On-device focus and hover appearance remain open.
- Event-boundary audit against the official [checkbox](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/checkbox), [radio](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/radio-button) and [switch](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/switch) guides: these real Compose controls emit state transitions, but delayed native events can arrive after Flutter has rebuilt with a different state. The Flutter bridge now accepts only the next checkbox state (including the configured tri-state cycle), a radio select or permitted toggle-off, and the opposite switch state. This prevents a binary checkbox from reporting an indeterminate value and a selected non-toggleable radio from clearing. Four invalid-event tests failed before the bridge changes; all 23 focused selection tests passed afterward. Native touch timing and accessibility remain device checks.
- Standalone selection-label audit: the official [checkbox guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/checkbox) and [radio guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/radio-button) pair controls with descriptive text, while the [Compose semantics guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/semantics) preserves built-in role and state properties when additional semantics are applied. `AdaptiveCheckbox`, `AdaptiveRadio`, and `AdaptiveSwitch` previously offered no way to name a standalone native control. Each now accepts an optional `semanticLabel` forwarded to the real Compose control through `Modifier.semantics { contentDescription = ... }`, without clearing its checked/selected state. The radio and switch Flutter fallback also receive a label; Flutter's checkbox receives its built-in `semanticLabel`. The new bridge test failed on missing public parameters before the change and passed afterward. Capped offline one-worker Kotlin compilation succeeded with a 768 MiB heap after memory rose to 36% free. On-device TalkBack verification remains pending.
- Rechecked the current official [radio-button guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/radio-button) and pinned AndroidX 1.5.0-rc01 `RadioButton.kt`. Its real `RadioButton` animates the dot with the fast spatial spring and color with the default effects spring, which the package inherits by calling that composable. The guide makes `Modifier.selectableGroup()` essential for a group rendered in one Compose hierarchy, and recommends `onClick = null` on the inner radio only when a surrounding Compose `Row.selectable` owns the action. The package's public `AdaptiveRadio` is a standalone per-option platform view, so its own nonnull native click is appropriate; separate Flutter-hosted options are not a single Compose selectable group. Group-level TalkBack navigation cannot be claimed from source inspection and remains a device check or future group-level API decision. The pinned `Checkbox.kt` likewise confirms that the package uses the actual `TriStateCheckbox` and its native checked-state motion rather than a drawn substitute.

## Dropdown and context menus

- Official sources: [Compose `DropdownMenuItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/DropdownMenuItem.composable), [`MenuDefaults.itemColors`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/MenuDefaults), [Compose `ButtonDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ButtonDefaults), [Compose `IconButtonDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButtonDefaults), and [minimum touch targets](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults).
- The anchored popup, inline popup and long-press context menu wrote `onSurface` or `error` directly into their `Text` and icon slots. This bypassed `DropdownMenuItem`'s disabled text/icon colors, so disabled options could appear enabled. These routes now leave slot colors to the native item and apply destructive foreground overrides through `MenuDefaults.itemColors`; Compose retains its disabled palette and accessibility state.
- The inline popup trigger reserved only 32 dp for a text button or 44 dp for an icon button in Flutter. This could constrain Compose's native small button and 48 dp touch target. Android hosts now reserve at least 48 dp, while a caller's larger text-button height reaches Compose. Text triggers use `ButtonDefaults` height-dependent shapes, content padding, type and icon size, giving the native pressed shape transition; icon triggers use the corresponding native filled, tonal, outlined or plain icon button with its own shape transition. Without an explicit tint, the native button's content color remains in control.
- The icon-popup API exposed a `size` value but only changed the Flutter host; Compose always drew a small button and icon. The value now crosses the bridge, and Compose chooses the largest official XS/S/M/L/XL `IconButtonDefaults` container that fits it. The icon size, resting shape and pressed shape come from the same token tier. Extra host space centers the native button; host touch space remains at least 48 dp.
- The Flutter long-press context menu, custom-child anchored popup, native action sheet, modal navigation drawer and both native alert-dialog variants ran selection callbacks from native events before their overlay routes closed. An action calling `Navigator.pop` could therefore close only the menu, sheet, drawer or dialog. These bridges now await the route result before invoking the chosen action. The drawer also closes its Flutter scaffold placeholder first. Widget tests confirm the underlying route is current at callback time.
- The inline text trigger initially gives Compose the parent's available width so its real button can measure without clipping. Compose then reports the trigger's size and Flutter narrows only the platform view's hit area. On channel creation, Flutter also queries the last measured size so an early native layout event cannot leave the host wide. A changed label, height, style or inherited text scale releases the old width for another native measurement.
- The pinned AndroidX 1.5.0-rc01 [`Menu.kt` and `MenuDefaults.kt` source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3-android/1.5.0-rc01/material3-android-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) exposes an expressive `DropdownMenuItem` overload with an explicit shape, native effect-spring color transitions where item state changes, and a supporting-text slot. The package's top-app-bar overflow, inline popup, anchored popup and context menu pass `MenuDefaults.itemShape` for each item's position; popup dividers start a new shape group and use `HorizontalDividerPadding`. Popup subtitles use the official `supportingText` slot. The source forwards plain action rows with `selected = false`, and their `shapeByInteraction` follows selection, not press. Therefore those rows have native ripple and grouped resting shapes, but no pressed-shape morph to verify. Selectable and checkable rows use the AndroidX selected-state shape transition. The button-group overflow has no item position from `ButtonGroupScope.customItem` and uses the official selectable/checkable item shapes without guessing one.
- The same pinned source's `AndroidMenu.android.kt` keeps `DropdownMenu` composed while its `MutableTransitionState` is entering or exiting (`currentState || targetState`). The inline popup already queues a selection until this native exit finishes, but the anchored popup and context menu previously emitted `selected` or `dismissed` immediately. Flutter then disposed their overlay route and cut off the Compose exit. Both menus now set `expanded = false`, retain the first pending event, and deliver it when the menu content leaves composition after the AndroidX transition. Flutter still resolves selected IDs against its current enabled entries before calling the app. Menu exit timing and rapid repeated taps remain Android device checks.
- The native context menu returned a bare index after its overlay route closed and then called the old widget's action callback. A changed action list could trigger the wrong callback; a stale event also invoked a disabled action. Its stateful Flutter bridge now resolves the native item ID against the current enabled list and ignores removed actions. The Flutter menu fallback also uses that ID for delayed selection. Two native tests failed before this change; a third test reproduced the same reorder error in the Flutter fallback before its ID fix. All six context-menu tests pass. Native route timing remains a device check.
- Still to verify on a device: native size-event delivery and first-frame width, neighboring touch targets, icon-popup sizes across all five tiers, disabled contrast with a dynamic wallpaper palette, destructive actions, pointer/keyboard focus, menu position near screen edges, hover, long labels, custom button heights, and dismissal. Bitmap menu images do not automatically adopt disabled icon tint and need a separate visual check.

## Native text input

- Official sources: [Compose text input guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/text/user-input), [state-based migration](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/text/migrate-state-based), [`TextFieldState`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/text/input/TextFieldState), and [`OutlinedTextField`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedTextField.composable).
- Prior finding: the Android bridge sent only a `String`, collapsed every Flutter selection at the end, and ignored the selection property on the native side. Compose's value-based `String` overload could not report the IME composing range. The official guidance recommends state-based input for these cases.
- Current change: Compose owns a `TextFieldState` and reports text, selection and IME composition through `snapshotFlow`. Flutter applies its input formatters to structured edits and returns corrections or programmatic controller changes with an external revision; ordinary native event echoes do not reset active composition. The installed Compose foundation is 1.12.0, while `InputTransformation.maxLengthReject` starts in 1.13.0-beta02, so a compatible native `InputTransformation` rejects excess input without replacing the whole value at every keystroke. The native semantics announce the maximum text length, and masked numeric input requests a password keypad. Five focused Flutter bridge tests and a capped Kotlin compile passed. Device checks remain for mid-string insertion, range replacement, long-press selection, CJK composition, formatter feedback, programmatic edits, text length limits and IME submission.
- Obscured editable single-line fields now use the official [`OutlinedSecureTextField`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedSecureTextField.composable), which disables inappropriate copy/cut/drag actions. The API has no `readOnly` parameter; read-only or multiline obscured fields use the Flutter route to preserve their behavior. Native keyboard actions submit the latest `TextFieldState` value and invoke Compose's default action for Done/Next/Previous behavior. [Keyboard action guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/text/user-input#keyboard-actions).
- Native text style now starts from `MaterialTheme.typography.bodyLarge`, retaining its line height and letter spacing unless the caller overrides individual attributes. The official [`OutlinedTextFieldDefaults.roundedShape`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedTextFieldDefaults) is recommended for Expressive styling and now shapes both ordinary and secure fields. [Material Web's M3 text-field documentation](https://github.com/material-components/material-web/blob/main/docs/components/text-field.md) shows a counter when a maximum length is set; the native supporting slot now places helper/error text at the start and the counter at the end. Android ICU's [character BreakIterator](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/icu/text/BreakIterator) counts logical characters for native limits and the counter, closer to Flutter's [grapheme-based `maxLength` contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/TextField/maxLength.html) than UTF-16 length. ICU's Unicode data varies by Android release, so complex emoji sequences need device checks.
- The public `AdaptiveTextField.style` accepted italic, letter spacing and relative line height, but its native bridge silently dropped all three. It now sends those values into the real Compose field's `TextStyle` for both ordinary and secure inputs. The shared supported-style check also keeps unsupported per-widget families, fallback families and Flutter-specific text effects on the Flutter `TextField` route, rather than rendering a different native font without notice. Flutter's [font-family fallback contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/painting/TextStyle/fontFamilyFallback.html) is ordered per glyph; the current native font loader does not reproduce that contract for one-off fields. Two focused tests failed before the change; all 19 field/form bridge tests and capped Kotlin compilation pass afterward. Actual line height, font scaling and IME text rendering need a device check.
- Compose's source uses a 56 dp minimum field, 16 dp supporting line and 4 dp supporting top padding. The Flutter host now reserves that 20 dp line for a character counter even without helper/error text. The host still uses a fixed estimate for multiline content and large font scaling; wrapped supporting text may require a dynamically sized host. Sources: [outlined text-field layout](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/2c498ed799191b8b273eccae8ed99a5c3fe14ade/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedTextField.kt), [text-field internal tokens](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/6f64060ca62af57e2c1199b9a80e11948ac635ca/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/internal/TextFieldImpl.kt).
- Multiline keyboard audit: Flutter 3.47.9 `TextField` defaults to `TextInputType.multiline` when `maxLines != 1` and to `TextInputAction.newline` when the keyboard is multiline. The bridge previously sent `text`/`done` for `AdaptiveTextField(maxLines: 3)` and collapsed an unbounded `maxLines: null` to one native line. It now forwards the matching effective keyboard and newline action. Compose maps newline/none to `ImeAction.None` with no custom action handler, leaving the real field and IME to insert Return; explicit Done/Go/Search/Send still submit. The unbounded field and form field now use a dynamic native host, described below. Both focused tests failed before the keyboard change; all 12 bridge tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Keyboard behavior and large-font height remain Android runtime checks. Sources: [Flutter TextField constructor and action contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/TextField-class.html), [Compose keyboard options](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/text/KeyboardOptions).
- Unbounded multiline native host: Compose's `Modifier.wrapContentHeight(unbounded = true)` measures the actual `OutlinedTextField` height beyond the temporary platform-view constraint. `onSizeChanged` sends that height in dp through the existing event channel; Flutter updates the existing AndroidView host without remounting it. The root aligns text fields to the top during the initial measurement. This replaces the temporary Flutter fallback for `maxLines: null` in both text and form fields. One focused test failed before the change and passed afterward, including growth, shrinkage and stable AndroidView state. The full 12-test bridge suite, targeted Dart analysis, capped offline one-worker Kotlin compilation and `git diff --check` passed. A native device is still needed to verify intrinsic measurement, IME insertion and one-frame resize behavior. Sources: [Compose `wrapContentHeight` documentation](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/layout/package-summary), [Compose `onSizeChanged` documentation](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/layout/onSizeChanged.modifier).
- The same native height measurement now covers bounded single-line and multiline fields. Previously the Dart bridge accepted `measuredHeight` only when `maxLines == null`, so supporting text that wrapped, larger Android fonts, and ordinary fields growing toward their line limit stayed inside a fixed 56/76 dp estimate and could clip. An initial height based on `minLines` keeps the first frame usable; Compose then reports the actual field height, including its supporting slot. A focused test failed on the old `autoHeight: false` path and passed after it accepted the measured height for both text and form fields. The 15-case bridge suite and targeted Dart analysis passed. The cross-process intrinsic size, font scaling and transient resize still need on-device verification.
- The same detached-`FocusNode` bug affected the native text and form field: app code could call `requestFocus()` but no Flutter `Focus` widget ever attached the node. `AndroidM3ETextField` now hosts its effective node with `Focus.withExternalFocusNode`, sends programmatic focus changes to its existing Compose `FocusRequester`, and reflects native focus events into the Flutter focus tree. The parent disables focus for a disabled field while respecting caller-owned node settings. Two tests failed before attachment, then two disabled-state tests failed before the parent guard; all 24 combined search/text bridge tests and targeted Dart analysis now pass. Native keyboard traversal, IME and focus handoff remain device checks. Sources: [Flutter `Focus`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/Focus-class.html) and [Compose focus requests](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/touch-input/focus/change-focus-behavior).
- The native `AdaptiveTextFormField` previously wrapped `AndroidM3ETextField` in a plain `FormField<String>` that only called `didChange` for native typing. Programmatic changes to an external `TextEditingController` left validation and save callbacks on the old value, and `Form.reset()` did not restore that controller. The native route now uses a `FormFieldState` that owns or listens to the effective controller, synchronizes changed text, handles controller replacement, and restores the initial text on reset, following the installed Flutter `TextFormField` state behavior. Four focused form/controller tests cover external validation, controlled and uncontrolled reset, and controller replacement; Android device input and restoration remain to verify.
- Prefix/suffix icon fidelity: official [`OutlinedTextField`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedTextField.composable) and [`OutlinedSecureTextField`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedSecureTextField.composable) accept real Compose icon slots, while Flutter [`InputDecoration`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/InputDecoration/prefixIcon.html) can specify child icon size, color, semantic label and a static prefix/suffix icon color. The bridge previously sent only icon names. It now passes explicit child size/color/label and a static decoration tint into both native field variants, with an explicit child color taking precedence. Flutter icon paint effects and state-dependent `WidgetStateColor` decoration values keep the Flutter field because this bridge cannot reproduce their hover/focus variation. Four focused tests failed before the change; all 23 text-field bridge tests, capped Kotlin compilation and full Flutter analysis pass afterward. Device icon geometry, focus color and TalkBack remain to verify.

## Lists

- Official sources: [M3 lists overview](https://m3.material.io/components/lists/overview), [Compose `ListItem` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ListItem.composable), [Compose Material 3 release notes](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/compose-material3), and [AndroidX list implementation](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/androidx-main/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ListItem.kt).
- The pinned Compose 1.5 release includes expressive `ListItem` overloads. The old `headlineContent` overload is deprecated and uses baseline styling; the newer overloads supply native selection, disabled states, colors, shape morphing and interaction feedback. The Compose tokens define 56 dp one-line and 72 dp two-line minimum heights, with 10 dp top and bottom content padding.
- Current change: `AdaptiveListTile` now uses the expressive native `ListItem` overload. Tappable tiles use its selected overload and native click/long-click handling; non-tappable tiles use the new noninteractive overload. Flutter's `padding` is passed as internal `contentPadding`, and the platform host reserves the token height plus any extra vertical padding and text scaling. Explicit background color still overrides the container color.
- Current extension: switch tiles, expansion headers, and app-bar search suggestions now use the expressive list overloads as well. A switch tile without a separate `onTap` makes the whole native row toggle the switch and exposes switch state; with `onTap`, tile and switch keep their separate actions. A null `onChanged` disables the switch. The expansion header now uses the native click surface, token height and internal padding; its default chevron rotates with the active expressive spatial motion spec, and expanded/collapsed color overrides are applied by state. Search suggestions receive Compose's own click feedback and shape motion.
- Native switch rows used a fixed 72 dp Flutter host even without supporting text. The pinned AndroidX 1.5.0-rc01 `ListItem.kt` chooses the 56 dp one-line token and adds 8 dp vertical padding around its tallest child. `Switch.kt` reserves a 48 dp interactive target only when its own `onCheckedChange` is present: a row-level switch action therefore starts at 56 dp, while separate row and switch actions need 64 dp. Supporting text starts at the two-line 72 dp token. The host listens for the real Compose `ListItem` height through `wrapContentHeight(unbounded = true)` and `onSizeChanged`, so longer content and larger Android text can grow it without recreating the platform view. Focused tests failed on both the old fixed host and the one-line initial estimate before passing. Device measurement, wrapping and switch touch bounds still need verification.
- A list tile with `onLongPress` but no `onTap` previously wrapped the noninteractive native `ListItem` in `combinedClickable(onClick = {})`. That created a false click action and focus target. The real `ListItem` now keeps its noninteractive overload; [Compose's long-press detector](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/gestures/package-summary) handles only the requested gesture, and a matching `onLongClick` semantics action exposes it to accessibility without a dummy click. Disabled rows install neither action. The capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed; TalkBack and touch behavior still need device checks.
- The noninteractive `ListItem` overload always passes `selected = false` to its internal color and shape transition. A selected read-only row therefore retained the unselected shape, and the package supplied only an approximate container/text color. It now feeds the public [`ListItemDefaults.shapes()`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ListItemDefaults) selected shape and all corresponding `ListItemColors` roles into that same native overload. The real Compose component still animates changed shape with FastSpatial and color with DefaultEffects. The capped Kotlin compile passed; selected-to-unselected morph and disabled selection still need device inspection.
- The [M3 list guide](https://m3.material.io/components/lists/overview) also defines a segmented style, and the pinned Compose artifact exposes real [`SegmentedListItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SegmentedListItem.composable) overloads. `AdaptiveListTile` now accepts an Android group index/count and chooses those native overloads with `ListItemDefaults.segmentedShapes(index, count)` and `segmentedColors()`. Compose owns the 2 dp `SegmentedGap`; Flutter mirrors that token in its fixed host height so no gap is left after the last item. Missing/out-of-range group positions and unsupported Flutter content fail explicitly on Android. The focused Flutter test first failed on the missing API, then passed for first/last geometry and invalid input; capped Kotlin compilation passed. Grouped visual shape, selection motion and TalkBack remain device checks.
- `AdaptiveListTile` also now uses the shared measured-height host. Compose measures its real `ListItem` or `SegmentedListItem` in an unbounded-height column and reports the height including `ListItemDefaults.SegmentedGap`; Flutter updates the existing platform view instead of holding the 56/72 dp estimate when text wraps, font scale grows, or custom padding changes the item. A focused test failed on the previous fixed host and passed after growth and shrinkage without remount. The same host helper serves switch rows. Eleven focused list/switch tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed.
- The `AdaptiveExpansionTile` header now uses that measured-height host as well. Its native expressive `ListItem` uses unbounded height for intrinsic measurement and reports its size while retaining the official shape/selection and chevron spring. A focused test failed at the old fixed 72 dp header and passed with a 120 dp native measurement and stable `AndroidView` state; all 17 focused expansion tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. The Flutter child expansion animation remains separate because its arbitrary child widgets are Flutter content.
- Expansion header style parity: [Compose `Text`](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/text/style-text) supports size, weight, italic style, letter spacing, relative line height, alignment, wrapping, maximum lines and overflow within the real `ListItem` slots. The Android bridge previously forwarded only color and discarded those explicit Flutter `Text` settings. It now serializes the supported options for title and subtitle into native `Text` parameters, including an explicit normal style so an italic inherited style does not leak through. Flutter-specific fonts, text effects and other settings that cannot be reproduced by this bridge use the framework `ExpansionTile`, preserving the caller's text and enabled state. Tests failed first on the missing options and unrecognized custom font; all 19 expansion tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped Kotlin compilation passed after the correction. Exact native type metrics and custom-font fallback appearance still need device review.
- Expansion icon-slot parity: official Compose [`ListItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ListItem.composable) slots accept real [`Icon`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Icon.composable) content with size modifier, tint and content description. `AdaptiveExpansionTile` previously forwarded only an icon name, so explicit Flutter leading/trailing size, color and semantic label were discarded. Both child icons now send those values into the native slots; an explicit child tint takes precedence over the tile's expanded/collapsed icon color, and the default chevron keeps its existing Compose spring rotation. Unsupported Flutter icon effects keep the framework `ExpansionTile`. Two focused tests failed before the change; all 21 expansion tests, capped Kotlin compilation and full Flutter analysis pass. Native icon geometry and TalkBack remain device checks.
- List item style parity: `AdaptiveListTile` previously sent every styled `Text` title or subtitle to Flutter `ListTile`, even for attributes supported by Compose's real [`Text` composable](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/text/style-text). The list and expansion bridges now share one support check and serialization path; native `ListItem` and `SegmentedListItem` retain common explicit typography and color through their Compose slots. Unsupported Flutter font assets/effects keep the regular-item fallback, and segmented items still fail explicitly rather than silently losing their native grouping. A focused test failed red on the absent `AndroidView`, then seven list and four expansion bridge tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Device typography, inherited text styles and first-frame height remain to verify.
- Icon slot parity: Flutter [`Icon`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/Icon-class.html) exposes explicit size, color and semantic label; Compose [`Icon`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Icon.composable) exposes matching modifier, tint and content description in the real [`ListItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ListItem.composable) slots. The list bridge formerly sent only the icon name and always passed a null native description. It now forwards those three values to both leading and trailing native icons. Explicit Flutter icon effects and font axes unsupported by this bridge keep a regular item in Flutter or report an unsupported segmented child. A focused test failed first on the missing native size; all nine list tests, capped one-worker Kotlin compilation, and full Flutter analysis pass. Device appearance and TalkBack checks remain open.
- Switch-row style parity: `AdaptiveSwitchListTile` formerly rejected even a simple `TextStyle(fontWeight: ...)`. It now reuses the same supported-text serializer and the real expressive `ListItem` title/supporting slots. Unsupported custom fonts use Flutter's existing `ListTile`/`Switch` rendering instead of throwing; the `secondary` slot remains explicitly unsupported in the native path. Two focused tests failed on the old exception, then all eight switch-row, seven list-item and four expansion tests passed. Targeted Dart analysis and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Device text metrics, switch semantics and fallback appearance remain open.
- Remaining device checks: shape morph, touch/hover/ripple, selected and disabled semantics, switch row versus trailing-switch focus, expansion arrow direction, actual text wrapping, large font scales and custom padding. The first Flutter frame still uses a token-based estimate until Compose sends its size; device timing and clipping need inspection.

## Action sheet

- Official sources: [Compose `ModalBottomSheet`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ModalBottomSheet.composable), [Compose `SheetState`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SheetState), [Compose `ListItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ListItem.composable), and [Material 3 release notes](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/compose-material3).
- The AndroidX release page checked on 2026-11-10 still lists Material 3 `1.4.0` as stable and `1.5.0-rc01` as the latest release candidate. The package's pinned `1.5.0-rc01` therefore remains the current official path for the expressive APIs it uses; a newer stable Material 3 artifact is not yet listed.
- The action sheet already used Compose `ModalBottomSheet`, but its state came from deprecated `rememberModalBottomSheetState`, its container and scrim were forced to local color choices, and its action rows were manually assembled from `Row`, `clickable` and a fixed rounded shape. Selecting an action removed the Flutter host immediately, before the native sheet's exit animation.
- Current change: use `rememberBottomSheetState(initialValue = Hidden, enabledValues = {Hidden, Expanded})` to preserve the no-partial behavior with the current API. Let `ModalBottomSheet` use its native container, scrim, shape, handle and insets. Render actions through expressive `ListItem` so native interaction shapes and feedback apply. Action selection waits for `SheetState.hide()` before notifying Flutter; repeated taps are ignored during closing.
- Interruption follow-up: the pinned [`SheetState.hide()`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SheetState) can be interrupted by another gesture or animation. Previously that canceled the action coroutine while `selectionPending` stayed true, leaving the still-visible sheet unable to select or dismiss. The action now checks `SheetState.isVisible` in `finally`: a hidden sheet completes the selection, and a visible sheet clears the pending flag for another interaction. This follows the [official bottom-sheet example](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/bottom-sheets), which removes the host only after the state is no longer visible. Kotlin compilation passed; rapid tap/drag and predictive-Back races remain Android runtime checks.
- Dismissal-source audit: the [official `ModalBottomSheet` implementation](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/e42756c681033003da3719e052ee9f5adce8c643/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ModalBottomSheet.kt) sends `onDismissRequest` after its own `hide()` settles for scrim, drag-handle and gesture dismissal. The package's `dismissed` event therefore removes its Flutter host after the native exit in those paths. The selection branch separately waits on `SheetState.hide()`. Predictive Back uses a distinct completion callback in AndroidX, so interrupted Back and simultaneous selection still need on-device timing review; no extra package animation was added.
- Still to verify on a device: entrance and exit motion, scrim tap, drag-to-dismiss, predictive Back, nested focus/TalkBack, long lists with system insets and keyboard, destructive/default colors, and dismissal race behavior. There is no connected Android device in the current environment.
- Rechecked the current [official `ModalBottomSheet` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ModalBottomSheet.composable): the package leaves its sheet width, shape, container/content colors, scrim, drag handle, gesture setting, and content insets at their native defaults. The documented example previously called `Navigator.pop(context)` inside every action. Android's native route has already closed when that callback runs, so the example could pop the underlying page. The example now pops only on the Cupertino/Flutter routes that still require caller dismissal. A future public API cleanup should make dismissal timing uniform across platforms without cutting off the Compose sheet's exit animation or changing existing callbacks silently.
- Text-slot fidelity: the Android action-sheet route rejected any styled `Text`, including a title size or action color that the package already serializes for native lists and toolbars. The same supported-text bridge now forwards title, message and action-label options and explicit colors into Compose `Text` inside the real `ModalBottomSheet` and `ListItem`. The native title/message retain their Material typography when no override is supplied; default-action weight and cancel alignment remain defaults unless the caller explicitly sets them. When a title, message, action label or icon cannot be represented by Compose, the package uses its Flutter Material sheet, preserving the supplied widget. That Android fallback closes before the selected callback runs, after its route transition completes, matching the native contract. The unsupported-content test failed first on the old exception and then on the sheet remaining open; all six focused action-sheet tests now pass. Actual typography and large-text layout still need a device check.
- Icon-slot fidelity: the Android action sheet recognized an `Icon` by identifier but dropped explicit Flutter color, size and semantic label. The native action model now sends those overrides to the real Compose [`Icon`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Icon.composable) inside its official `ListItem`. Without an override, Compose retains the list item's `LocalContentColor` and normal icon size; an unlabeled decorative icon keeps a null content description. The focused widget test failed first on the missing color and then on the missing label before all seven action-sheet tests and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Visual size, destructive color precedence and TalkBack announcements need Android device review.
- Predictive-Back interruption follow-up: the pinned [Material 3 `ModalBottomSheet.kt`](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3/1.5.0-rc01/material3-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) has a dialog Back path that invokes `onDismissRequest` on hide-coroutine completion without checking `SheetState.isVisible`; other scrim, drag and nested Back paths do check it. If the hide is interrupted while the sheet remains visible, forwarding that callback would remove the Flutter host prematurely. The package now emits `dismissed` only when no selection is pending and the native sheet is actually hidden. This retains the real `ModalBottomSheet` and its animations. Capped one-worker offline Kotlin compilation and `git diff --check` pass; predictive Back interruption and simultaneous selection still need Android runtime testing.
- [Compose's accessibility defaults](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults) merge clickable `ListItem` descendants into one logical action. An action with `Text('   ')` had no meaningful visible name; an optional icon description is not a substitute for the required action label in this API. The Android bridge now rejects blank or whitespace-only action and cancel labels before opening the native sheet. Direct malformed platform-view maps skip empty rows instead of exposing an unnamed native click target. The focused regression failed on the previous behavior, then all eight action-sheet tests, targeted Dart analysis, formatting and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. TalkBack behavior still needs a device check.

## Buttons

- Official sources: [Compose `ButtonDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ButtonDefaults), [Compose button APIs](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Button.composable), and [Material button guidance](https://m3.material.io/components/buttons/overview).
- The native button bridge used a fixed Material shape, a fixed 18 dp icon, 8 dp icon spacing, 14 sp text and baseline padding. It also serialized theme colors into every native button. This bypassed the expressive button overload's pressed-shape morph and size-dependent tokens. The Android host sizes of 40/44/48 dp did not align with the official five container heights of 32/40/56/96/136 dp.
- Current change: `AdaptiveButton` maps its existing small/medium/large presets to Android 40/56/96 dp and exposes `androidSize` for all five expressive sizes without changing iOS presets. Native Compose buttons use `ButtonDefaults.shapesFor`, `contentPaddingFor`, `iconSizeFor`, `iconSpacingFor` and `textStyleFor`; caller colors and padding override the native defaults only when supplied. The extra-small 32 dp button is centered in a 48 dp Flutter host for a usable touch target. Flutter measures the label with the corresponding text role and forwards logical padding for RTL.
- Height-dependent host-width audit: the official [`ButtonDefaults` functions](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ButtonDefaults) choose text style, icon size, icon spacing and content padding from the button height. The Flutter host estimated width from the requested size preset before applying `minSize.height` or tall custom padding, while Compose used the resulting taller height. A small button raised to 96 dp reserved only 96 dp of width for native large text and padding. The host now recalculates its text/icon width tier from the actual height and remeasures when custom vertical padding crosses a tier. Compose still supplies the real button, pressed morph and tokens; Flutter only reserves its platform-view bounds. The former 96 dp width reproduced in a failing test, and the raised-height and tall-padding cases now pass. Font rasterization and very narrow parents still need device inspection.
- SF Symbol route audit: `AdaptiveButton.sfSymbol` is documented as an Apple icon route. On Android, native eligibility returned false before the native method's explicit SF Symbol error could run, so the Flutter fallback drew an unrelated circle. Android now rejects this unsupported input at the public build boundary with an actionable error directing callers to `AdaptiveButton.icon` and a Material icon. This prevents a fake icon from replacing the requested symbol in an otherwise native Android button. The focused test failed on the old circle fallback and passed after the guard; ordinary Android Material icon buttons still use the native Compose path.
- A `Row` passed to `AdaptiveButton.child` could silently drop a padded child, reorder a trailing icon to the start, or throw during build after the native eligibility check accepted two labels. The bridge now accepts only content it can serialize faithfully, forwards a trailing icon and an explicit middle gap, and uses `ButtonDefaults.contentPaddingFor(hasEndIcon = true)` for that native variant. Compose's [`Button` content slot](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Button.composable) supports text/icon content, and the pinned 1.5.0-rc01 `ButtonDefaults` source exposes both `hasStartIcon` and `hasEndIcon` padding. Custom Flutter child styling still takes the existing Flutter path instead of being discarded by the native bridge.
- Flutter's [`Row` layout algorithm](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/Row-class.html) lets `spacing`, `mainAxisSize`, and axis alignment change child placement. The native button adapter accepted a `Row(spacing: 14)` but replaced that gap with the Compose token; it also accepted a full-width `spaceBetween` row and centered the content. The adapter now forwards `Row.spacing` (including its contribution around an explicit middle `SizedBox`) and limits native serialization to compact, start-aligned, vertically centered rows. Flexible/custom row layouts keep their original Flutter child. Two focused tests reproduced both mismatches before passing; Kotlin is unchanged.
- [Compose accessibility guidance](https://developer.android.com/codelabs/jetpack-compose-accessibility) requires a localized description for a meaningful icon; the [Material 3 icon-button example](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButton.composable) supplies it on the icon inside the native control. `AdaptiveButton.icon` previously had no accessible-name parameter and `NativeM3EButton` always set the icon's `contentDescription` to null. The optional `semanticLabel` now reaches the Compose icon for icon-only buttons and the existing Flutter/iOS icon children. A plain `Icon(semanticLabel: …)` supplied through `AdaptiveButton.child` also remains on the native route. In a button with visible text, the icon stays decorative to avoid a duplicate announcement. Callers must supply a localized label for each icon-only action; there is no reliable localized name to infer from `IconData`.
- The same [official icon-button example](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButton.composable) pairs an icon-only action with `TooltipBox`/`PlainTooltip`. The real Compose `AdaptiveButton` now presents its supplied localized `semanticLabel` as a native plain tooltip for icon-only buttons, while keeping the button's expressive shape and press handling in the original `Button` overload. A visible text button does not add a redundant tooltip. Hover, long-press timing, popup placement and TalkBack announcement remain device checks.
- `AdaptiveIconButton` now maps all five native container heights and all three width options for each size through `IconButtonDefaults`. Compose owns the size-specific icon dimensions, round/square base shapes, pressed and selected shape morphs, and default variant colors. The Flutter host reserves at least 48 dp in each axis and centers smaller native controls. The native route falls back to Flutter if a selected icon cannot be serialized. A bridge audit found that Flutter initially reserved the requested width but omitted the `width` enum from native properties, so Compose always used `Uniform`; the width option is now serialized and covered by a failing-then-passing bridge test. Sources: [Compose `IconButtonDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButtonDefaults), [Compose icon button APIs](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButton.composable), [Compose `IconButtonShapes`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButtonShapes), and [Material icon button guidance](https://m3.material.io/components/icon-button/overview).
- Native-selected-icon audit: Android callers can provide `nativeSelectedIcon` without a parallel Flutter `selectedIcon`, but the bridge tested only the Flutter widget before choosing or serializing the selected icon. Consequently a checked Compose `IconToggleButton` kept the unselected icon. The bridge now treats either selected-icon source as sufficient, forwards the native-selected icon for both checked and unchecked states, and preserves a fallback accessible name from the original icon widget when no selected Flutter widget exists. The selected/unselected bridge test failed before the fix and all eight icon-button tests pass afterward. Compose still owns the checked color and shape morph; a runtime visual toggle check remains.
- The Android icon-button route previously treated `tooltip` only as an accessibility description. It now wraps a supplied nonempty tooltip in Compose `TooltipBox`/`PlainTooltip`, matching the official [icon-button example](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButton.composable) and the package's existing app-bar pattern. The caller's `semanticLabel` still takes precedence for the button's description.
- [Compose accessibility guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults) requires a textual name for a meaningful icon action. `AdaptiveIconButton` could accept an `Icon(semanticLabel: ...)`, serialize its vector, then send a null name to the real Compose button unless the wrapper supplied `semanticLabel` or `tooltip`. The native bridge now uses the selected or ordinary icon's existing label as the final fallback, after the wrapper's explicit label and tooltip. A focused bridge test reproduced the null name before the fix and passed afterward; TalkBack announcement still needs device verification.
- The current [Compose `IconButton` sample](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButton.composable) gives even a named icon a plain tooltip for long press, hover and keyboard focus, with a pane title and assertive live-region semantics. `AdaptiveIconButton` previously forwarded `Icon.semanticLabel` as its action name but left `tooltip` null, so Compose skipped that tooltip. The bridge now uses the resolved action name when no explicit tooltip is provided, preserving an explicit tooltip's precedence. The focused test failed on a null tooltip before passing, and the native `PlainTooltip` now carries the sample semantics. Kotlin compilation passed; popup placement and TalkBack timing remain device checks.
- The official [Material 3 `BadgedBox` sample](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/BadgedBox.composable) gives badge numbers a contextual `contentDescription`. `AdaptiveIconButton.badgeValue` formerly sent only raw text to its native `Badge`. Its optional `badgeSemanticsLabel` now reaches the real Compose badge text, while the icon button retains its separate action name. The focused test failed on the missing API before passing. Screen-reader merging and spoken order remain device checks.
- Icon content parity: [Flutter `Icon`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/Icon-class.html) accepts explicit size and color, while native [Compose `Icon`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Icon.composable) accepts a size modifier and tint inside the real [expressive `IconButton`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButton.composable). The package previously discarded a caller's explicit values and always used `IconButtonDefaults` icon dimensions and button content color. The bridge now passes size and tint for the visible icon, including a selected Flutter icon or a native selected icon with no separate Flutter widget. Unspecified values continue to use Compose's official size and `LocalContentColor`; unsupported Flutter icon effects use the Flutter fallback. A focused test failed on the absent size before all ten icon-button tests, capped Kotlin compilation and full Flutter analysis passed. Device icon geometry, colors and selected-state motion remain open.
- Custom filled button colors previously selected black or white text at 0.5 relative luminance. That chooses white on some midtone surfaces where black has much stronger contrast. Using the [WCAG contrast formula](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG21/Understanding/contrast-minimum), the equal-contrast crossover is approximately 0.17913; both the native Compose bridge and Flutter fallback now use it for arbitrary custom backgrounds. When no background override is supplied, Compose keeps its official `ButtonDefaults` content color and the Flutter fallback uses the theme's paired `onPrimary` role. An explicit caller text/icon color still wins. The focused midtone test failed before this change and passed afterward; actual disabled-state colors and dynamic palettes still need visual review.
- Outlined-button color parity: the official [`OutlinedButton` expressive overload](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedButton.composable) accepts a `border` and defaults to `ButtonDefaults.outlinedButtonBorder(enabled)`. `AdaptiveButton(style: bordered, color: ...)` already colored the Flutter fallback's outline, but Android serialized `color` only as foreground and always drew the default native border. The bridge now forwards a separate border color and passes a `BorderStroke` only for that explicit override; a null color keeps the official enabled/disabled default border. The focused test failed red on the missing border property, then all 26 native/general button tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Visual border width, contrast and disabled state still need device review.
- Custom button corner fidelity: the Android bridge previously sent only `BorderRadius.topLeft.x` and used it for all four corners. Distinct caller corners therefore became a uniform shape, including in RTL. It now sends all four physical circular radii to Compose's native [`AbsoluteRoundedCornerShape`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/shape/AbsoluteRoundedCornerShape) while retaining the official `ButtonDefaults.shapesFor` pressed shape. Elliptical or invalid custom corners stay on the Flutter path because this Compose shape accepts one radius per corner. A focused RTL bridge test failed on the missing four-corner property before passing. Native outline and pressed morph still need device inspection.
- Flutter's [`IconButton.enableFeedback`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/IconButton/enableFeedback.html) controls acoustic/haptic feedback, not the visual ripple. Compose UI 1.12.0 provides [`SoundEffectOnInteraction`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/platform/SoundEffectOnInteraction.composable) for per-component click sound control. The native icon-button route now uses the already-forwarded `enableFeedback` value there, leaving the official Material ripple and pressed-shape motion intact. A false value silences the Compose click sound; Compose's icon-button/tooltip haptics still need device inspection before claiming full Flutter feedback parity.
- Still to verify on-device: tooltip placement and long-press/hover/focus behavior, pressed/selected shape morph, size-specific text/icon alignment, narrow-width clipping, large font scales, disabled contrast, outlined border, RTL padding, custom minimum sizes, icon-button focus/ripple and click sound, haptic behavior, and selection semantics.

## Split buttons

- Official sources: [Compose `SplitButtonLayout`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SplitButtonLayout.composable), [`SplitButtonDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SplitButtonDefaults), and [Material split-button guidance](https://m3.material.io/components/split-button/overview).
- Menu selection follow-up: the native split-button menu used the older `DropdownMenuItem` plus a manual `selected` semantic and check icon. The pinned AndroidX [`SelectableDropdownMenuItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SelectableDropdownMenuItem.composable) owns selected shape/color transitions and RadioButton semantics. The menu rows now use it with `MenuDefaults.itemShape(index, count)` while keeping the existing check mark, disabled state, identity, and delayed selection callback after the real popup exits. Capped offline one-worker Kotlin compilation passed; native shape morph and TalkBack order still need a device check.
- The official Compose sample applies the chosen height to both segments and selects leading and trailing icon sizes, icon spacing, and text style from the same size token. The package previously changed button shapes and padding by size but left those content dimensions on generic defaults. Both segments now receive a minimum native height and their content reads the matching Compose tokens.
- The menu segment previously stayed enabled when the primary action existed even if `onSelected` was absent or all menu options were disabled. Dart now sends separate primary and menu enabled states; Compose closes an open menu and disables its trailing control when the menu cannot act.
- The official sample gives the icon-only trailing segment a `TooltipBox` and an explicit expanded/collapsed `stateDescription`. The package now applies both; Flutter supplies localized state labels through `MaterialLocalizations` or caller overrides. The menu label remains caller-localizable through `menuButtonSemanticLabel`.
- The trailing segment previously built an English `"More $label options"` description when callers omitted `menuButtonSemanticLabel`, despite the bridge already having host `MaterialLocalizations`. The bridge now supplies `moreButtonTooltip` as its localized default; callers can still provide a contextual label, which also appears in the native tooltip. The focused split-button tests and full Flutter analysis pass. Device TalkBack announcement remains to verify.
- The native split menu added a 4 dp vertical offset that is absent from the official [`SplitButtonLayout` example](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SplitButtonLayout.composable). [`DropdownMenu`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/DropdownMenu.composable) defaults to a zero offset, so the bridge now lets Compose place the anchored popup with its own default geometry. The pinned `SplitButtonLayout` measure policy already gives both segments the same measured height; adding `fillMaxHeight` to the trailing segment would duplicate that behavior. Popup edge placement and long-label layout remain device checks.
- Width and height audit: the split button reserved only a Flutter host height, so its `AndroidView` received infinite width in a horizontal scroller and otherwise filled the whole row. A focused widget test reproduced Flutter's `RenderAndroidView` infinite-size assertion. The host now estimates its first-frame width from the pinned [Compose `SplitButtonDefaults` sizes, padding and 2 dp gap](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SplitButtonDefaults), including the chosen label text scale and optional leading icon. The real `SplitButtonLayout` reports measured width and height; Flutter adjusts its host without replacing the platform view. For a bounded narrow parent, native intrinsic-width escape is disabled so Compose can wrap the label. All eight split-button tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped Kotlin compilation passed. Exact native first-frame metrics, large text wrapping and popup anchoring still need device inspection.
- Event and exit-motion audit: a queued native `primaryPressed` or `selected` event could still invoke Dart after the corresponding action or menu item became disabled. The Flutter bridge now rechecks the current enabled flags and item bounds; all nine focused split-button tests pass. Selection also used to notify Flutter in the same click that set `DropdownMenu(expanded = false)`, so a navigation callback could dispose the view before Compose rendered its exit. The pinned AndroidX [`DropdownMenu` source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3-android/1.5.0-rc01/material3-android-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) retains its popup while `MutableTransitionState.currentState || targetState`. The native bridge now holds one pending selection and sends it from the menu content's `DisposableEffect.onDispose`, after that real popup leaves composition; the trailing control cannot reopen during the pending exit. Capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Runtime menu exit, navigation timing, Back and rapid repeated taps still need Android device checks.
- Delayed-item identity audit: if the app reordered split-menu items during that native exit, a bare selected index could call the wrong current item. Flutter now assigns each item object a weakly held unique ID; Compose carries the ID with the pending selection and Dart resolves it against the current enabled items. Removed items are ignored. All ten focused split-button tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation pass. Reorder and removal are covered in the widget test; the real popup timing remains an Android device check.
- Still to verify on a device: segment sizing when the leading label wraps, separate minimum touch targets, tooltip placement, selection shape morph, keyboard order, RTL, TalkBack state announcements, and menu anchoring near screen edges.

## Badges and dividers

- Official sources: [Compose badge API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Badge.composable), [AndroidX badge implementation](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/8b69435f88b1ce9544f9d7b230473304fa46cf42/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Badge.kt), [Compose divider guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/divider), and [Flutter divider height contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/Divider/height.html).
- A Compose badge without content is the official 6 dp dot; a badge with content has a 16 dp minimum and native text padding. The package supplied a non-null content lambda even for an empty label, so its dot rendered as a text badge. It also reserved a fixed 24/32 dp width, clipping longer labels, while an unconstrained `Align` could expand the Flutter `Stack` past its child. The bridge now uses a contentless native `Badge` for dots, sizes the host from the label's scaled text, and positions the native view without changing its anchor's layout size. The existing opt-in `isLarge` setting increases the native minimum to 24 dp. Native default badge color now comes from `BadgeDefaults`.
- The badge previously forced `onError` for its text even when the caller chose a different container color and omitted `textColor`. The official [`Badge` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Badge.composable) defaults to [`contentColorFor(containerColor)`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/contentColorFor.composable). The bridge now uses that Compose mapping; an explicit caller text color still takes precedence. For arbitrary colors outside the theme, Compose falls back to `LocalContentColor`, so contrast still needs a device check.
- Label precedence: `AdaptiveBadge` lets callers provide both `count` and an overriding `label`, but its early zero-count check hid the badge even when that explicit label contained text. The visibility rule now applies `showZero` only when no label is supplied. The real Compose `Badge` receives the explicit label; its dot/text geometry and colors remain native. The focused bridge test first found no Android view before the fix, then all 33 badge/card/list/tooltip tests passed. Device placement remains a separate check.
- Dot fallback parity: the native Compose route already uses a contentless `Badge` for an empty label, but the Flutter Material route always supplied `Text('')`, drawing a text badge rather than its official dot variant. The fallback now passes a null label for empty badge text. When an explicit accessibility description exists, a separate semantics container retains it and keeps the decorated child as its own semantics node. The dot test failed on the old `Text('')` widget, and a second test caught initially merged semantics before `explicitChildNodes` was added. All 35 focused badge/card/list/tooltip tests pass; native rendering remains unchanged.
- The noninteractive badge platform view previously used an opaque Flutter hit-test target, blocking taps on the child beneath its corner. Its host now uses [`PlatformViewHitTestBehavior.transparent`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/rendering/PlatformViewHitTestBehavior.html), allowing the child to receive the tap. A focused Flutter widget test reproduced the blocked corner tap before the fix and passed afterward; Android device touch behavior still needs checking.
- The pinned [AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3-android/1.5.0-rc01/material3-android-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) places a text badge at the anchor's logical end minus 12 dp and its top minus badge height plus 14 dp; the dot uses 6 dp for both offsets. The Flutter host had aligned every badge fully inside the corner. It now follows those native `BadgedBox` offsets for the default alignment, mirrors the horizontal placement in RTL, and preserves caller-supplied alignment and offset. Focused widget tests failed for LTR and RTL positioning before the corresponding fixes and now pass. The separate Flutter child prevents using a single Compose `BadgedBox`, so actual device placement and ancestor clipping still need visual review.
- An empty dot badge has no text for accessibility services, and a bare count may not explain what changed. `AdaptiveBadge.semanticsLabel` now lets the caller provide a contextual phrase such as "5 unread messages" or "New activity". On Android the real Compose `Badge` uses `clearAndSetSemantics` only when a description is supplied, replacing any duplicate label text; the separate Flutter child keeps its own semantics. The iOS badge pill and Flutter fallback replace only the pill's text semantics in the same case. The [Compose accessibility guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults) recommends describing meaningful graphics and leaving purely decorative ones unannounced. TalkBack traversal across the Flutter/Compose boundary remains a device check.
- A custom `AdaptiveDivider.height` reserves vertical space around the line. The native `HorizontalDivider` used to sit at the top of that space. It is now centered in the platform view and reads the official `DividerDefaults.color` when no caller color is supplied. The package's existing default one-dp host height is unchanged.
- The Android divider route uses the official `HorizontalDivider` API. The bridge previously sent a literal 1 dp thickness even when the caller omitted it; Compose now reads `DividerDefaults.Thickness` for that case and still accepts explicit overrides. Start/end insets use Compose padding. The current widget is horizontal; no vertical variant is exposed. On iOS, `height: 8` previously measured 16 because 4-point top and bottom margins were added outside the declared height. The hairline is now centered inside the reserved height and horizontal insets follow text direction. Focused widget tests reproduced both old defaults and now pass.
- Geometry input audit: the [Flutter `Divider` constructor](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/Divider/Divider.html) requires nonnegative height, thickness and side insets, and [Compose padding](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/Modifier) rejects negative values. The adaptive wrapper previously forwarded negative or infinite values to the native `HorizontalDivider` host. It now rejects nonfinite and negative values for all four dimensions with a field-specific error before layout or native serialization; valid values still use the real Compose divider. Two focused tests failed on the former bridge and pass after the guard.
- Still to verify on a device: badge anchor placement, RTL offset, label width with system font scaling, TalkBack announcements, custom-color contrast, large badge appearance, and divider rendering on different screen densities.

## Pull to refresh

- The official [Compose pull-to-refresh guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/pull-to-refresh) places `PullToRefreshBox` around a Compose scrollable. `AdaptiveRefreshIndicator` accepts an arbitrary Flutter scrollable, so Flutter still recognizes the gesture. The Android path now uses `RefreshIndicator.noSpinner` and forwards its pull distance to the actual `PullToRefreshDefaults.LoadingIndicator` from pinned AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01. The native `PullToRefreshState` drives the official progress morph while dragging, animates to the 80 dp threshold during refresh, and animates to hidden before the native view is removed. The 48 dp indicator's default final center is 56 dp from the edge, following those native dimensions; an explicit caller `displacement` still sets the center. The public `displacement` getter remains 40 dp when omitted for the existing Flutter routes. This is a cross-framework gesture bridge; it does not wrap the Flutter child in Compose `PullToRefreshBox`.
- The public `AdaptiveRefreshTriggerMode.anywhere` reached the Android no-spinner gesture recognizer but was omitted from the iOS `RefreshIndicator.adaptive` and other Flutter routes, silently reverting those platforms to `onEdge`.
