A three-platform bridge test failed on iOS first; all nine focused refresh tests pass after mapping the public option once and passing it to every Flutter gesture recognizer. The Android indicator remains the real Compose `PullToRefreshDefaults.LoadingIndicator` and its native state/motion are unchanged.
- The Flutter arm point is derived from the scrollable viewport: 25% drag distance multiplied by the 2/3 visual arm point in the [active Flutter 3.47.9 source](https://github.com/flutter/flutter/blob/3.47.9/packages/flutter/lib/src/material/refresh_indicator.dart). These are private framework details and must be rechecked with a Flutter upgrade. The bridge follows Flutter's opposite scroll-delta sign for reversed lists. `onStatusChange` reports `canceled` or `done` but does not report the final internal `null`; the native view therefore sends a `hidden` event after its own exit animation, which Dart uses to dispose it. Focused tests cover short-drag cancellation and progress in both list directions.
- The active native indicator's platform view was an opaque hit-test target above the Flutter list. A focused widget test showed a tap on its area did not reach a child and passed once the view became pointer-transparent. `RefreshIndicator.noSpinner` removes the former invisible Flutter spinner and shadow. On-device checks remain for drag feedback and frame pacing, native threshold/exit motion, positioning, scrolling under the active indicator and TalkBack.
- Flutter's `RefreshIndicator.noSpinner` renders an empty container, so its `semanticsLabel` and `semanticsValue` do not label the visible native indicator. The Android bridge now sends a caller label or Flutter's localized refresh label, plus any caller value, to Compose. The native expressive loading indicator retains its own `ProgressBarRangeInfo`; a supplied value is exposed as `stateDescription`. A focused test failed before the value bridge and now passes. TalkBack must still confirm the spoken order and whether the explicit value duplicates the native progress announcement.
- The [Flutter `triggerMode.anywhere` contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/RefreshIndicator/triggerMode.html) starts a refresh drag when an ongoing scroll reaches the edge. Flutter 3.47.9's `_handleScrollNotification` returns immediately on the notification that changes status to `drag`, leaving its private drag offset at zero. The package's outer listener received that same notification after the status callback and counted the entire scroll-to-edge delta as overscroll, giving the Compose indicator 0.8 progress before any pull past the edge in a focused test. The bridge now skips exactly the first notification after entering `drag`, matching Flutter's counter, then forwards real overscroll to the native `PullToRefreshDefaults.LoadingIndicator`. The regression failed before the change; all ten refresh tests, targeted Flutter analysis and formatting pass. This is a Dart-only correction; device drag pacing and native motion remain open.

## Date and time pickers

- Official sources: [Compose `TimePickerDialog`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TimePickerDialog.composable), [expressive `VibrantTimePickerDialog`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/VibrantTimePickerDialog.composable), [date picker guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/datepickers), and [time picker guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/time-pickers).
- The native time route nested a Compose `AlertDialog` inside an additional `Dialog`, forcing two modal layers instead of using the official time-picker dialog. It also hardcoded English action labels and title. The route now uses `TimePickerDialog` on compact screens and the official expressive `VibrantTimePickerDialog` on larger screens. The same native `TimePickerState` drives dial and text input; short screens start in input mode, the native toggle switches modes when the dial fits, and invalid input disables confirmation. The dialog owns its shape, color, spacing and modal window.
- The native date route already uses `DatePickerDialog`, but its action labels were hardcoded English. Both date and time overlay calls now pass Flutter's localized confirm/cancel labels. Date confirmation is disabled when there is no selected date. Compose's public [`rememberDatePickerState`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/rememberDatePickerState.composable) supports calendar and text-input display modes but has no initial year-grid option. The Dart bridge previously sent an unused `initialYearMode` flag, silently ignoring `DatePickerMode.year`. Android now uses Flutter's actual Material year picker for explicit year-first and month-year requests, and retains the real Compose picker for ordinary calendar requests. Both year-mode widget tests and the native-date bridge test pass; native picker behavior still needs a device check.
- Picker locale follow-up: Flutter's `MaterialApp` can select a locale different from Android's current locale. Both date and time bridges send its BCP 47 tag in their initial platform-view parameters. For those views only, Android wraps the host `Context` in a [`ContextThemeWrapper`](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/view/ContextThemeWrapper) with an override [`Configuration.setLocale`](https://developer.android.com/reference/android/content/res/Configuration#setLocale(java.util.Locale)) before constructing `ComposeView`. Pinned Compose UI 1.12 source derives `AndroidComposeView.localeList` from that context's configuration. Pinned Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 source derives `DatePicker`'s calendar locale from `LocalLocale`, its built-in title/headline strings from the resource context, and `TimePicker` labels and descriptions from native resources. The official `rememberDatePickerState` remains in use, retaining its saved-state behavior. Both focused bridge tests failed first on missing tags, then passed; capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Native month names, title/headline language, time picker descriptions, RTL and locale changes while a dialog is open still need Android runtime inspection.
- Minute interval follow-up: The official [time picker guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/time-pickers) and pinned Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 `TimePicker`, `TimeInput`, `TimeScroll` and `TimePickerState` APIs expose no minute-step parameter. The package keeps the real Compose picker and snaps a confirmed result to `minuteInterval`; the initial value now snaps onto the same grid before opening the native picker, including hour rollover. The user may still choose an off-grid minute inside Compose before confirmation. The focused bridge suite passed (3 tests, serial `flutter test --no-pub --concurrency=1`) during an idle compiler window on the 8 GiB machine.
- Bounded-grid follow-up: a date-time boundary such as 10:07 could clamp the already snapped initial time to 10:07 with a 15-minute interval. That opens the real Compose picker on a value the package cannot confirm. The bridge now rounds the lower bound up and the upper bound down to selectable grid minutes, starts within that range, and skips the dialog when it contains no valid tick. A focused regression failed at 10:07 before the fix. The directly exported native time bridge now applies the same interval validation as its adaptive entry point; its zero-interval regression failed with an integer division error before the guard. All 18 picker bridge tests and targeted analysis pass afterward. Compose still owns the dial, text input, dialog and interaction; device selection and boundary behavior remain open.
- Native date-result boundary: Compose's [`SelectableDates`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SelectableDates) disables days outside the permitted UI range, but the Flutter side accepted any `submitted` timestamp from the platform channel. A late or malformed result could therefore return an out-of-range date. The bridge now rechecks the returned UTC calendar day against the configured first selectable day and last day before returning it or opening the time dialog. The regression returned November 12 from a November 9–11 range before the change; all 19 picker tests pass afterward. Device selection and result timing remain open.
- Device checks to complete after implementation: one modal layer, small-screen input fallback, dial/input focus and keyboard behavior, localized labels, date range boundaries, orientation, large font size, TalkBack and result timing.

## Alert dialogs

- Official sources: [Compose `AlertDialog`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AlertDialog.composable) and the AndroidX [Material 3 dialog layout](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/57bcd397cd83546566e9ab3ef7e70042b479ec52/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AlertDialog.kt).
- The native alert route wrapped `AlertDialog` in an extra `Dialog`, creating two modal windows and scrims. It now renders the official alert dialog directly. The action slots previously wrapped all confirming actions in a `Row`, which made them a single child of Compose's internal `FlowRow` and blocked its responsive button wrapping. The actions are now emitted as individual native text buttons into the official action layout.
- The optional alert input previously used the value-based `String` text field and truncated UTF-16 code units. It now uses the same state-based `OutlinedTextField` or `OutlinedSecureTextField` as standalone input, with the native grapheme-aware length filter, counter and expressive rounded shape. The dialog still returns its native input state with the selected action.
- Still to verify on a device: long action labels and three-plus actions, input focus and IME, predictive Back/scrim dismissal, modal transition, large text, RTL action order, color contrast for custom semantic action styles and TalkBack traversal.

## Floating toolbar

- Official sources: [M3 toolbar overview](https://m3.material.io/components/toolbars/overview), [toolbar guidelines](https://m3.material.io/components/toolbars/guidelines).
- Package path: `android/src/main/kotlin/com/ledexsoft/adaptive_liquid_glass/M3EAndroidContent.kt`.
- Finding: the horizontal selected action passed `selected` into its icon tint; the vertical `AppBarColumn` did not. Both axes now pass the state.
- The docked toolbar used literal `64.dp` height and 16 dp horizontal padding even though its native [`FlexibleBottomAppBar`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FlexibleBottomAppBar.composable) exposes `BottomAppBarDefaults.FlexibleBottomAppBarHeight` and `FlexibleContentPadding`. It now reads both official tokens. Inspection of the pinned 1.5.0-rc01 bytecode confirms they currently resolve to 64 dp and 16 dp per side, matching Flutter's 64 dp host reservation; this removes a duplicate native token definition without changing current geometry.
- The public [`HorizontalFloatingToolbar`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/HorizontalFloatingToolbar.composable) and [`VerticalFloatingToolbar`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/VerticalFloatingToolbar.composable) each expose `expandedShadowElevation` and `collapsedShadowElevation`, with different defaults depending on whether an adjacent FAB is present. `AdaptiveToolbar.elevation` had been accepted by Flutter but dropped from the Android bridge. It now sets both native shadow elevations when supplied and keeps the official per-variant defaults when omitted. The pinned `FlexibleBottomAppBar` has no such parameter; an explicit custom elevation for the docked native route now reports a field-specific error instead of being silently ignored. Runtime shadow appearance still needs a device check.
- The [official bottom-app-bar implementation](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/f92200f1c34e6b7059c87f1d746e4af08cbc750f/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AppBar.kt) applies horizontal and bottom system-bar insets by default. The package replaced all of those with a bottom-only override for its docked toolbar, leaving actions too close to side system bars in landscape or cutout layouts. Flutter now forwards the inherited physical left/right padding when `safeArea` is enabled, and Compose applies all three insets to the real `FlexibleBottomAppBar`. A bridge test covers enabled and disabled safe areas.
- Toolbar title parity: the [official floating toolbar](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/VerticalFloatingToolbar.composable) exposes composable content, so a simple styled title can remain a real Compose `Text` inside the native toolbar. The package previously serialized only the title string and discarded size, weight, color and overflow. Horizontal, vertical and docked toolbar title slots now reuse the supported-text serializer; the shared native helper retains the variant's one-line limit and existing default overflow. Unsupported Flutter fonts/effects report an error on Android: falling through to the Flutter `BottomAppBar` would change a floating toolbar's layout. A focused title test failed red on missing text options, then 29 app-bar/toolbar/list tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Device text metrics, clipping and focus remain open.
- Still to verify: standard and vibrant selection colors with custom palette roles, overflow, focus, and docked/floating geometry including landscape side insets on-device.

## Progress indicators

- Official sources: [M3 progress overview](https://m3.material.io/components/progress-indicators/overview), [Compose flat defaults](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ProgressIndicatorDefaults), [Compose wavy defaults](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/WavyProgressIndicatorDefaults), and AndroidX [circular](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/268d841a45644cadf438fc335c793869728449ec/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/tokens/CircularProgressIndicatorTokens.kt) / [linear](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/268d841a45644cadf438fc335c793869728449ec/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/tokens/LinearProgressIndicatorTokens.kt) tokens.
- The native linear and circular progress views and the contained/uncontained loading view inherited an opaque Flutter platform-view hit-test target even though none handles user input. Per [Flutter's platform-view hit-test contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/rendering/PlatformViewHitTestBehavior.html), an opaque target blocks widgets visually behind it. The three decorative hosts now use transparent hit testing. New tests failed on the old default and all thirty focused progress/loading tests passed after correction. The official Compose indicators, shapes, colors, progress specs and morph animation are unchanged; physical overlay touch behavior remains a device check.
- Finding: the Flutter host reserved 24 dp for both default circular variants, while the native flat and wavy containers use 40 and 48 dp. The default wavy linear host was 16 dp high while its native wave-height token is 10 dp. Native active and track colors were forced to generic primary/secondary-container roles instead of the API's variant-specific defaults.
- Current change: default circular geometry now matches 40/48 dp, the default circular stroke is 4 dp, wavy linear geometry starts at 10 dp, and native colors use `ProgressIndicatorDefaults` or `WavyProgressIndicatorDefaults`. An optional `androidSize` gives callers an explicit Android size while preserving iOS `radius`; existing nondefault `radius` values still size Android when no `androidSize` is set.
- Linear width finding: the package added a 4 dp inset on both sides by default, shortening the native Compose bar even though the official [flat](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LinearProgressIndicator.composable) and [wavy](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LinearWavyProgressIndicator.composable) APIs fill the width their parent gives them. The default `linearInset` and native fallback are now 0 dp; callers can still request an explicit inset.
- Explicit corner finding: `AdaptiveProgressIndicator.linear(borderRadius: ...)` forwarded only the top-left horizontal radius and the native route ignored it. The official Compose indicator has no `borderRadius` parameter, so the bridge now applies a Compose `Modifier.clip` with the supplied four elliptical corners to the real flat or wavy indicator. This leaves the native default geometry and stroke caps intact when no radius is supplied; visual clipping with large/asymmetric radii needs a device check.
- Container-height audit: the official [`LinearWavyProgressIndicator` thick example](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LinearWavyProgressIndicator.composable) pairs an 8 dp stroke with `Modifier.height(14.dp)`. Flutter reserved the corresponding 14 dp host, but Compose still used its default 10 dp container because only the stroke crossed the bridge. The computed native host height now also reaches the real Compose indicator's modifier. The flat variant receives the same container height, so an explicit `minHeight` affects the drawn indicator as well as its Flutter host. The focused Flutter test covers 8/14 dp wavy and 12 dp flat values; capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Device inspection remains for wave amplitude, stop indicator and clipping at custom heights.
- The pinned AndroidX `ProgressIndicator.kt` draws a flat linear indicator with `strokeWidth = size.height`, so `minHeight` intentionally thickens the actual flat stroke when it exceeds `trackThickness`. The wavy API instead accepts explicit active and track `Stroke` values, retaining their thickness inside a taller container. The public Dart documentation now states that difference; no native drawing replacement is needed.
- Rechecked the pinned wavy tokens and APIs: both active and track strokes are 4 dp by default, with a 10 dp linear wave container; the bridge's token sizes matched. The animation-spec distinction between linear and circular wavy progress was found in the later motion audit below. A separate bridge mismatch remained: `AdaptiveProgressIndicator` sent its raw value to native Compose while `AdaptiveLoadingIndicator` and the Cupertino path clamped values first. A nonfinite value could therefore cross the platform channel and enter `animateFloatAsState`. The circular and linear native branches now clamp before serialization. Focused tests reproduced `NaN`/negative-infinity payloads before all 12 progress tests passed; native drawing still needs device inspection.
- Loading-indicator finding: `ContainedLoadingIndicator` used the generic primary color for its morphing shape on a primary-container surface. [LoadingIndicatorDefaults](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LoadingIndicatorDefaults) defines separate contained indicator and container colors. The native branch now reads those defaults, preserving explicit caller overrides.
- The pinned 1.5.0-rc01 API also exposes determinate [`LoadingIndicator`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LoadingIndicator.composable) and [`ContainedLoadingIndicator`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ContainedLoadingIndicator.composable) overloads. The package previously exposed only the indeterminate loop. `AdaptiveLoadingIndicator.progress` now selects those official overloads, with the no-bounce, very-low-stiffness Compose spring shown in the API example; null keeps the existing loop. Flutter and Cupertino fallbacks receive a determinate value. AndroidX's [implementation](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/dd7d2935e6a5ab12f6957a813926eea1ca38188e/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LoadingIndicator.kt) supplies determinate progress semantics. Two focused Flutter tests and capped Kotlin compilation passed; device morph and TalkBack checks remain.
- The same official loading-indicator overloads accept a list of at least two `RoundedPolygon` shapes. The package already exposes the full Compose `MaterialShapes` catalog through `AdaptiveExpressiveShape`; `AdaptiveLoadingIndicator.polygons` now forwards an optional sequence into those real overloads. A null sequence selects AndroidX's own determinate or indeterminate defaults. Four focused bridge/fallback/validation tests, capped Kotlin compilation and full Dart analysis pass; custom morph appearance remains a device check.
- Pinned-source semantics audit: `ProgressIndicator.kt` and `WavyProgressIndicator.kt` attach `ProgressBarRangeInfo` to determinate indicators and indeterminate progress semantics to their looping variants. `LoadingIndicator.kt` does the same for its two overload families. The native bridge uses those composables directly and adds only the caller's content description; replacing the native semantics with a Flutter percentage would discard the official progress role. Android TalkBack remains the runtime check.
- Semantics-chain follow-up: the pinned [AndroidX `LoadingIndicator.kt`](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/2c498ed799191b8b273eccae8ed99a5c3fe14ade/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LoadingIndicator.kt) appends `progressSemantics()` or `ProgressBarRangeInfo` after the caller's `Modifier`. Foundation's [`progressSemantics()` implementation](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/fe5b47cd0b5428e99aa926e916d50ea72a328fcc/compose/foundation/foundation/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/ProgressSemantics.kt) already sets `mergeDescendants = true`; [Compose's merging guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/merging-clearing) says a parent cannot merge a child that also merges. Therefore adding a second outer merge would not prove that the optional caller label and native progress state form one TalkBack target, and that attempted change was removed. Blank optional descriptions are now ignored. A capped offline Kotlin compile passes; a device semantics-tree and TalkBack check is required before changing the remaining label composition or replacing AndroidX progress semantics.
- Native geometry input audit: the bridge previously forwarded a non-finite circular `strokeWidth`, negative linear `trackThickness`, or negative `linearInset` into the real Compose progress APIs. It also silently clamped an explicit zero `androidSize` or negative `minHeight` to a different container. Five focused tests failed before validation and pass after the Dart bridge rejects invalid dimensions with field-specific errors, before mounting the native view. Valid dimensions still reach the official flat/wavy composables and their native motion unchanged.
- Progress normalization audit: the pinned AndroidX `ProgressIndicator.kt` and `LoadingIndicator.kt` explicitly substitute 0f when a NaN progress value would enter `ProgressBarRangeInfo`; Dart's `double.nan.clamp(0.0, 1.0)` instead produced 1.0, making unknown progress look complete. Both package indicators now share a boundary normalizer that maps NaN to zero and clamps other values to 0–1 on Android and fallback platforms. Four native/fallback assertions failed before the correction and pass afterward. The loading indicator also rejects zero or non-finite `size` before the host or Compose can receive invalid geometry; two tests failed before that guard. The real Compose morph, color defaults, polygon sequence and recommended spring remain intact.
- Follow-up to that normalization: the official [Compose progress guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/progress) accepts finite determinate values from 0 to 1; [Flutter's progress contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ProgressIndicator/value.html) treats 1 as complete. Positive infinity was still clamped to 1, falsely reporting completion. The shared bridge now maps every non-finite input to 0 before forwarding it to Compose or a Flutter fallback. New tests for both native indicator APIs failed at 1.0 before the change and passed at 0.0 afterward. Finite out-of-range values still clamp to the nearest endpoint.
- Still to verify: visual clipping at custom stroke widths and sizes, determinate/indeterminate semantics, RTL linear direction, reduced-motion behavior, and host height with large text or narrow parents on a device.
- Rechecked the exact [Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 source archive](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3/1.5.0-rc01/material3-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) against the [official Compose progress guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/progress). Its circular flat/wavy size tokens are 40/48 dp, linear wavy height is 10 dp, and both active and track strokes are 4 dp; the Dart host and Compose bridge already use those defaults. This first pass missed the circular wavy animation-spec distinction, which is corrected in the later motion audit below. The same source confirms each real progress composable adds its own progress semantics after the caller modifier, so the optional caller label still needs the planned device semantics-tree check before changing modifier order.

## Snackbar action color

- Official sources: [Compose snackbar guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/snackbar), the pinned AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 `Snackbar.kt` source, and [Flutter `SnackBarThemeData`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/SnackBarThemeData-class.html).
- The native `Snackbar(snackbarData = …)` overload draws its action `TextButton` using `actionColor`. Its separate `actionContentColor` parameter does not set that button's text color. The bridge previously sent only the content color, so a semantic red/green/orange surface or a themed action could leave the native action with the wrong color.
- The Flutter bridge now sends a distinct `actionColor`: `SnackBarThemeData.actionTextColor` for the neutral snackbar, or the package's semantic contrast color. Compose applies that color to both action parameters and retains `SnackbarDefaults` when the caller supplies no action color. The action-background theme property is not exposed by this official `Snackbar(snackbarData)` overload; no simulated button was added.
- The pinned `Snackbar(snackbarData = …)` source also forwards `dismissActionContentColor` to its close icon. When accessible navigation keeps an actionable snackbar visible, the close icon now uses the same contrast color as its message on semantic backgrounds; neutral snackbars retain their native content color.
- The old white text had calculated contrast ratios of 4.12:1 on green 700 and 2.70:1 on orange 700, below 4.5:1 for normal text. Black yields 5.10:1 and 7.77:1 on those surfaces; red 700 keeps white at 4.98:1. Both the native bridge and the Flutter fallback now use those contrast colors.
- The focused Flutter bridge tests verify the distinct themed action and all three semantic contrast colors. Actual native colors, exit animation, queued presentation, touch and TalkBack still need an Android device.
- [AndroidX's `Snackbar` contract](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/88e1988b6bf1969184b60bb87c92237a13fdf0b5/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Snackbar.kt) says an actionable snackbar should not time out before another user action; indefinite snackbars should offer a close affordance. The native route previously timed out action messages unless Flutter reported accessible navigation, and a later snackbar could wait forever behind an indefinite one. Action messages now use the real `SnackbarHostState` and `Snackbar` with an indefinite timer and native close action. The Dart queue signals the preceding actionable request when a new snackbar is enqueued, even if the predecessor has not yet mounted; Compose dismisses its current data once present and retains its host exit motion. The new Flutter bridge test failed before this queue signal and passed afterward; capped offline Kotlin compilation also passed. Action and close timing, concurrent navigation, and accessibility announcements still need device checks.
- The pinned [Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 `Snackbar.kt` source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3-android/1.5.0-rc01/material3-android-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) applies 12 dp outer padding in `Snackbar(snackbarData)` and caps its inner container at 600 dp. Its `Scaffold.kt` places the `SnackbarHost` directly above the system bottom inset, bottom bar or FAB. The package's custom popup host had added another 16 dp below and reserved 16 dp on each horizontal side, resulting in 28 dp visual margins and a maximum 536 dp inner container. The host now ends at the supplied bottom obstacle and measures up to the full available width or 624 dp, leaving the official Compose padding to create a 12 dp visible gap and up to a 600 dp container. Capped offline Kotlin compilation passed after the competing build ended. Device checks remain for horizontal system insets, landscape, narrow windows and FAB movement.

## Text field placeholder semantics

- Official [Compose text input guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/text/user-input) and [`OutlinedTextField` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedTextField.composable) distinguish a persistent/floating `label` from a `placeholder` shown inside an empty field. The [`OutlinedSecureTextField` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedSecureTextField.composable) offers the same separate slots.
- `AdaptiveTextField.placeholder`, `AdaptiveTextFormField.placeholder`, and `AdaptiveAlertDialogInput.placeholder` promise a hint. Cupertino and Flutter fallback paths already use placeholder/hint slots, but Android had mapped an undecorated text field's hint into the floating label and did the same for native alert input.
- The native Android text/form bridge now sends the public placeholder into Compose's placeholder slot. An explicit `InputDecoration` still owns both fields: `labelText` becomes the native label and `hintText` the native placeholder, matching the existing Flutter decoration precedence. Native alert inputs use Compose's placeholder slot in both regular and secure fields. Runtime appearance, focus, semantics, and password IME behavior still need an Android device.

## Floating toolbar scroll motion

- The pinned AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 `FloatingToolbar.kt` source defines `FloatingToolbarDefaults.ScrollDistanceThreshold` as 40 dp for its expansion behavior. The package's `scrollThreshold` now applies to `collapse`; official `exitAlways` movement instead responds to every consumed delta.
- [Compose's official floating toolbar scroll behavior](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingToolbarDefaults) uses a `FloatingToolbarState`, a default effects snap spec, and a spline fling decay. The package delegates expanded/collapsed visuals to the real Compose toolbar. Its `exitAlways` route now forwards page deltas and an end signal from Flutter to the official behavior, and Flutter clips the AndroidView host using the native visible fraction. The former three fixed-duration Flutter exit animations no longer run on that route. The bridge sends zero residual velocity at scroll end, so a device must still verify spring settling and content-fling parity.
- Toolbar tooltip audit: Flutter previously forwarded only an explicit `IconButton.tooltip`/`FloatingActionButton.tooltip`; nested `Icon.semanticLabel` was lost, leaving native leading/trailing/FAB actions with generic English names. The bridge now uses the explicit tooltip before a nonblank nested icon label. A focused widget test reproduced null descriptions before passing. The real Compose leading/trailing `IconButton`s are now each wrapped in `TooltipBox` across horizontal, vertical and docked layouts. The floating toolbar FAB's tooltip previously wrapped only its inner `Icon`, so pointer hover/long press outside that small area could miss it; the tooltip now wraps the whole real Compose FAB. Native action tooltips use the pane-title/assertive-live-region semantics shown in the official [icon-button](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButton.composable) and [FAB](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButton.composable) examples. Runtime touch, focus, TalkBack and tooltip geometry still need a device check.
- The floating toolbar's adjacent native FAB was always clickable even when it had no callback; Flutter simply ignored the event, leaving a false ripple, focus action and enabled appearance. Flutter now sends `fabEnabled` only when an explicit FAB action or expansion callback exists; Compose uses the same disabled interaction modifier and Material disabled colors as the package's standalone FAB. `nativeFabTooltip` supplies a localized name when callers use `nativeFabIcon` without an `Icon` widget. The pinned [FloatingToolbarDefaults source](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingToolbarDefaults) exposes a `modifier` but no `enabled` parameter for its real standard/vibrant FABs. The focused bridge test first failed on the missing API before all seven toolbar tests passed; device disabled contrast, semantics and ripple remain to inspect.
- The pinned AndroidX 1.5.0-rc01 [`AppBarDsl.kt` source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/%2B/88e1988b6bf1969184b60bb87c92237a13fdf0b5/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AppBarDsl.kt) creates a full-control `TooltipBox` around each `clickableItem`/`toggleableItem` icon button, and its `PlainTooltipInternal` already sets `paneTitle` and an assertive live region. The package had inserted a second `TooltipBox` inside each icon slot. That could produce duplicate tooltip popups and limited the extra anchor to the icon itself. The nested tooltip is removed; `AppBarRow` and `AppBarColumn` own the real tooltip, focus and overflow behavior. Runtime tooltip geometry and TalkBack still need device verification.
- `AdaptiveToolbar` serialized disabled action state correctly, but its Flutter event handler still invoked a disabled action's callback if an old native event arrived after a state change. It now checks `enabled` and callback availability before dispatching both presses and selection changes. A focused synthetic platform-event test reproduced the wrong callback before passing all eight toolbar tests.
- The official [`AppBarScope.toggleableItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AppBarScope) separates `onCheckedChange` from an ordinary click callback. A package action with `activeIndex` and `onActiveIndexChanged` but no `onPressed` was nevertheless serialized as disabled, so the real Compose toggle could never change selection. The bridge now treats the selection callback as a valid action, while an action with neither callback remains disabled. Flutter fallback actions use the same rule and invoke selection before an optional press callback. Two focused tests first reproduced the native disabled state and inert Flutter fallback, then all ten toolbar tests passed. Compose selected-state and overflow behavior still need a device check.
- The pinned 1.5.0-rc01 [`FloatingToolbar.kt` source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3-android/1.5.0-rc01/material3-android-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) exposes a `FloatingToolbarScrollBehavior` with mutable offset/content state and native snap/fling specs. `ExitAlwaysFloatingToolbarScrollBehavior.onPostScroll` adds the content's consumed vertical delta to `FloatingToolbarState.offset`; its modifier places the actual toolbar at that partial pixel offset. The bridge now reverses Flutter's increasing scroll offset into Compose's consumed-y direction, converts logical to physical pixels at `LocalDensity`, and invokes `onPostScroll`/`onPostFling` on the real behavior.
- The pinned [`FlexibleBottomAppBar`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FlexibleBottomAppBar.composable) also exposes `BottomAppBarScrollBehavior`. [`BottomAppBarDefaults.exitAlwaysScrollBehavior`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/BottomAppBarDefaults) supplies the native motion-scheme snap and decay. Its layout reduces the surface height as `BottomAppBarState.heightOffset` changes. The bridge now uses that behavior for docked `exitAlways` and reports its visible fraction to the Flutter clip. This corrects the earlier research claim that docked toolbars lacked a native behavior.
- Host geometry: the official floating modifier translates relative to a stable Compose parent, while the docked component measures a smaller native surface. Flutter's `Align(heightFactor)` (or `widthFactor` for vertical floating toolbars) sizes only the surrounding slot while its child AndroidView retains the full preferred size. Widget tests cover half and zero docked height, vertical width, and accessible-navigation reset. The Compose parent therefore keeps a stable offset limit while `Scaffold` regains the hidden bar's space. Native drag/fling visuals, TalkBack, RTL placement, safe-area behavior and system animation-scale response remain device checks.

## Segmented button intrinsic width

- The pinned AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 `SegmentedButton.kt` source gives `SingleChoiceSegmentedButtonRow` its intrinsic minimum width and distributes each real `SegmentedButton` equally. The Flutter bridge previously ignored `AdaptiveSegmentedControl.shrinkWrap` on Android and forced the Compose row to fill its host.
- With `shrinkWrap: true`, Compose now measures its own row width and reports it to the existing platform view. Flutter centers the measured host within a bounded parent and caps it at the parent's width. A horizontal scroller starts with a finite provisional width and accepts Compose's later intrinsic measurement. Changing labels, icons, icon size or typography clears the prior measured width without remounting the Android view.
- The default `shrinkWrap: false` still fills the available width. Native first-frame width, text scaling, RTL and clipping with long labels remain device checks.

## Popup menu disabled actions

- The official [Compose Material 3 `DropdownMenuItem` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/DropdownMenuItem.composable) says `enabled: false` blocks input and marks the item disabled for accessibility. The package already passes that state into its real native menu items. A late platform event can still cross the Flutter boundary after a menu item changes state, so the inline icon/text and anchored menu handlers now recheck the current entry's `enabled` flag before calling the app. The Flutter popup fallback and post-dismiss Cupertino callback apply the same guard. Visual disabled state and TalkBack behavior still need device checks.
- The inline native icon/text menu previously sent its selection to Flutter immediately after setting `expanded = false`. A callback that navigates can dispose the host before Compose finishes the menu exit. The menu now retains the selected index until its `DropdownMenu` leaves composition, then sends the event through the latest callback, matching the package's existing split-button route. It also closes when the trigger becomes disabled. Native exit timing still needs Android runtime verification.
- Popup avatar decoding audit: the inline and anchored menus formerly decoded image bytes on every recomposition. The current `nativeM3EPopupLeadingIcon` uses the shared `rememberM3EImageBitmapResult` helper: Android samples images to a 32 dp target on `Dispatchers.IO`, limits concurrent decodes to two, and preserves a 32 dp icon slot while loading. Compose composition no longer performs that decode; first-open image latency and failure fallback still need device inspection. [Compose performance guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/performance/bestpractices) recommends keeping expensive work out of composition.
- The inline menu retains a selection through Compose's exit transition, while the anchored menu returns after its overlay route closes. Either delay can outlive a Flutter item reorder. The Android bridge now sends the selected item identity with its former index, and Dart resolves that identity against the current list before invoking the callback. Removed or newly disabled items are ignored; older index-only native events remain supported. This follows the real `DropdownMenuItem` lifecycle without adding a second animation. Widget tests reproduce the reorder for icon, text and anchored menus, plus removal for both inline triggers. Native exit timing still needs a device check.

## Native tooltip typography

- The official [`PlainTooltip` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/PlainTooltip.composable) accepts composable content. The Android bridge previously forwarded only `TextStyle.color` and `fontSize`, silently losing explicit weight, italic style, letter spacing and line height. Its content now uses the existing native `NativeM3EListText` serializer inside the real `PlainTooltip`, retaining Compose's default tooltip text style for unspecified properties. Explicit font families and other Flutter text effects that serializer cannot represent use Flutter's `Tooltip` renderer rather than dropping their styling. Two focused tests failed on the old bridge and passed after the change; native visual typography still needs device inspection.
- The same official `PlainTooltip` exposes a `shape` parameter. A caller's `BoxDecoration.borderRadius` was previously ignored by the Android route, while gradients and other decoration effects were also silently dropped. Four finite circular corners now reach a real Compose `AbsoluteRoundedCornerShape`; color-only decoration stays native, and unsupported borders, shadows, gradients, images, blend modes and elliptical corners retain Flutter's renderer. Two focused tests failed on the prior bridge and passed afterward. Android popup outline and RTL placement still need device inspection.

## Cross-component native item identity

- The [Compose lifecycle guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/lifecycle) requires stable, unique keys among dynamic siblings that retain interaction state. Flutter object identity alone is insufficient when one canonical item instance appears more than once in a list. `NativeOccurrenceIds` now allocates a stable ID for each occurrence ordinal of the same object using a weak object key; button groups, carousels and bottom navigation use it for their native event bridge. Each has a red/green duplicate-instance widget test. This does not add a second rendering or animation path.
- Split-button and FAB menus had the same duplicate-instance collision: the second occurrence reused the first occurrence's native ID. Both now serialize per-occurrence IDs and resolve delayed selection against the current list; removed second occurrences are ignored. The two duplicate tests failed before this change, and all 17 split/FAB menu tests pass afterward. The native Compose controls and their exit transitions are unchanged; runtime menu timing still needs Android device inspection.
- Native context and popup menus also had per-object ID collisions. In an icon or text popup, removing the second occurrence while its delayed selection was pending would fall back to the first occurrence; the context menu had the same path after its overlay closed. Their Android and Flutter-menu bridges now use per-occurrence IDs, so a removed second occurrence has no match. Three duplicate-instance tests failed before the fix; all 24 focused context/popup tests pass afterward. The anchored popup uses the same serialization and resolution helper, but its duplicate-instance close timing still needs device verification.
- App-bar actions were the final per-object native action ID bridge in this audit. A repeated action instance appeared twice in the native action array with one ID; after removing the second occurrence, its delayed event could activate the first. The scaffold now serializes per-occurrence IDs and resolves the current action by that ID. The duplicate test failed before the change; all 25 app-bar action tests pass afterward. Repeated indistinguishable objects can only be identified by occurrence order unless callers supply a distinct application key.
- Toolbar actions had a separate index-only event bridge. The official pinned [`AppBarScope`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AppBarScope) collects clickable/toggleable items and supplies the real Material overflow, but its action callbacks can arrive after Flutter has reordered the list. The toolbar now serializes per-occurrence IDs and Compose sends the selected ID with its former index for both click and checked-state changes; Flutter resolves the current enabled action and current selected index, ignoring removed actions. A reorder test failed before the bridge change; 19 focused toolbar tests passed after it. A repeated-instance test also confirms distinct IDs and ignores a removed second occurrence. Capped offline one-worker Kotlin compilation passed with no active competing compiler and 38% free system memory at launch. Actual focus state after reorder and overflow dismissal still require device checks; the pinned `AppBarScope` DSL does not expose an item key.

## Native text-field icon colors

- The official [Compose `OutlinedTextField` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedTextField.composable) exposes leading and trailing icon slots. Compose [`Icon`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Icon.composable) accepts a size modifier, tint and content description. The bridge now preserves an explicit Flutter `Icon` size, color and semantic label in both regular and secure fields.
- The pinned Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 `OutlinedTextFieldDefaults.colors` exposes focused, unfocused, disabled and error icon roles separately for leading and trailing icons. Flutter [`InputDecoration.prefixIconColor`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/InputDecoration/prefixIconColor.html) may resolve a `WidgetStateColor` from field state. The bridge serializes each representable role and lets `OutlinedTextFieldDefaults` supply the real Compose state transition; an explicit child `Icon.color` takes precedence. Hover-specific or incompatible combined-state color rules use Flutter rendering so their behavior is not silently lost.
- Native icon geometry, focus/error tint changes and TalkBack descriptions still need Android device inspection.

## Native bottom-navigation icon fidelity

- The official [`NavigationBarItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/NavigationBarItem.composable) and [`ShortNavigationBarItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ShortNavigationBarItem.composable) both take a composable icon slot. The package already used those real controls, but its Flutter bridge sent only a glyph identifier for `AdaptiveNavigationDestination.icon` and `selectedIcon`. Explicit Flutter `Icon.size` and `Icon.color` disappeared in both tall and short bars.
- The bridge now sends separate normal/selected icon styles to the native `Icon` in each bar. An explicit tint overrides the item's native color role, as it does in Flutter; missing values retain Material's size and `LocalContentColor`. When labels are hidden, an explicit `Icon.semanticLabel` supplies the native description before the destination label. A visible destination label remains the accessibility name, avoiding a duplicate icon announcement. Unsupported Flutter icon effects now produce a field-specific error directing callers to a custom bar instead of being silently discarded.
- On-device icon geometry, badge overlap at large sizes, selected-state colors and TalkBack output still need verification.

## Native top-app-bar icon fidelity

- The official [`TopAppBar`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TopAppBar.composable) exposes navigation and action composable slots. The native bridge already places real Material 3 [`IconButton`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButton.composable) controls there, but an explicit `Icon.size` or `Icon.color` on `AdaptiveAppBar.leading` or an action's `iconWidget` was dropped when only the glyph crossed to Compose.
- The bridge now forwards the icon's size and tint into Compose [`Icon`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Icon.composable) in the leading button, visible action buttons and overflow menu icons. The action's explicit semantic label still wins; otherwise the child icon's semantic label supplies the tooltip and accessible action name. Unsupported Flutter icon effects keep the existing Flutter `AppBar` route instead of losing their paint options in Compose. Default sizes and colors continue to come from the native button content when callers omit overrides.
- Native geometry, overflow icon placement, tooltip content and TalkBack output remain device checks.

## Native switch-row leading icon fidelity

- The official [`ListItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ListItem.composable) exposes a leading composable slot beside the real [`Switch`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Switch.composable). `AdaptiveSwitchListTile` already uses those real controls, but it serialized only the leading icon glyph. A caller's explicit `Icon.size`, `Icon.color` and `Icon.semanticLabel` were silently lost.
- The bridge now passes those options to Compose's `Icon` in the leading slot. Unspecified values keep Material's native defaults. Flutter-only icon effects keep the package's existing Material `ListTile` fallback, preserving the caller's widget instead of dropping its drawing options. The measured native row height remains controlled by Compose.
- Large-icon row geometry, switch focus order and TalkBack output still require Android device inspection.

## Search and switch disabled event bridge

- The official Compose [`SearchBarDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SearchBarDefaults) defines the input-field height used by the package's 56 dp native search host. The [Compose search bar guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/search-bar) does not prescribe extra outside spacing for every app, so this audit did not add fixed margins around the field.
- The official [`Switch`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Switch.composable) blocks input when disabled. The native search input likewise receives `enabled`, but Flutter can still receive a delayed platform-channel action after a rebuild disables the widget. The search and switch-row Dart bridges now ignore those stale tap, submit, clear or change actions when disabled, including a late search text edit that would otherwise change the Flutter controller.
- The Flutter Material search fallback now forwards the public `onTap` callback and disables its clear button when the field is disabled. Native search focus, IME timing and disabled visual treatment still need device inspection.
- The native clear affordance exposed a separate Compose `IconButton` inside the search field. Disabling [`SearchBarDefaults.InputField`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SearchBarDefaults) alone did not disable that button. It now receives the same `enabled` value; the official [`IconButton` contract](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButton.composable) removes click response and marks the control disabled for accessibility services. The existing Flutter late-event guard remains for callbacks already in flight.

## Embedded icon-button shape motion

- The official [`IconButtonDefaults.shapes()`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButtonDefaults) returns the Material 3 Expressive normal and pressed shapes. Its [`IconButtonShapes`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButtonShapes) overload lets Compose morph between them during interaction. A source scan found two remaining embedded native icon buttons using the static-shape overload: the standalone search clear affordance and the floating toolbar's leading/trailing edge actions. Both now pass `IconButtonDefaults.shapes()` to their existing real Compose `IconButton`; their existing enabled state and callbacks remain intact. Device press motion and touch geometry still need inspection.

## Embedded text-button shape motion

- The pinned [Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3-android/1.5.0-rc01/material3-android-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) provides two Compose `TextButton` overloads. The older `shape` overload retains a fixed container and `TextButtonContentPadding`; the expressive `shapes` overload uses `ButtonShapes` and `contentPaddingFor(ButtonDefaults.MinHeight)`. The official [`ButtonDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ButtonDefaults) supplies the small button's normal and pressed shapes through `shapes()`, including the same round shape as the older text button at rest.
- Seven embedded text buttons still used the fixed-shape overload: a text-only top-app-bar action, alert confirm/dismiss actions, and date/time confirm/dismiss actions. They now use `ButtonDefaults.shapes()` in the existing real Compose buttons, preserving their callbacks, colors and enabled states while adopting the native pressed morph and expressive content spacing. Press animation and dialog layout remain device checks.

## Card and slider interaction follow-up

- The official mobile Compose [`Card`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Card.composable) and [`CardDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/CardDefaults) expose a shape and interaction-driven elevation, but no `CardShapes` pressed-morph overload in this dependency. The package's interactive card surface and carousel items already use the real clickable `Card` overload and its `MutableInteractionSource`; adding a custom card-shape animation here would no longer be a native Material component behavior. No card rendering change followed this API audit.
- The official state-based [`Slider`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Slider.composable) documents separate `onValueChange` and `onValueChangeFinished` callbacks and an `enabled` state. The native range slider already clears its local change-start bookkeeping when disabled, but the single slider did not. Disabling it during a drag could leave the start flag set if no finish callback arrives, suppressing the next `onChangeStart`. The single slider now clears that flag on disable while keeping the real Compose `Slider`, thumb, label and native interactions. This timing remains a device check.
- The official [`RangeSliderState`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/RangeSliderState) stores `Float` start/end values. `AdaptiveRangeSlider` already normalized valid Dart `double` ranges and asserted their bounds, but Dart omits constructor assertions in production. The Android build path now explicitly rejects nonfinite or unordered bounds/values and nonpositive divisions before creating a native view. Valid ranges, including a single-point disabled track, retain their existing normalized payload. Debug tests cover those valid paths; this extra release guard is verified by source review rather than a release-mode widget test.
- The same release-assertion gap remained in `AdaptiveSlider`: its native bridge guarded nonfinite values by disabling the track, but silently clamped an out-of-range value or treated nonpositive divisions as continuous. Android now rejects invalid bounds, values and divisions before mounting the real Compose slider. A valid `min == max` still renders disabled at zero; full finite double spans keep the existing overflow-safe normalization. Debug tests exercise the valid native payload and constructor assertions, while the additional release guard is source-reviewed.

## Disabled native list-item events

- The official Compose [`ListItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ListItem.composable) says `enabled = false` removes response to user input and marks the item disabled for accessibility. The package already forwards `enabled` to the real `ListItem` and `SegmentedListItem`, but a tap or long-press event queued before a Flutter rebuild could still invoke the Dart callback afterward. The Dart bridge now checks its current `enabled` value before dispatching either action. Native gesture and accessibility behavior still need Android device inspection.

## FAB-menu action icon fidelity

- The official [`FloatingActionButtonMenuItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButtonMenuItem.composable) accepts a composable icon slot. The package already uses the real Material 3 item, but its Flutter bridge serialized only the icon name, discarding an explicit `Icon.size` or `Icon.color`. It now forwards those optional values into the native `Icon` slot. An omitted override still uses the Material item size and `LocalContentColor`; the visible text remains the action's accessible name, so its decorative icon keeps a null content description. Android rendering and touch bounds with an oversized icon still need device inspection.
- The official [`ToggleFloatingActionButtonDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ToggleFloatingActionButtonDefaults) provides `iconColor(initialColor, finalColor)` and `iconSize(initialSize, finalSize)` interpolation functions for `Modifier.animateIcon`. The menu toggle previously serialized only the add and close glyph names, dropping explicit Flutter `Icon.size` and `Icon.color`. The bridge now supplies those optional opening/closing endpoints to the native interpolation functions. Missing endpoints retain the matching Compose size token or theme color, and the real `ToggleFloatingActionButton` still drives the transition through its checked progress. Device rendering and animation timing remain to verify.

## Circular progress size validation

- The official [Compose progress guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/progress) demonstrates that the real circular indicator accepts a `Modifier` for explicit sizing. The package sizes that native indicator through a Flutter host: `androidSize` has priority, while a nondefault legacy `radius` sets the diameter when `androidSize` is absent. An infinite or negative radius previously became a plausible 24 or 240 dp host through clamping, hiding invalid caller input. The Android route now requires the effective legacy radius to be finite and positive; the later circular-size audit removed that silent clamp for all valid sizes. This changes no Compose indicator drawing or motion.

## Native motion follow-up and shape input

- The official [`MotionScheme`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/MotionScheme) assigns spatial springs to shape/bounds/position changes and effects springs to color/opacity changes. The native expansion and split-button arrows use `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec()`. The app bar uses `TopAppBarDefaults.snapAnimationSpec`; [AndroidX explicitly assigns its search-bar snap](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SearchBarDefaults) the default effects token, which the Flutter scroll bridge also uses. Flat and wavy progress indicators use their respective `ProgressAnimationSpec` defaults. The determinate `LoadingIndicator` has no built-in progress animation: the package now applies Compose's official `ProgressIndicatorDefaults.ProgressAnimationSpec`, matching the [AndroidX determinate loading-indicator sample](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/a955844256f38a45bb01973b36dec1e6e2342b2e/compose/material3/material3/samples/src/main/java/androidx/compose/material3/samples/LoadingIndicatorSamples.kt). The only native `tween` is the carousel's caller-specified positive tap duration; an omitted duration keeps `CarouselState.animateScrollToItem`'s own animation. Fixed-duration Flutter animations in the same package belong to iOS/fallback routes or the toolbar host's inert visibility wrapper; the native toolbar `exitAlways` and `collapse` behaviors remain Compose-owned. Runtime frame pacing and system reduced-motion behavior still need device verification.
- The official [Compose shape guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/graphics/draw/shapes) defines `Morph` progress from 0 to 1. `AdaptiveExpressiveShapeView` already draws the real Compose `MaterialShapes`/`Morph`, but accepted nonfinite or negative host dimensions and relied on a Dart constructor assertion for progress validity. The build path now rejects invalid dimensions and invalid progress in production before creating the native view. Zero dimensions remain valid for a hidden decorative shape. Caller-controlled timing and the native interpolated path are unchanged.

## Styled icons in native buttons

- The official [`Button` composable](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Button.composable) accepts icon content, and [`ButtonDefaults.iconSizeFor`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ButtonDefaults) supplies a default size for the chosen button height. The package's `AdaptiveButton.child` had sent a simple icon or icon-plus-label row to Compose only when the Flutter `Icon` had no explicit size or color; a styled icon therefore switched the whole button to Flutter. The bridge now serializes finite nonnegative `Icon.size` and `Icon.color` into the real Compose `Icon` inside the real Material 3 button. An unspecified size or color still uses the Compose token and `LocalContentColor`. The Flutter host reserves the explicit icon's height and width, including sizes above the default button tier. Flutter's [`WidgetStateColor`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/WidgetStateColor-class.html) uses its default value when supplied to an `Icon.color` property as a plain `Color`; the native bridge sends that same value. Unsupported Flutter paint effects retain the Flutter route. On-device color, clipping and touch-target appearance remain to verify.
- The host measures button text with a one-line `TextPainter`, but the native `Text` previously had Compose's unbounded `maxLines`; a narrow parent could force multiple native lines into a single-line host. Following [Compose's text-layout API](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/text/configure-layout), the real native `Text` now uses `maxLines = 1` with `TextOverflow.Ellipsis`. The original label string remains in the composable and its bridge payload. Narrow-width appearance and TalkBack reading of the complete label still need device checks.

## Floating action button host geometry

- The official [`SmallFloatingActionButton`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SmallFloatingActionButton.composable) delegates to the real Compose FAB with a minimum container size; [AndroidX source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/dd7d2935e6a5ab12f6957a813926eea1ca38188e/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButton.kt) shows that its centered content can enlarge the minimum-sized surface. The Flutter host previously stayed at 40 or 56 dp even when a serializable `Icon.size` was larger. It now reserves at least the explicit icon size while keeping the same native small/default FAB, shape, colors, elevation and tooltip. An 80 dp small-FAB test failed at a 40 dp host before the change and passes at 80 dp afterward. Oversized icon appearance and interactive bounds still need device inspection.
- The official [`IconButtonDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButtonDefaults) supplies size and width tokens for the real Compose icon buttons. This package fixes the native button modifier to those token dimensions, so an explicit `Icon.size` above the token was clipped even though its bridge value reached Compose; the Flutter platform view also stayed at the token/48 dp touch size. Both the Compose modifier and Flutter host now use at least the explicit icon size on each axis while retaining the native token minimum, selected/pressed shape morph, colors and badge. The ordinary token geometry is unchanged. An 80 dp icon test failed at a 48 dp host before the change and passes at 80 dp; native clip, shape and touch feedback still need a device check.

## Generic fonts in native text slots

- [Compose's font guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/text/fonts) documents four built-in generic families: sans serif, serif, monospace and cursive. The package already maps those names into the native theme but its per-widget text guard rejected every explicit `TextStyle.fontFamily`. Plain text in a list, toolbar, app bar, sheet or tooltip therefore left the native component even for a built-in generic family. The bridge now accepts only those four names, carries `fontFamily` in its text options, and selects the corresponding real Compose `FontFamily` in `NativeM3EListText`; native text fields receive the same per-widget option. Arbitrary family names and font fallback lists retain Flutter rendering until their asset and glyph behavior can be verified. List and field tests failed before the change and pass afterward. Android font appearance and glyph fallback remain device checks.

## Progress and refresh follow-up

- The [official wavy defaults](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/WavyProgressIndicatorDefaults) expose separate active and track strokes. The AndroidX [linear](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/268d841a45644cadf438fc335c793869728449ec/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/tokens/LinearProgressIndicatorTokens.kt) and [circular](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/268d841a45644cadf438fc335c793869728449ec/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/tokens/CircularProgressIndicatorTokens.kt) tokens assign both strokes 4 dp with round caps, matching the package's current default when it forwards its public thickness. The native wavy composables still own wavelength, amplitude, gap, stop and wave motion. No stroke replacement was warranted by this source check.
- The Flutter refresh bridge retains its native indicator host until the real Compose `PullToRefreshDefaults.LoadingIndicator` reports `hidden`. A focused test now covers completed Flutter refresh, extra simulated time and the final native event. The host remained mounted and carried `exitRequested` before that event, so the suspected early disposal did not reproduce in the bridge test. Android animation timing remains a device check.
- The official [loading indicator API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LoadingIndicatorDefaults) exposes a 48 dp container and 38 dp active shape through its [generated AndroidX tokens](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/268d841a45644cadf438fc335c793869728449ec/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/tokens/LoadingIndicatorTokens.kt). The package's default 48 dp Flutter host and native `Modifier.size(48.dp)` match the real container for both regular and contained indicators; Compose still controls the active shape and morph. No host resize is needed for the default case. Explicit caller sizes remain a device clipping check.

## Physical list padding in RTL

- Flutter's [`EdgeInsetsGeometry.resolve`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/painting/EdgeInsetsGeometry/resolve.html) produces physical left/right values. The native list-item and expansion-header bridges forwarded those values in `[left, top, right, bottom]` order, but Compose interpreted them with direction-aware `PaddingValues(start, top, end, bottom)`. An asymmetric caller inset therefore moved to the opposite physical edge in RTL. Both native `ListItem` routes now use Compose's official [`PaddingValues.Absolute`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/layout/PaddingValues.Absolute), preserving the already resolved left/right values without replacing the real expressive list components. The button bridge was checked separately: it already converts its physical values back to logical start/end before calling direction-aware `PaddingValues`, so it needed no change. RTL bridge cases for physical and directional Flutter padding pass; the capped offline Kotlin compile also passes. Actual Android placement still needs a device check.
- The same physical/logical error affected the native `PlainTooltip` margin and message padding, plus the outer padding of `OutlinedTextField`. Those Flutter bridges also send resolved physical edges. Compose now applies its official [`Modifier.absolutePadding`](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/dcaa116fbfda77e64a319e1668056ce3b032469f/compose/foundation/foundation-layout/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/layout/Padding.kt), leaving the real M3 controls and their animation/semantics intact. The editable text itself also conflated Flutter's physical `TextAlign.left/right` with Compose's logical `Start/End`; it now maps all four directions separately, as defined by Flutter's [`TextAlign`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/dart-ui/TextAlign.html) and [Compose paragraph alignment](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/text/style-paragraph). RTL bridge tests confirm the serialized edges and alignment. Android visual placement remains a device check.
- A wider RTL audit found that package-owned `ComposeView`s did not inherit a locally overridden Flutter `Directionality`; Compose's default followed its Android host instead. The shared platform-view bridge now sends Flutter's `ltr`/`rtl` on creation and on changes, and the native expressive theme supplies it through the official [`LocalLayoutDirection`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/platform/package-summary) to every real Material 3 component under that view. This is required for logical start/end geometry, icon mirroring and text layout when an app uses a local direction override. A bridge test failed on the missing property before the change, then passed for creation and LTR→RTL updates without view recreation. Capped one-worker Kotlin compilation passed; device rendering and overlay/drawer traversal still need confirmation.
- The [Compose navigation drawer guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/drawer) places a `ModalDrawerSheet` in `ModalNavigationDrawer`'s drawer slot. The package's overlay uses the same controls: for `endDrawer` it reverses the layout direction only around the drawer container, then restores the caller's direction inside the sheet. Both modal and nonmodal Flutter overlay helpers capture the caller's `Directionality` and reinstall it around the one-pixel native host. Thus the new global direction bridge reaches these overlays without making destination labels read backwards. Runtime edge placement and focus traversal in RTL remain device checks.

## Loading indicator size and native morph

- The current [AndroidX `LoadingIndicator` source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/e75468509878289ddf57f05fa600f9887d0fd226/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LoadingIndicator.kt) applies the caller's modifier before its default 48 dp container and draws the morph against the measured canvas. Its scale calculation accounts for rotated polygon bounds and the 38/48 active-to-container ratio. The package's `AdaptiveLoadingIndicator.size` gives Flutter and the real Compose indicator the same square constraints; no separate bitmap scaling or imitation path is involved. The official [API contract](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LoadingIndicator.composable) requires at least two polygons, matching the Flutter validation. The attached Android device remains necessary to inspect small or oversized custom containers, semantic range announcements, and the continuous native morph.

## FAB menu direction and attachment

- The official [`FloatingActionButtonMenu` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingActionButtonMenu.composable) defaults to logical `Alignment.End` and demonstrates a `ToggleFloatingActionButton` anchored at `BottomEnd`. The package's `AdaptiveFabMenuPosition.right` follows that logical end: its Flutter overlay chooses the physical anchor using the caller's `Directionality`, and Compose positions the native menu with `Alignment.End`; `left` follows logical start on both sides. The shared direction bridge preserves this match when an app overrides direction locally. The real `FloatingActionButtonMenu` owns staggered item motion. No placement correction was indicated by source review; visual anchor alignment and touch bounds in RTL still need a device check.

## Native text-field state colors

- The official [state-based `OutlinedTextField` source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/dd7d2935e6a5ab12f6957a813926eea1ca38188e/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedTextField.kt) uses `textStyle.color` first and consults `TextFieldColors.textColor(enabled, isError, focused)` only if that style color is unspecified. The package's native bridge was assigning `MaterialTheme.colorScheme.onSurface` when the caller had no explicit text color, overriding Compose's disabled, error and focused text colors. It now supplies `Color.Unspecified` in that case; an explicit Flutter `style.color` still wins. The real `OutlinedTextField` and `OutlinedSecureTextField` keep their official color state and transitions. The capped offline Kotlin compile passed; actual contrast in disabled and error states still needs device inspection.
- The official [filled `TextField`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TextField.composable) and [filled `SecureTextField`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SecureTextField.composable) are separate Material components with native container, indicator and state colors. `AdaptiveTextField` previously routed every `InputDecoration(filled: true)` through `OutlinedTextField` and only painted its background, so it retained an outline instead of the filled component. It now selects the real filled composable for ordinary and secure input when `filled` is true, while an explicit `OutlineInputBorder` keeps the real outlined route. A caller's explicit `fillColor` is forwarded for focused, unfocused, disabled and error containers; otherwise the filled component uses its official state colors. The 28 focused bridge tests and offline Kotlin compilation pass. Border geometry and all four state appearances still need device inspection.
- Flutter's [`InputDecoration.applyDefaults`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/InputDecoration/applyDefaults.html) replaces only null fields from the nearest [`InputDecorationTheme.of(context)`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/InputDecorationTheme/of.html). The native bridge had read only the widget's `decoration`, so an app-wide or local theme could request filled styling, an outline, a fill color or padding while Compose received none of them. It now resolves the effective decoration before choosing the native field and serializing those supported properties; explicit per-field values still win. A focused ThemeData case failed on `filled: false` before this correction and passes afterward; a separate local `InputDecorationTheme` case confirms that it wins over the app theme. Rich decoration styles and exact themed border geometry still need separate review.

## Control state-color audit

- The pinned [AndroidX `Snackbar` implementation](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/dd7d2935e6a5ab12f6957a813926eea1ca38188e/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Snackbar.kt) gives `actionColor` to the real `TextButton`'s label and uses `actionContentColor` for the action slot; both defaults are the action-label token. The package forwards an explicit action color to both parameters, so it does not paint same-color text over a colored action container. The native `Button`, `Checkbox`, `RadioButton`, `Switch` and `Slider` routes also leave unspecified per-state colors with their respective Compose defaults and pass only requested overrides. This source audit found no second forced-text-color issue analogous to the text field. Actual disabled/error contrast and transition frames remain device checks.

## Outlined text-field corner geometry

- Flutter's [`OutlineInputBorder.borderRadius`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/OutlineInputBorder/borderRadius.html) carries four circular corners. The real Compose [`OutlinedTextField`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedTextField.composable) exposes a `shape` parameter, so the package now serializes those four physical radii and passes an `AbsoluteRoundedCornerShape` to both normal and secure outlined fields. It retains `OutlinedTextFieldDefaults.roundedShape` when the caller provides no outline. If a decoration requests different corner radii across focus/error/disabled borders, the existing Flutter text field is used because this single-shape Compose overload cannot reproduce those state-dependent corners. One focused bridge test failed on absent radii before the change; all 32 text-field tests, full Flutter analysis, and offline one-worker Kotlin compilation pass.
