Custom border side color/width and focus-transition geometry remain further work; real border rendering still needs a device check.

## Outlined text-field border state

- Flutter's [`InputDecoration` border selection](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/InputDecoration/border.html) uses explicit state borders when present and otherwise resolves the base border through its theme. The package now forwards compatible state colors to real Compose [`OutlinedTextFieldDefaults.colors`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedTextFieldDefaults). AndroidX's [1.4 source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/ed866ae1a6d03f77385a880659ad400282834121/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TextFieldDefaults.kt) gives the complete control a 1 dp unfocused and 2 dp focused stroke. Explicit state borders with other widths or mixed outline/underline geometry retain Flutter's field; changing width inside Compose requires a `BasicTextField` with a custom `DecorationBox` container. Compose has one error border color for both focus states, so distinct Flutter error colors also retain Flutter. For a disabled field with an explicit error border, the bridge sends that error color as Compose's disabled border color to preserve Flutter's error precedence. Kotlin compilation and 36 focused text-field tests passed; native border animation and focus/error rendering still require device inspection.

## Native underline text-field variant

- Flutter's [`UnderlineInputBorder`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/UnderlineInputBorder-class.html) draws an indicator along the bottom, including when [`filled`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/InputDecorationThemeData/filled.html) is false. The bridge previously sent an explicit unfilled underline to Compose's outlined branch. It now selects the real Compose [`TextField`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TextField.composable) or `SecureTextField` with `Color.Transparent` container when unfilled. Compatible explicit indicator colors use [`TextFieldDefaults.colors`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TextFieldDefaults); custom filled-container corner radii use the composable's `shape`. State borders with non-token widths, mismatched error colors or differing radii retain Flutter rendering. The four new tests failed on the old bridge and the 40 focused text-field tests, full Flutter analysis and capped offline Kotlin compilation pass. Native indicator motion and filled-surface appearance remain device checks.

## Borderless fields and inner padding

- Flutter's [`InputBorder.none`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/InputBorder/none-constant.html) requests no drawn border. The native bridge now selects Compose's real `TextField`/`SecureTextField` with transparent indicator colors and a transparent container when unfilled; filled borderless fields retain their requested container color. Mixed state-border geometry stays with Flutter. Flutter's [`InputDecoration.collapsed`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/InputDecoration/InputDecoration.collapsed.html) also uses `InputBorder.none` but removes decoration padding and sizes to the input; that compact variant stays with Flutter because Compose's complete text-field control retains its own minimum layout.
- Flutter's [`InputDecoration.contentPadding`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/InputDecoration/contentPadding.html) describes padding **inside** the decoration container. The bridge formerly attached the serialized values to the Compose control's outer `Modifier.absolutePadding`, shifting the entire border. It now supplies physical Flutter insets as logical start/end values to the `contentPadding` parameter on the four official Compose [filled](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TextField.composable) and [outlined](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/OutlinedTextField.composable) variants, preserving physical left/right margins in RTL. When no inset is supplied, the appropriate `TextFieldDefaults` or `OutlinedTextFieldDefaults` label-aware token stays in use. Native visual placement still needs device inspection.
- `AdaptiveTextFormField` previously bypassed the border/style routing and `InputDecorationTheme` defaults used by `AdaptiveTextField`. Its form state now delegates editing to `AdaptiveTextField`, so validation, controller reset, autofocus, autofill hints and outside-tap callbacks keep their form behavior while the same native/fallback decision handles outlines, underlines, and borderless variants. A themed-fill test and three form-border tests failed before the changes. The Android device was showing another project's `CubaFit.debug` activity, so no example app was installed over that shared session.

## Expansion state accessibility

- The official [Compose semantics guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/semantics) provides `stateDescription` for custom controls. Flutter's [ExpansionTile](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ExpansionTile-class.html) announces the current state using localized `MaterialLocalizations.collapsedHint` (the value “Expanded”) while expanded and `expandedHint` (the value “Collapsed”) while collapsed. The native `AdaptiveExpansionTile` header already uses a real clickable Compose `ListItem`, but it omitted that state announcement. Its bridge now sends the localized description, and the native header applies it with `Modifier.semantics`. The existing Compose click target and ripple remain. The accessibility tree and TalkBack announcement still require an Android device check.
- Flutter's [ScrollEndNotification.dragDetails](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/ScrollEndNotification/dragDetails.html) can be null when a ballistic fling finishes. The current Flutter-to-Compose top-app-bar bridge forwards offset, scrolling state and end serial, but does not carry the original fling velocity. Compose's [TopAppBarScrollBehavior](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TopAppBarScrollBehavior) exposes fling behavior; obtaining the original velocity for arbitrary Flutter scrollables needs a dedicated integration path. Inferring it from a later end notification would be inaccurate, so this remains an integration gap rather than a synthetic approximation.

## Search app-bar action fidelity

- The official [M3 app-bar overview](https://m3.material.io/components/app-bars/) allows actions inside and outside the search field, and Compose's [SearchBarDefaults.InputField](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SearchBarDefaults) provides a composable trailing-icon slot. The package already serialized each action's optional icon size, color, filled/tonal style and width, but the inside-field branch built a separate plain `IconButton` and dropped those options. It now reuses the same real Compose app-bar action button as the outside actions, preserving its tooltip, enabled state, pressed shape, style, width, icon tint and size. Actual fit in a narrow search field still needs a device check.

## Search field semantics and API lifecycle

- The official [Compose semantics guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/semantics) states that `Text` already supplies its text as semantic content. The search app bar's placeholder had added an identical `contentDescription` to its `Text`, risking duplicate spoken information. It now lets the real `SearchBarDefaults.InputField` merge the plain placeholder text, matching the [Compose search-bar example](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/search-bar). The standalone search field already used plain `Text` and was left as-is. TalkBack output still needs a device check.
- The [current SearchBar API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SearchBar.composable) separates collapsed `SearchBar` from an expanded full-screen or docked result view and uses `SearchBarState`; the standalone package widget has no expanded result-view contract. Its older, still real Compose overload pins `expanded = false`. Replacing that overload with the state-based one without defining the result-view behavior would change taps, focus and search interactions, so migration needs a separate host contract and device test.
- The official [TooltipBox example](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TooltipBox.composable) currently adds `liveRegion = Assertive` and `paneTitle` to `PlainTooltip` as a workaround for an Android accessibility announcement bug. That explains the same pattern in this package's native tooltips; removing it based only on general live-region guidance would risk losing tooltip announcements. Revisit when the upstream issue is fixed and TalkBack can be checked.

## Floating-toolbar focus and geometry

- The official [VerticalFloatingToolbar example](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/VerticalFloatingToolbar.composable) explicitly prevents hidden action buttons from taking keyboard focus while its FAB is the visible collapsed control. Compose's [focus guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/touch-input/focus/change-focus-behavior) confirms that a parent's `focusProperties` applies to its descendants. The package's floating-toolbar content now blocks keyboard focus when an attached FAB is collapsed, while keeping the FAB itself outside that focus block. The block is lifted during touch exploration because the official toolbar remains expanded for TalkBack. Runtime keyboard and TalkBack traversal still need device verification.
- A floating toolbar previously reserved the entire available Flutter width, while the vertical variant also reserved a fixed 288 logical-pixel visible height and forced its `AppBarColumn` to fill that height even for one action. The official [AppBarColumn](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AppBarColumn.composable) determines overflow from available height, while [FloatingToolbarDefaults.ContentPadding](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/FloatingToolbarDefaults) specifies padding around 48 dp touch targets. The bridge now reports the real Compose floating toolbar width and height and clips the Flutter visible/hit region to those measurements. The embedded platform view keeps the parent's bounded measuring width and height so content can grow again and native overflow decides what fits; an unbounded vertical parent receives a finite 64 dp horizontal or 288 dp vertical fallback. The same geometry wrapper works with `exitAlways` clipping and RTL. Device rendering and hit-testing remain to verify, particularly under tight parent constraints, during spring expansion and with large-font overflow.
- The official [HorizontalFloatingToolbar](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/HorizontalFloatingToolbar.composable) and [VerticalFloatingToolbar](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/VerticalFloatingToolbar.composable) APIs give the non-FAB overload a permanent central `content` slot and `leadingContent`/`trailingContent` slots shown only while expanded. The package formerly put every icon, title and action in central `content`, making `expanded` a no-op without FAB. The bridge now keeps the action group (or first available edge icon/title) central and places secondary content in the native animated slots. Empty optional slots are passed as null. Runtime visual, touch, keyboard and TalkBack behavior still needs device verification.

## Native defaults cross-check

- AndroidX documents [`expressiveLightColorScheme()`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/package-summary#expressiveLightColorScheme()) only for light mode and explicitly recommends `darkColorScheme()` for dark `MaterialExpressiveTheme`. The bridge's dark fallback follows that contract; there is no `expressiveDarkColorScheme()` API to substitute. Dynamic dark wallpaper colors and the package's full host-derived fallback palette still override this base when present.
- The official [determinate `LoadingIndicator` sample](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LoadingIndicator.composable) animates its progress with `Spring.DampingRatioNoBouncy`, `Spring.StiffnessVeryLow`, and a `1/1000f` visibility threshold before passing it to the real morphing indicator. The package uses those exact parameters for both contained and uncontained loading indicators. Replacing that component-specific sample spring with a generic motion-scheme spec would depart from the official example.
- The [AndroidX versions table](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/versions) lists Material 3 `1.5.0-rc01` as the newest Expressive-capable release as of 2026-11-10; stable `1.4.0` removed experimental Expressive APIs, so the package correctly pins the RC. An offline Gradle `dependencyInsight` resolved Compose UI `1.12.0` and its atomic UI group to that same version. AndroidX's latest stable UI `1.12.1` is a patch release, but its [release note](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/compose-ui#1.12.1) lists no component change relevant to this polish; a dependency bump is not required to access the current Expressive API.
- Compose's [carousel defaults](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/CarouselDefaults) use a medium-low-stiffness spring for `singleAdvanceFlingBehavior`, and `HorizontalUncontainedCarousel` defaults to `noSnapFlingBehavior`. The package delegates to those native behaviors unless callers explicitly request free scrolling. Its three dedicated carousel layouts use real Compose carousel components. The multi-aspect extension uses Compose `LazyRow`/`LazyColumn` with AndroidX's parallax modifier because AndroidX exposes no multi-aspect Material carousel composable; its non-Compose-material layout status should remain explicit in package documentation.
- The [Compose `AccessibilityManager`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/platform/AccessibilityManager) timeout API asks for content flags matching the visible UI. An unactionable native snackbar shows text and no icon; its old `containsIcons = true` could request an unnecessarily long accessibility timeout. The bridge now passes `false`. Actionable snackbars show the dismiss icon but use an indefinite duration, so the recommended finite timeout is only applied to text-only snackbars. AndroidX's [snackbar API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SnackbarHostState) recommends a dismiss action for indefinite snackbars, which the bridge already provides.
- Multi-aspect carousel release audit: [AndroidX's 1.5 release notes](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/compose-material3) introduced multi-aspect support in alpha10, then renamed and simplified it to the global `carouselParallaxScrollEffect` modifier in alpha28. The current [Compose API reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/carousel/carouselParallaxScrollEffect.composable) explicitly prescribes `CarouselParallaxScrollEffectState` with `LazyRow` or `LazyColumn` for variable-width items. The package's `NativeM3EMultiAspectCarousel` follows that official pattern, with its own list only to supply item sizes, spacing, callbacks and a vertical option. No dedicated multi-aspect composable exists in the pinned 1.5.0-rc01 API, so replacing this path would remove native behavior rather than improve it. Device scroll, crop and RTL checks remain.
- The official [Compose time-picker state](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TimePickerState) exposes hour and minute but no minute-grid parameter. `AdaptiveTimePicker.minuteInterval` intentionally aligns the initial value and confirmed result outside the real native dialog. Live snapping while editing would interfere with `TimeInput`'s raw minute entry; the documented post-confirm alignment remains the least intrusive behavior until AndroidX offers a constrained minute selector. The native dialog still owns its picker, input, shape, color and mode transitions.
- Accessibility audit for toolbar icon actions: the official [Compose accessibility guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults) requires an icon action's content description to identify the action using a localized string. `AdaptiveToolbar` already forwarded an `IconButton.tooltip` or nested `Icon.semanticLabel`, but Compose substituted the English strings `Navigate`, `More actions`, and `Primary action` when these were absent, even for unrelated icons. The bridge now accepts explicit `nativeLeadingTooltip` and `nativeTrailingTooltip` overrides, alongside the existing `nativeFabTooltip`. Compose uses those labels for the icon and native tooltip and no longer invents an English action name. A later follow-up requires a nonblank caller-provided name for each present leading, trailing or FAB icon, including disabled icons; otherwise construction fails clearly instead of presenting an unnamed Compose button. The earlier public API bridge test and capped offline one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. The new validation passes all 32 focused toolbar tests, full `flutter analyze --no-pub`, formatting and `git diff --check`. TalkBack and tooltip timing still require device verification.

## Split-button large-text first frame

- The native split button's Flutter host already estimates its initial width using `MediaQuery.textScalerOf(context)`, while real Compose `SplitButtonLayout` reports its eventual width and height. It previously ignored the scaled label height and always reserved only the size's minimum touch target. A widget test with 4× text showed the Android view still at 48 dp. The host now reserves at least the measured Flutter label height until Compose reports its own height. The current [AndroidX `SplitButtonDefaults` source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/2c498ed799191b8b273eccae8ed99a5c3fe14ade/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SplitButton.kt) defines leading content padding horizontally; the bridge does not invent extra vertical spacing. All 12 focused split-button tests pass. Flutter-only `MediaQuery` text-scale overrides do not change Android's Compose font scale, so the first-frame estimate can differ from the final native size; device font-scale and popup checks remain pending.

## Segmented-button large-content first frame

- The official [AndroidX segmented-button source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/2c498ed799191b8b273eccae8ed99a5c3fe14ade/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SegmentedButton.kt) gives the real Compose buttons default content padding with 8 dp above and below the content. The Flutter host previously reserved only 40 dp on its first frame, even if scaled labels or explicit icons needed more. It now estimates the tallest single-line label or icon plus those 16 dp, while sending the original 40 dp/user height as Compose's minimum. That separation lets Compose report and own its eventual height; a Flutter-only `MediaQuery` override does not force a larger native container. The first-frame large-text test failed at 40 dp, and a second assertion caught the forced-height regression before this separation. All 23 focused segmented-control tests pass, with targeted Dart analysis and formatting checks. Actual Android font scaling and animated selection still need device inspection.

## Button-group large-text first frame

- The real native group uses AndroidX `ButtonGroup` and size-aware `ToggleButton` variants. The [AndroidX toggle-button source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/2c498ed799191b8b273eccae8ed99a5c3fe14ade/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ToggleButton.kt) provides vertical content padding; Compose remains responsible for the final size and its pressed/selected shape motion. The Flutter host measured label width but reserved fixed button heights. A 4× text test showed the horizontal host remained 48 dp. Width and height are now estimated in the same text-measurement pass, with at least 16 dp vertical allowance. Horizontal groups reserve their tallest button; vertical groups sum each button's estimated height and the existing gap. Compose's measured-size callback still corrects the host when text wraps or Android's font scale differs. All 23 focused button-group tests and targeted Dart analysis pass. First-frame native parity, wrapping and motion remain device checks.

## App-bar action platform content

- The official [Compose app-bar examples](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/app-bars) give action icons localized descriptions. `AdaptiveAppBarAction.iosSymbol` is an iOS-only value, while `androidIcon` is only available to the Compose route. Before this check, an iOS-symbol-only action could reach the native Android map with neither icon nor title and become a generic `Action N` text button. The Android native route now rejects an action without an Android icon or visible title; the Flutter Android fallback independently rejects an action without a Flutter icon or visible title. A paired `iosSymbol`/`androidIcon` action remains valid natively, and existing visible text or Flutter icon actions remain valid. The iOS-only case failed before the guard; the final suite also covers the paired fallback case. The first 31 focused app-bar action tests and 14 related scaffold/top-bar tests passed. The follow-up below covers the icon-label requirement. Android runtime semantics still need TalkBack inspection.

## App-bar icon action semantics

- The official [Compose accessibility guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults) says interactive icons need a localized `contentDescription`; the [Compose app-bar examples](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/app-bars) supply one. The real native top-bar action previously fabricated `Action N` when neither `semanticLabel` nor a visible title was available, then used that placeholder as the icon description and tooltip. The native bridge now requires a nonblank `semanticLabel`, title, or `Icon.semanticLabel` for icon actions, including those that may move into the overflow menu. It uses that supplied name for the real Compose icon and menu item; no placeholder is invented. `TooltipBox` is present only when a name exists. The example's affected actions now supply descriptions, and its iOS-only blur action has a real Android close icon. All Compose button styles, shapes, clicks and ripple behavior remain. The missing-label widget test failed before the guard and now passes; 33 app-bar action tests, eight native top-bar tests, 24 related scaffold/picker tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped offline Kotlin compilation pass. Flutter tests cannot inspect Compose's accessibility tree, so TalkBack and tooltip timing still need a device check. This is a new Android-native validation requirement for existing icon-only callers.

## Toolbar action names and overflow

- Compose's [AppBarScope](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AppBarScope) uses each item's `label` in its overflow menu, and the [Compose accessibility guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults) requires meaningful localized descriptions for icon actions. `AdaptiveToolbarAction.label` was required syntactically but could be blank; native code then fabricated `Action N`. The Android bridge now rejects blank or whitespace-only labels even if a tooltip is supplied, because the label is the visible overflow name. The native `AppBarRow`/`AppBarColumn` path uses the label for overflow, and an optional nonblank tooltip for the icon's description. Malformed raw maps are skipped instead of receiving an invented name. The focused test failed before the guard; all 31 native toolbar tests, targeted Dart analysis, formatting, diff checks and capped offline Kotlin compilation now pass. On-device TalkBack and overflow checks remain pending.

## Button-group icon names

- The [Compose accessibility API guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults) requires a localized description for interactive icons; the real [Compose ButtonGroup](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ButtonGroup.composable) can move actions into a dropdown menu. The bridge previously humanized `AndroidM3EIcon.arrowBack` into English `Arrow back` when an icon-only action lacked a caller name, and native overflow had a final `Action N` fallback. Android now requires a nonblank `semanticLabel`, `tooltip`, or visible `label` for every action; it keeps all three inputs distinct and no longer invents English names. The native dropdown skips malformed raw items instead of displaying a generic label. The new missing-name test failed before the correction, then all 24 button-group tests, targeted Dart analysis, formatting, diff checks and capped offline Kotlin compilation passed. Existing Android icon-only callers that relied on the inferred name must supply a localized one. Actual TalkBack and narrow-screen overflow remain device checks.

## Segmented-button icon names

- The official [Compose segmented-button example](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/segmented-button) names its icon-only option through `contentDescription`, consistent with the [Compose accessibility API guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults). This package previously fell back to the Material icon identifier or English `Segment N` when no visible text or caller name existed. Android now requires a nonblank `semanticLabels` entry for every segment without visible text. Compose keeps its actual `SegmentedButton`, selection state and built-in icon transition; it uses the caller's name for icon-only and numeric fallback content, and treats whitespace-only labels as absent. The example's three icon-only options now provide names. The missing-name test failed before validation; both segmented-control test files pass 24 tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped offline Kotlin compilation pass. Existing Android callers with unlabeled icon-only or numeric segments must provide localized names. Real TalkBack announcements remain a device check.

## Carousel item descriptions

- The official [Compose carousel example](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/carousel) supplies an accessibility description for every image. This package's native card is interactive even without a caller `onTap`, because tapping also animates the item toward the focal position. An empty title, supporting text and `semanticDescription` therefore produced an unnamed clickable card, with image and icon content intentionally marked decorative inside it. Android now requires at least one nonblank visible text field or an explicit localized `semanticDescription` for each `androidItems` entry. This leaves the real Compose carousel, native card interaction, mask and scroll motion intact. The new empty-card test failed before validation and passes with the 14 focused carousel tests and targeted Dart analysis. This change is Dart-only; on-device TalkBack and image masking remain unverified.

## Screenshot app-bar inset audit

- The [official Compose app-bar guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/app-bars) places `TopAppBar` in `Scaffold` and passes `innerPadding` to content. The reported Noticias Cuba screen instead supplies its own `AdaptiveAppBar.appBar` with a `PreferredSize` of 64 dp and a direct import of the package's internal `AndroidM3EPlatformView`. That explicit override bypasses the package's native adapter, which reserves the 64 dp small bar plus the top status inset and moves the Flutter body below the resulting height. A 24 dp inset requires an 88 dp host in this example. The app's adapter was created to keep an action icon-only, but the current package action path already renders an icon when both `icon` and `title` are supplied, using the title for semantics and tooltip. The app can use the public `AdaptiveAppBar` path without its custom adapter. This finding applies to that app-specific override; it does not establish a universal requirement for an additional gap below every M3 app bar. The app repository was inspected read-only because other agents are working in parallel there. A package regression test covers the public icon action and 88 dp geometry, and all seven focused app-bar tests pass. Real Android rendering still needs device verification.

## Input-chip avatar icon

- The [official Compose chip guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/chip) shows an `InputChip` with an icon in its `avatar` slot. The package previously exposed only an image for that slot; its `leadingIcon` is a different Compose parameter with different avatar sizing. The adaptive API now accepts `avatarIcon` and `nativeAvatarIcon`, passes the resolved icon to the real `InputChip.avatar` lambda at `InputChipDefaults.AvatarSize`, and keeps `avatarSemanticLabel` as its optional content description. An image wins over an avatar icon, and either avatar wins over the leading icon. The Flutter `InputChip` fallback uses its own avatar field. Two new tests first failed because the API was missing; a third checks precedence, and all ten input-chip tests pass. Full Flutter analysis reports no issues. Offline Kotlin compilation with one worker and a 768 MiB heap passed after an idle-compiler/40% free-memory preflight. Avatar visual size, semantics and image decoding still need device checks.

## Standalone search contract and clear action

- The current [state-based `SearchBar` reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SearchBar.composable) pairs its collapsed bar with an `ExpandedFullScreenSearchBar` or `ExpandedDockedSearchBar` using one `SearchBarState`. The standalone `AdaptiveSearchBar` only exposes an editable field and callbacks; it has no results view. The [AndroidX source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/a955844256f38a45bb01973b36dec1e6e2342b2e/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SearchBar.kt) shows the new state-based `InputField` automatically expands on input, even if no expanded results surface is mounted. Replacing the current Boolean-expanded Compose overload in isolation would therefore create an invisible expanded state and change focus/IME behavior. The [overview guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/search-bar) still presents the older Boolean API, while the current reference marks it deprecated. This remains a native API migration tied to an explicit expanded-results contract, not an isolated overload rename.
- In the Flutter Material fallback, the clear button used a literal English `Clear` tooltip even though [Flutter's `MaterialLocalizations.clearButtonTooltip`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/MaterialLocalizations/clearButtonTooltip.html) supplies the locale-specific accessible name (`Clear text` in the default English localization). A focused test failed on the literal and passes after using the Material localization. The redundant light/dark conditional around the same `surfaceContainerHigh` role was removed without changing its color. All nine search-bar tests pass; native Compose search behavior is unchanged.

## Vertical divider

- Optional AI icon audit: [Google's icon research](https://design.google/library/ai-sparkle-icon-research-pozos-schmidt) found that a sparkle is a useful AI cue but does not identify the exact action; UI context and a specific action label remain necessary. `AdaptiveAiSparkleIcon` is an opt-in icon decoration, not an official M3 Expressive component. Its Android route keeps the real Compose icon with a Compose-drawn sparkle and a caller-provided contextual label, while the decorative platform view remains transparent to pointer hits. A new native bridge test verifies those properties. The public `size` now rejects non-finite and non-positive values before building an invalid Flutter host or Compose modifier; the invalid-size test failed before the guard and both focused tests pass. Visual alignment and TalkBack output still need device checks.

- Decorative native-view audit after the divider/progress fixes: the explicit no-op event routes for `AdaptiveBadge`, both dividers, progress, loading, and the `cardSurface` background in `AdaptiveFormSection` now all request transparent Flutter hit testing. `AdaptiveExpressiveShapeView` and `AdaptiveAiSparkleIcon` already did so. Interactive cards, controls, navigation and menu routes still retain their own hit targets. This is a source inventory; stacked Android touch delivery still needs device checks.

- The [Flutter platform-view hit-test API](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/rendering/PlatformViewHitTestBehavior.html) says an opaque view blocks targets visually behind it, while a transparent view does not receive touch events. Both native divider hosts inherited `AndroidM3EPlatformView`'s opaque default even though the Compose `HorizontalDivider` and `VerticalDivider` are decorative. Both now request transparent hit testing; their actual visuals remain the official Compose components. Two focused tests failed on the opaque behavior first, then all thirteen divider tests passed. Physical overlay touch behavior still needs device verification.

- The official [`VerticalDivider` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/VerticalDivider.composable) is separate from `HorizontalDivider` and supplies the same `DividerDefaults.Thickness` and `DividerDefaults.color`. The package exposed only a horizontal divider, so a vertical separator could not use the real Compose component through its public API. `AdaptiveVerticalDivider` now shares the existing `divider` platform-view route with an orientation property. Compose renders the actual `VerticalDivider`, fills the host's bounded height, and applies `indent`/`endIndent` above and below the line; omitted thickness/color use the official tokens. The Flutter fallback uses `VerticalDivider`, and iOS/macOS use a centered Cupertino hairline. The native host requires finite, bounded height. Flutter's `RenderAndroidView.computeDryLayout` returns the largest incoming constraint, so the public usage note recommends an explicitly sized `Row` instead of suggesting `IntrinsicHeight` without a platform-view test. The missing-class tests failed first, then all eleven divider tests and capped offline Kotlin compilation passed. Android paint, density and RTL placement remain device checks.

## Centered slider track

- The official [`SliderDefaults.CenteredTrack`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SliderDefaults) is a real Compose track that fills from the center and supports both [`Slider`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Slider.composable) and [`VerticalSlider`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/VerticalSlider.composable). `AdaptiveSlider` and `AdaptiveSlider.vertical` now expose `centeredTrack: true` as an Android option. Compose owns the track geometry, ticks, colors and disabled state. The default still uses each control's documented regular track; iOS and other Flutter fallbacks retain their native/default track. Both caller-unit and precision-safe normalized ranges place the visual center at the midpoint. Runtime track drawing and touch semantics remain device checks.

## Small native icon motion

- The [official Material motion guide](https://m3.material.io/styles/motion/overview/how-it-works) assigns spatial springs to rotation and the fast speed to small controls. The package's expansion-header arrow and split-button chevron are small Compose icons rotated by package code; both now use `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastSpatialSpec()` under the existing `MaterialExpressiveTheme`. Their surrounding real `ListItem` and `SplitButtonDefaults` controls continue to own pressed shapes, colors and interactions. The split-button documentation previously contradicted the implementation by claiming a standard scheme; it now describes the expressive fast spatial spring. The official [Compose split-button sample](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SplitButtonLayout.composable) also rotates its chevron with `animateFloatAsState`, so no separate drawn control was introduced. Runtime spring pacing and system animator-scale behavior remain device checks.

## Styled text in native buttons

- The official [`ButtonDefaults.textStyleFor`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ButtonDefaults) supplies a size-specific label style, and the real Compose button slots accept `Text`. `AdaptiveButton.child` previously sent a plain `Text` or simple icon/text row to Compose but returned to Flutter for any styled `Text`, losing the real button's pressed-shape and motion behavior even when the existing text bridge could serialize the style. The native path now keeps supported size, weight, italic, tracking, line height, generic family and OpenType feature settings; Compose starts with the size token and applies the caller overrides. Flutter estimates first-frame width/height with the same child style. Explicit child text color reaches the native label. Unsupported fonts, effects, semantics overrides and multiline children still retain their Flutter rendering. Runtime font metrics, wrapping, disabled-color contrast and press morph remain device checks.

## Slider value semantics

- The [AndroidX Slider source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/a955844256f38a45bb01973b36dec1e6e2342b2e/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Slider.kt) derives `stateDescription`, progress range and accessibility actions from its `SliderState` and each `RangeSliderState` thumb. The bridge now gives both real Compose states caller-unit `trackRange` values when single-precision `Float` can resolve the useful positions. Thus a typical 20–100 slider exposes 20–100 to native semantics while retaining its original callbacks. The bridge continues to normalize extremely narrow or large-offset Dart `double` ranges to 0–1, because passing those bounds directly would collapse the native track; those exceptional ranges still need a device-level spoken-value check. The shared range adapter rounds native `Float` endpoint events back to exact caller endpoints and discrete events to ticks. Compose retains its own SetProgress action; no replacement semantics node is added. The visual thumb labels alone do not prove TalkBack behavior, so the native semantics tree and spoken values remain device checks.

## Chip API cross-check

- The current [official Compose chip guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/chip) distinguishes assist, filter, input and suggestion chips and documents elevated assist, filter and suggestion variants. The package calls those seven real Compose composables directly. Filter chips pass the controlled `selected` state and use `FilterChipDefaults.shapes()` for the native pressed/selected morph. Input chips pass their controlled state, distinct leading and avatar slots, and a trailing close icon for the whole-chip dismiss action, matching the guide's `InputChip` example. Assist and suggestion chips retain their separate native slots and elevation variants. Their Flutter APIs accept string labels, so the styled-`Text` child fallback found in buttons does not apply here. This source review found no missing chip variant or label-style bridge to implement; device focus, TalkBack, avatar decoding and shape animation remain the checks that can change this conclusion.

## Flutter font-metrics sentinel in Compose text slots

- [Flutter's `TextStyle.height` contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/painting/TextStyle/height.html) defines `kTextHeightNone` (zero) as an instruction to use the font's own line metrics. The shared native text serializer previously sent that zero as a Compose `.em` line height, which has a different meaning. It now sends an explicit font-metrics flag; `NativeM3EListText` clears its inherited Material line-height token for that case while preserving the real Compose `Text`. The [AndroidX Material `Text` source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/f21d9872130100b23bd87c82f20bb94e1448bb63/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Text.kt) confirms that its `style` and `lineHeight` are merged before `BasicText`, so simply omitting a zero override would still inherit the token. A native-button bridge test failed on the former zero property and passes after the correction; all 26 button tests and a capped one-worker Kotlin compile pass. The same serializer serves native list, app-bar, tooltip, action-sheet and expansion text slots.
- The theme typography bridge had the same sentinel mismatch: it omitted `height: 0`, leaving Compose's baseline role line-height token in place. It now forwards the font-metrics flag for any baseline or emphasized role, and `nativeM3ETypography` clears that role's inherited line height. The new theme bridge test failed before the change and all eight typography tests pass after it. Targeted Dart analysis, formatting and capped Kotlin compilation pass; on-device font-metrics rendering remains to verify.

## Badge host follows native size

- The [official Compose badge guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/badges) places the real `Badge` in a `BadgedBox` over its anchor. The pinned [Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3/1.5.0-rc01/material3-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) uses a 6 dp dot, a 16 dp text-badge minimum, 4 dp horizontal padding on each side of text, and 12/14 dp text or 6/6 dp dot offsets. The package already mirrored those offsets but estimated text width as Flutter text plus 12 dp; the actual Compose badge could be narrower or wider. Worse, `Positioned.fill` constrained a long badge host to the decorated child's width. Compose now reports its measured badge size with a content identity key; a Flutter `OverflowBox` lets the native view extend beyond its anchor, and the host recomputes the offset from that measured size. Old reports after a label change are ignored. Two geometry tests reproduced the size/identity defects, all 12 badge tests pass, targeted Dart analysis and a capped Kotlin compilation pass. Native paint, edge clipping, RTL and TalkBack still need a device check.

## Native snackbar timer begins after entry

- The official [`SnackbarHostState`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SnackbarHostState) suspends until the current snackbar is addressed, and the pinned AndroidX `SnackbarHost.kt` keeps a dismissed item composed through its native fade/scale exit. The package's custom finite-duration coroutine previously began its delay before `showSnackbar` made the item visible. A very short duration could dismiss an absent item, leaving the later `Indefinite` snackbar and Dart queue stuck. The timer now waits for the actual `Snackbar` content's `DisposableEffect` entry before delaying. After a visible item is addressed, the overlay still waits for that effect's disposal after the official exit animation. An external dismissal before the first native frame skips that exit wait because no item entered. A capped, offline, one-worker Kotlin compilation passes; device timing remains to verify.
- The same native popup can recompose for bottom-inset or theme changes. Its host state, entry signal and exit signal now share the request's message/action/duration identity. The coroutine uses that host identity as its restart key, following AndroidX's behavior of fixing the accessibility-adjusted timeout when an item appears; a later accessibility recomposition no longer restarts the same request with a completed entry signal. The terminal callback uses `rememberUpdatedState`, so it remains current without restarting the native animation. A second capped offline Kotlin compilation passed; hardware timing and live accessibility-service changes still need a device check.

## Tooltip touch duration begins on release

- The [Compose tooltip guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tooltip) uses a real `TooltipBox` and `PlainTooltip` for long-press and hover help; the package already hosts those native components. The public [`showDuration` contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/Tooltip/showDuration.html) measures the visible time after a long press is released. The Android bridge had sent a finite native timer at long-press recognition, so a long hold could dismiss the tooltip under the finger before release. The Flutter gesture bridge now keeps the native tooltip persistent through the hold and starts a dismissal timer from `onLongPressEnd`; hover and keyboard focus retain their own persistent lifecycle, and entering either cancels a pending touch dismissal. The new held-touch test failed before the change and passes with all 13 focused tooltip tests. Device placement, haptics and TalkBack remain unverified.
- [Flutter's `RawTooltip`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/RawTooltip-class.html) wraps its child in `Semantics(tooltip: …)`, allowing a screen reader to discover the help text on the anchor. The native route showed a Compose popup with its own live region but did not expose the tooltip on the Flutter control before opening it. The Android wrapper now applies the same anchor semantics while preserving the child's label and tap action. The new semantics test failed before that wrapper and passes with all 14 focused tooltip tests, targeted Dart analysis and formatting. Android TalkBack should still verify how Flutter's anchor semantics and Compose's popup announcement interact.
- The same `RawTooltip` returns its child immediately when the tooltip message is empty. The adaptive Android path previously created an empty Compose popup on long press. `AdaptiveTooltip` now returns the child for an empty message on every platform. A focused long-press test failed before the guard and passes with all 15 tooltip tests; targeted analysis and formatting pass. Removing a visible tooltip by changing its message to empty disposes the native wrapper and requests popup dismissal.

## Native search app-bar localization

- Flutter's [`MaterialLocalizations.searchFieldLabel`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/MaterialLocalizations/searchFieldLabel.html) is the localized hint for a search field; Android's [localization guidance](https://developer.android.com/guide/topics/resources/localization) likewise advises against hardcoded user-facing strings. The native search app bar previously sent the constructor's English `Search` default to the real Compose `SearchBarDefaults.InputField` on every locale. `AdaptiveAppBar` now retains whether a hint was supplied: the native bridge uses the current Material localization when absent and keeps an explicit override through `copyWith`. A Spanish delegate regression failed with `Search` before the change and passes with `Buscar`; the explicit-hint case and seven existing top-bar cases also pass. The public `searchHint` getter still reads `Search` by default for source compatibility; the rendered native hint is localized. Language-change recomposition and TalkBack remain device checks.

## Native input keyboard options

- The official [Compose `KeyboardOptions` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/text/KeyboardOptions) lets state-based outlined and secure fields request the actual IME keyboard layout. `AdaptiveAlertDialogInput.keyboardType` had crossed Flutter as a numeric enum index, but its real Compose `OutlinedTextField` never applied it. The native dialog now receives the same named keyboard contract as `AdaptiveTextField`, supplies `KeyboardOptions` to its real text field, and retains the secure field's autocorrect-off/password defaults. Both routes share the Dart encoder and Compose parser; the parser maps the current [native signed, decimal, date/time, visible-password, name and address keyboard types](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/text/input/KeyboardType) without discarding Flutter's signed and decimal flags. Secure numeric inputs use Compose's corresponding password keyboard types. `TextInputType.none` means no software keyboard in Flutter; Compose's `showKeyboardOnFocus` only controls focus-triggered display, so these requests retain the Flutter field rather than silently opening an IME. Keyboard layouts are ultimately chosen by the installed IME and still need device checks. The email and signed-decimal dialog tests failed before the bridge change; all 57 focused dialog/field tests and capped Kotlin compilation pass after it.

## Alert dialog icon tokens

- The official [`AlertDialogDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AlertDialogDefaults) exposes a dedicated `iconContentColor` and `IconSize`, and AndroidX's [`AlertDialog` implementation](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/b77e8fee1b5b7a6c01591d85e16b1d1af45c748c/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AlertDialog.kt) provides the icon color through `LocalContentColor` inside its icon slot. The package's native icon had forced the theme's `primary` color and a local 24 dp size when callers omitted overrides. It now consumes the actual dialog color and size tokens while preserving explicit Flutter `iconColor` and `iconSize`. The real Compose `AlertDialog` still controls layout and motion. A one-worker offline Kotlin compilation passed after the change; visual color, size and contrast in light/dark dynamic palettes remain device checks.
- The same [AndroidX dialog layout](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/dd7d2935e6a5ab12f6957a813926eea1ca38188e/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AlertDialog.kt) adds its text padding only when a non-null `text` slot exists. The package always supplied a `Column`, even for a title-and-actions dialog with no message, code or input. That empty column still caused Compose to reserve the text slot's padding. The native route now supplies `text = null` when those contents are absent and omits blank title/message/code values, so Compose owns the correct title-to-actions spacing. The real `AlertDialog`, its `FlowRow` actions and their native motion remain. A capped offline Kotlin compilation passed; precise device spacing remains to be checked.

## Alert dialog action identity and names

- Compose's [accessibility defaults](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults) merge a Material button's child text into its accessible action. `AlertAction` allowed an empty or whitespace-only title, creating a real clickable dialog `TextButton` without a spoken name. Both public alert entry points now validate every action before opening the route; malformed direct native action rows are skipped. A focused regression showed the missing validation before the change, then passed. TalkBack announcement order remains a device check.
- The native dialog returns an action index only after its route closes. If the app mutates the supplied `actions` list while the dialog is visible, resolving the index against that live list can invoke a different callback or change whether an input result counts as Cancel. Both Android routes now snapshot the presented `AlertAction` objects for serialization and result handling. The standard-dialog reorder test failed on the old lookup; it and an input-dialog Cancel reorder case pass after the snapshot. The real Compose dialog still owns button layout, pressed shapes and exit animation.

## Date picker range consistency

- The official [Compose date-picker guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/datepickers) uses `DatePickerState.selectedDateMillis` for confirmation and supports constrained dates through the picker state. The package already passed first/last dates to `SelectableDates`, but the native bridge could open with an initial date outside those limits, unlike Flutter's `showDatePicker` path. `AdaptiveDatePicker.show` now validates the initial day and bound order before opening a date or date-time picker, and the native confirm button accepts only a selected UTC day inside the requested range. Pure time mode does not impose a date range. The same real Compose `DatePickerDialog` and `DatePicker` remain in use. Two focused range regressions and one-worker offline Kotlin compilation pass; selection and accessibility still need device checks.
- [CupertinoDatePicker's range contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/cupertino/CupertinoDatePicker-class.html) applies to the full selected `DateTime` in date-and-time mode, while the [Compose date picker](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/datepickers) chooses a day and the [Compose time picker](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/time-pickers) separately chooses an hour and minute. The Android bridge had sent bounds only to the calendar, so the native time dialog could confirm an out-of-range hour. The aligned initial date-time is now checked against the full bounds. The selected boundary day supplies its first/last allowed grid minute to the real `TimePicker` or `TimeInput`; the native confirm button stays disabled for a snapped time outside that interval. The Dart bridge also rejects a malformed out-of-range result. When the first day has no valid interval minute, the calendar starts on the following day. A regression reproduced the missing 10:30 limit before the change and another reproduced the unavailable first day; all 12 focused picker tests, the complete 802-test Flutter suite, full Dart analysis and capped one-worker offline Kotlin compilation pass. Dial/input interaction and TalkBack still require a connected device. The separate time-only and Flutter year-grid fallback paths remain for a later bounds audit.

- The follow-up [Cupertino time-mode rule](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/cupertino/CupertinoDatePicker/minimumDate.html) constrains the hour only when combining it with the picker's initial calendar day. `AdaptiveDatePicker.show(mode: time)` now validates its aligned initial date-time and passes same-day first/last minute limits to the existing Compose `TimePicker`/`TimeInput` dialog. The Android year-first path still uses Flutter's real year calendar, then now passes the selected day's exact time limits into that same native Compose time dialog. The three regressions (missing time-only minimum, invalid initial hour, and missing year-first time limit) failed before correction; all 15 picker bridge tests and the full 805-test Flutter suite pass afterward, with full Dart analysis and formatting. The Kotlin dialog was compiled in the preceding check and is unchanged here. The non-native Flutter Material time-dialog fallback has no built-in bound controls and remains to review separately; device interaction and TalkBack remain unverified.

## Icon-only button names

- The official [Compose accessibility defaults](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults#graphic-elements) say interactive icons need a localized description because the icon glyph alone does not provide a spoken name. The native `AdaptiveButton.icon` and icon-only `AdaptiveButton.child` routes could previously create a real Compose `Button` with an unnamed icon. Android now requires a nonblank `semanticLabel` (or an `Icon.semanticLabel` for child content) before creating that native view. The existing Compose button, pressed shape, colors and tooltip behavior remain. Package examples and icon-only size/color tests now provide names; all 41 focused button tests pass. Existing Android icon-only callers without a name must add one. Actual TalkBack announcements remain a device check.

## Native icon-button names

- The same [Compose accessibility guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults#graphic-elements) applies to the separate real `IconButton` and `IconToggleButton` variants. `AdaptiveIconButton` previously passed a null or whitespace-only `contentDescription` when no usable action name was supplied. The native bridge now resolves the first nonblank `semanticLabel`, `tooltip`, selected `Icon.semanticLabel`, or base `Icon.semanticLabel`; it rejects a serializable Android icon button if all are absent. A blank semantic override no longer masks a valid tooltip. Existing native sizes, widths, selected/pressed shape morphs, colors, badges and click behavior remain. All 13 focused icon-button tests pass. Flutter fallback for an icon style Compose cannot serialize remains unchanged; Android TalkBack still needs a device check.

## Picker minute-grid contract

- Flutter's official [`CupertinoDatePicker.minuteInterval`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/cupertino/CupertinoDatePicker/minuteInterval.html) requires a positive integer factor of 60, even though Compose's real `TimePicker` has no minute-step parameter. The package's Android path previously accepted zero or nondivisors and rounded the confirmed time onto a grid that iOS cannot present. Both public adaptive picker entry points now validate the factor before opening any route; Compose still owns its actual time and date controls, and valid intervals retain the existing initial/result snapping. The check also applies to date-only mode because Cupertino's constructor asserts the same parameter there. The focused picker and snackbar suites pass 33 tests; this is Dart-only. On-device time entry and rollover remain to verify.

- The same [Cupertino minute-grid contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/cupertino/CupertinoDatePicker/minuteInterval.html) exposed a midnight rollover in the Android date-and-time bridge. Compose's real `TimePickerState` has no interval parameter, so a raw 23:59 confirmation with a 15-minute interval was rounded to 00:00 by the shared `TimeOfDay` helper; `AdaptiveDatePicker` then attached that time to the original selected day, moving the result almost 24 hours backward. Date-backed native dialogs now align to the closest interval minute *within* their selected day (23:45 in that case). The native confirm-button bound calculation uses the identical within-day rule, preserving real `TimePicker`/`TimeInput` interaction and avoiding a mismatch between an enabled button and the returned value. Standalone `AdaptiveTimePicker.show`, which has no calendar day, retains its documented wrap behavior. The integration test reproduced 00:00 before correction and passed with 23:45 afterward; all 16 picker bridge tests, full 806-test Flutter suite, full Dart analysis and one-worker capped Kotlin compilation pass. Physical dial/input and TalkBack checks remain pending.

## Input-chip enabled state and menu action names

- The Flutter 3.47.9 [`InputChip` implementation](https://github.com/flutter/flutter/blob/stable/packages/flutter/lib/src/material/input_chip.dart) combines `isEnabled` with the presence of `onSelected`, `onDeleted`, or `onPressed` before building its raw chip. Although the package's fallback passes its public `enabled` flag rather than the precomputed `interactive` flag, a chip without a callback is already disabled. Compose receives that same computed interactive state. This source audit found no input-chip color or interaction correction to make.
- The official [Compose menu guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/menu) uses visible `Text` inside every actionable `DropdownMenuItem`, and [Compose accessibility defaults](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults) merge that text into the action semantics. The package's popup and context-menu models allowed empty or whitespace-only titles, creating unnamed native rows. `AdaptivePopupMenuButton.text`, `.icon`, and `.widget` now validate item labels; the text trigger also requires a visible label, and `AdaptiveContextMenu` validates action titles. The real Compose dropdown and its item shape/exit transitions remain. Raw malformed channel rows are skipped defensively in the two popup routes and context route. All 27 focused menu tests, full Flutter analysis and one-worker offline Kotlin compilation pass. Existing callers with blank menu labels must supply meaningful text; TalkBack remains a device check.

- The [official Compose tooltip guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tooltip) distinguishes plain descriptions from rich explanations with an optional title and action. The public [`RichTooltip` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/RichTooltip.composable) provides those slots, native shape and color roles. `AdaptiveTooltip` now chooses real `RichTooltip` when Android-only title or action options are set and otherwise retains `PlainTooltip`. A real Compose `TextButton` emits its action once; Flutter requests native dismissal and calls the callback only after Compose reports the exit complete. The action label and callback must be paired. Title-only rich tooltips are also supported. Runtime touch and TalkBack behavior still need a connected Android device.
- The [official PlainTooltip API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/PlainTooltip.composable) and [RichTooltip API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/RichTooltip.composable) both accept `caretShape`; null omits the caret. `TooltipDefaults.caretShape()` supplies the real Material default, and its `DpSize` overload supplies custom dimensions. `AdaptiveTooltip.androidCaret` now chooses that default on Android, while a finite positive `androidCaretSize` selects the sized native shape. Omitting both preserves the current no-caret default. Neither option changes iOS or Flutter fallbacks.
- The [Compose search-bar guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/search-bar) uses a visible Search placeholder, while Flutter defines the localized search-field hint as [`MaterialLocalizations.searchFieldLabel`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/MaterialLocalizations/searchFieldLabel.html). The standalone `AdaptiveSearchBar` previously sent an empty string to its real Compose `SearchBarDefaults.InputField` when `hintText` was omitted. Its Android bridge and Material fallback now use the current Material localization, preserving an explicit hint including the empty string. A Spanish delegate proves that the default is not hardcoded English. The existing native collapsed-only search overload still needs a separate expanded-results contract before migration to the new state-based API.
- The [current state-based Compose `SearchBar`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SearchBar.composable) is the collapsed field and is paired with [`ExpandedFullScreenSearchBar`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ExpandedFullScreenSearchBar.composable). The standalone adaptive widget had no results content, so its default compact route cannot be swapped to the new overload without creating an empty expansion on focus. An opt-in `androidSuggestions` list now supplies real result data for the official state-based pair; Compose filters the strings, renders real `ListItem` entries, owns expand/collapse motion, and sends selection after the native close animation. The Back icon uses the caller locale's Material `backButtonTooltip`, and disabled results cannot be selected. Null retains the compact-only overload; an empty non-null list allows later query-driven updates. The bridge tests, full Dart analysis, capped Kotlin compilation after the localized Back action, and the 819-test Flutter suite all pass. Device focus, IME, Back, result scrolling and TalkBack remain open.

- The official [Compose tooltip position provider](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TooltipDefaults) accepts a native `Dp` anchor spacing. `AdaptiveTooltip.androidSpacing` was previously passed through without validation, so a non-finite or negative value could reach Compose as an invalid gap. The Android bridge now requires finite nonnegative spacing before creating the overlay; the focused regression failed before the guard and all 23 tooltip tests pass afterward. This is Dart-only, so no Android compilation was started while other projects may be building. Native placement still needs device inspection.

- The [official state-based search-bar source](https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/0624f640fd3a47edfcf8a070f609d278fb5eb41b/compose/material3/material3/src/commonMain/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SearchBar.kt) places expanded search in a separate dialog or popup and requests focus for its input on expansion. The standalone bridge currently reports each `InputField` focus change to the Flutter `FocusNode`; a transient loss from the collapsed field could race with the expanded field's focus request. This is a source-level risk, not an observed failure. An Android device check should tap to expand, type through the IME, collapse with Back, and move keyboard focus into results while observing the Flutter `FocusNode` on both compact and larger windows. Avoid changing focus ownership from this static inference alone.

- The official [`ExpandedDockedSearchBar` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ExpandedDockedSearchBar.composable) recommends a docked popup on medium and large screens, with [`ExpandedFullScreenSearchBar`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ExpandedFullScreenSearchBar.composable) on compact screens. Android's [window-size guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/adaptive-apps/guides/use-window-size-classes) starts medium width at 600 dp. The standalone suggestion route now reads the current native window configuration and uses the real docked composable at or above that threshold, retaining the same `SearchBarState`, native results list, Back action and callback-after-collapse path. AndroidX's docked layout constrains result height within the window and requests focus in its popup. A capped one-worker offline Kotlin compilation passed with 49% free system memory and no active compiler at preflight. Popup placement, window resize, IME, focus and TalkBack require a device.

- The [`ExpandedDockedSearchBarWithGap` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ExpandedDockedSearchBarWithGap.composable) and [`rememberSearchBarWithGapState`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/rememberSearchBarWithGapState.composable) specifically pair the docked popup and its native spatial/effects motion with `AppBarWithSearch`. The top-bar search route now uses that pair from 600 dp and retains `ExpandedFullScreenContainedSearchBar` below that width. It holds the active choice through expansion and pending result/submission callbacks before changing layout after collapse; both choices use the same `TextFieldState` and `LazyColumn` results. This is distinct from the standalone search route, which pairs ordinary `SearchBar` with `ExpandedDockedSearchBar` on medium/larger windows. A capped offline one-worker Kotlin compile passed after the code change. Popup positioning, width changes while open, focus, IME, Back, and TalkBack remain device checks.

- The standalone `AdaptiveSearchBar` already chose the official full-screen or docked result surface at the [600 dp medium-width breakpoint](https://developer.android.com/develop/adaptive-apps/guides/use-window-size-classes), but the choice was recomputed on every composition. A width change while results were open could replace the native dialog/popup before its search state closed. It now latches the chosen surface until `SearchBarState` is collapsed and its animation and any pending result/submission callback have completed, matching the app-bar route. The search text state and result list remain shared. This is a source-level transition fix; runtime resize behavior still needs an Android device. A capped offline Kotlin compilation passed after a no-compiler/55% free-memory preflight.

## Native motion call-site audit

- Rechecked every package-owned `animateFloatAsState`, `AnimationState.animateTo`, `spring`, and `tween` call in the Android Compose bridge against the official [`MotionScheme`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/MotionScheme) and the pinned component APIs. The two small arrow rotations use the theme's fast spatial spring; top app-bar snap uses its Material snap spec; search-bar snap uses the default effects spec; loading and progress use their official component-specific specs. Carousel item taps use Compose's native `animateScrollToItem` spring by default. Its only fixed-duration tween is selected when the caller explicitly supplies `scrollAnimationDuration`; zero requests an immediate jump. The other fixed curves found in Flutter are iOS or non-native fallback paths, plus a toolbar visibility wrapper whose private `_visible` state is never set false. A first attempt to remove the tab view's fixed-duration fallback failed two focused Flutter tests because the same callback is used by iOS and non-native fallback tabs; the branch was restored, and all eight focused native/fallback tab tests pass. No native animation call-site correction follows this audit. Actual pacing, reduced-motion behavior, and the carousel's custom-duration override still need device checks.

## Switch-row interaction source check

- The [official Compose switch guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/switch) describes tapping and dragging a switch. The pinned Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 `Switch.kt` source, however, contains an explicit pending swipe implementation and attaches a `toggleable` modifier only when `onCheckedChange` is non-null. In `AdaptiveSwitchListTile`, the real clickable `ListItem` owns the whole-row toggle when there is no separate tile action; its child `Switch` has a null callback to avoid a second interactive target. When a distinct `onTap` tile action exists, the trailing real `Switch` receives its own callback and touch target. This follows the actual pinned Compose behavior, but row versus trailing-control focus, ripple, and TalkBack nodes still need device verification. No custom drag simulation should be added to represent a behavior that this AndroidX release does not implement.

## Native localization and release recheck

- Reviewed the remaining English fallback strings in the Android bridge. The public app-bar, search-bar, popup, split-button, FAB-menu, date-picker, and time-picker routes send localized labels from Flutter `MaterialLocalizations` or explicit caller strings; Kotlin's English values are defensive defaults for a missing bridge property. Flutter's `expandedHint` and `collapsedHint` are named from the current expansion-tile state but their values are reversed relative to that name in the default English implementation, so the split-button bridge correctly sends `collapsedHint` for its expanded state and `expandedHint` for its collapsed state. Replacing these with literal English strings would regress localization. Device announcements remain to be checked.
- The [official Compose Material 3 release table](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/compose-material3) still lists `1.4.0` as stable and `1.5.0-rc01` as the release candidate on 2026-11-11. The package deliberately pins `1.5.0-rc01` because its expressive APIs used here are newer than the stable release. No dependency change follows this recheck.

## Standalone switch geometry

- The pinned [Material 3 `SwitchTokens`](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3/1.5.0-rc01/material3-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) define a 52 × 32 dp track, and the real `Switch` applies `minimumInteractiveComponentSize()` before its visual `requiredSize`. The standalone `AdaptiveSwitch` previously reserved a 64 × 48 dp Flutter host and imposed the same width on Compose, creating 6 dp of extra horizontal space on each side. Its host and native modifier now reserve 52 × 48 dp: the actual track width and a height above the 48 dp touch minimum. A focused widget test failed with the former 64 dp width and passes after the change; all 25 related selection tests and capped offline Kotlin compilation pass. On-device touch bounds and alignment beside text still need inspection.

## Rich tooltip pointer and focus transfer

- The official [Compose tooltip guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tooltip) shows `RichTooltip` with a real button action. In this package the anchor is Flutter, while `TooltipBox` renders its popup in a separate Compose window. Flutter's immediate anchor hover exit or blur previously requested dismissal before a pointer or keyboard focus could reach that action. This was reproduced by a widget test before the change.
- The public native `RichTooltip` and `TextButton` remain intact. Compose now reports pointer enter/exit on the popup and focus changes on the action. Flutter allows 250 ms for transfer and keeps the popup open while either its anchor or native action remains hovered or focused. An initial inactive focus state is ignored so touch long-press is not dismissed prematurely. Exiting both requests the normal native dismissal spring; the action callback still waits for exit completion. The 26 focused tooltip tests, Dart analysis and Kotlin compilation pass. Actual cross-window pointer traversal and accessibility timing still require a connected Android device.

## Rich tooltip action ownership

- The official [`TooltipBox` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TooltipBox.composable) exposes `hasAction` and says an action can force a focusable popup for TalkBack. Its sample passes `hasAction = true` for `RichTooltip` with a `TextButton`. The package previously omitted that argument; it now passes true only when an action is present, keeping plain and title-only tooltips on the native default.
- The pinned AndroidX `TooltipState.show()` suspends until the state dismisses, and `dismiss()` ends that suspension. The package's previous `LaunchedEffect` waited only for its Flutter dismissal signal, so a native outside-click dismissal could leave the one-pixel overlay and Flutter `_visible` flag alive indefinitely. A companion dismiss coroutine now watches the Flutter signal or timeout while the main effect joins the native show job; either native or Flutter dismissal waits for the actual exit transition before emitting `dismissed`. Offline Kotlin compilation passes. The TalkBack and outside-click paths need device confirmation.

## Compact search-bar API compatibility

- The pinned AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 [`SearchBar.kt` source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3/1.5.0-rc01/material3-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) couples focus to expansion in the new state-based `SearchBarDefaults.InputField`. Its state-based collapsed `SearchBar` wraps the input in `DisableSoftKeyboard`, so a collapsed-only migration without an `ExpandedFullScreenSearchBar` or `ExpandedDockedSearchBar` would stop ordinary touch typing. The package's `androidSuggestions` route already pairs the new state-based input with the official expanded view. The no-suggestions route intentionally keeps the deprecated but real Compose input and `SearchBar` overload, whose `onExpandedChange = {}` preserves the package's compact text-entry contract. This source-level audit found no safe one-line migration; the two deprecation warnings are understood. A future migration requires an explicit results/expanded-content contract and device IME verification.

## Native card elevation contract

- Flutter 3.47.9's `Card` constructors assert that an explicit elevation is nonnegative. The official [Compose `Card` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Card.composable) accepts a `CardElevation` for the real component; `CardDefaults.cardElevation` does not validate arbitrary bridge numbers before converting them to `Dp`. `AdaptiveCard` now checks that Android elevation is finite and nonnegative before passing it to that native factory. This affects malformed caller input only, preserving the existing native default when elevation is omitted. Eight valid-card widget tests and targeted Dart analysis pass; no Kotlin source changed in this follow-up.

## Native Material card variants

- The official [Compose card guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/card) distinguishes filled `Card`, `ElevatedCard` and `OutlinedCard`, each with its own native colors, shape and elevation tokens; Flutter exposes the matching [`Card.filled`, `Card`, and `Card.outlined`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/Card-class.html). The package previously exposed only the filled Compose surface even when callers wanted a real elevated or outlined card. `AdaptiveCard.style` now selects those official variants on Android and the matching Flutter framework constructors. An omitted style preserves the former Android filled surface and the former Flutter default `Card`, avoiding a silent visual migration for existing apps. `AdaptiveCard.glass` maps to the native elevated variant with its existing high surface color and one-dp elevation. A requested custom border behind an elevated card uses Compose's underlying `Card` with the elevated tokens because `ElevatedCard` has no border parameter; foreground custom borders remain in the existing pointer-transparent Flutter overlay. The new bridge test failed to compile before the API, then the card tests, targeted Dart analysis and capped offline one-worker Kotlin compilation passed. Runtime color, elevation animation, outline, interaction and accessibility remain Android device checks.

## Connected button-group shape-state audit

- The pinned AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 [`ButtonGroup.kt` source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3/1.5.0-rc01/material3-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) gives connected leading, middle and trailing buttons a shared `connectedButtonCheckedShape = ShapeTokens.CornerFull`. `ToggleButtonShapes` has separate ordinary, pressed and checked shapes.
