The package's vertical connected group adapts only ordinary and pressed top/bottom corners; it deliberately preserves the official full checked shape. Rounding just the checked group's outer corners would regress the native selected-state morph, so this audit makes no shape edit. Vertical grouping itself composes real native `ToggleButton` controls because the official `ButtonGroup` layout is horizontal.
- The same pinned [`Chip.kt` source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3/1.5.0-rc01/material3-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) exposes one `shape` for `AssistChip` and `SuggestionChip`, whereas filter and input chips expose state shapes. The package correctly applies native `FilterChipDefaults.shapes()` and `InputChipDefaults.shapes()` only to those selectable chips; adding a fabricated press morph to action chips would not use the official API. Device checks still need to observe selected/pressed group shapes and all chip variants.

## Button-group selection membership

- The real Compose [`ButtonGroup`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ButtonGroup.composable) receives one checked state per actual action. The Flutter bridge accepted a controlled `selectedIndices` set with indices that no longer existed after an action-list change. In a required-selection group, `{0, 99}` counted as two selections, so deselecting the sole visible action `0` was allowed and returned the invalid `{99}`. `_currentSelection` now intersects caller indices with the current action range. This keeps native checked values, the Flutter fallback and `selectionRequired` consistent after removal or reorder. The native-property regression failed before normalization; it and the fallback regression pass in the 26-test focused suite. The change is Dart-only; on-device selection announcements remain pending.

## Button-group no-op selection events

- The native [`ToggleButton`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ToggleButton.composable) receives a controlled `checked` value and reports proposed state changes. The Flutter bridge previously forwarded even a repeated current value to callers. A focused test first reproduced two false callbacks for a single-select group (`0` then `null`) and repeated membership callbacks for a multi-select group. The bridge now ignores an event when its requested `checked` value already matches the current valid membership, while retaining the existing action identity, enabled and required-selection guards. All 27 group tests and targeted Dart analysis pass. This does not replace an on-device check for rapid taps and delayed overflow events.

## Button-group single-selection range

- The native `ButtonGroup` composes checked values only for actual actions. After an action list shrank, the Flutter bridge could still serialize an explicit `selectedIndex` such as `99` even though that slot no longer existed. It now maps an explicit out-of-range index to the native no-selection sentinel `-1` and excludes it from `_currentSelection`. This retains the distinction between an invalid explicit controlled value and an omitted value: the former must not reactivate an action's `checked` hint through Compose's fallback. The regression first observed `99` in native properties and now sees `-1`; all 28 focused group tests and targeted Dart analysis pass. Android runtime selection announcements remain to check.

## Native progress default-token audit

- The [Android Compose progress guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/progress) uses the real determinate and indeterminate `LinearProgressIndicator` and `CircularProgressIndicator`, and animates determinate updates with `ProgressIndicatorDefaults.ProgressAnimationSpec`. The pinned AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 source also defines the expressive wavy variants. Its linear wavy container is 10 dp high; the circular flat and wavy sizes are 40 and 48 dp; the active and track strokes are 4 dp. The package's default Flutter host geometry, native wavy/flat composables and native default colors match these values. The distinct circular wavy animation recommendation is recorded in the later motion audit below.
- The pinned source supplies progress-range semantics inside each real indicator. When a custom `semanticsLabel` is present, the package adds an outer merging semantics modifier. Compose's nested merging-node behavior may expose a separate label node; this audit cannot establish the resulting Android accessibility tree from source alone. Keep the label plus progress announcement as an explicit device/TalkBack check before changing the modifier or claiming accessibility parity.

## Split-button menu availability parity

- Android's native split-button bridge already disables its trailing menu when `onSelected` is absent or every item is disabled. The existing Flutter and iOS fallback segments still opened an inert menu in those states. The shared `menuEnabled` condition now controls the native, Flutter and iOS routes, while each route keeps its existing real platform control. A focused fallback test failed on the former enabled menu; it and an iOS disabled-segment case pass in the 16-test split-button suite. Targeted Dart analysis passes. No Kotlin source changed, and Android popup exit/TalkBack checks remain pending.

## Segmented-control repeated selection

- The official [Compose segmented-button guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/segmented-button) describes a single-choice row with one selected option. The package's real `SegmentedButton` calls its native click handler even when the already selected item is pressed, and Flutter previously forwarded that as a new `onValueChanged` event. The bridge now compares the event with the effective selected index it serializes to Compose, including after clamping a stale caller index. A focused test failed with a redundant `[0]` callback before the guard, then all 25 segmented-control and related tests plus targeted Dart analysis passed. The native ripple remains Compose-owned; TalkBack and keyboard behavior still need device checks.

## Text-tab accessible names and reselection

- The official [Compose tabs guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tabs) gives each `Tab` either text or an icon and an `onClick` action. `AdaptiveTabBarView` exposes text labels only; a blank label therefore created a real interactive Compose tab with no accessible name. The bridge now rejects whitespace-only tab labels before any platform route builds. The new native case failed before validation; all nine tab-view tests and targeted Dart analysis pass afterward. Native focus and TalkBack still need a device check.
- A tap on the currently selected tab was also audited before editing page motion. Flutter's `ScrollPositionWithSingleContext.goBallistic` goes idle when the custom page physics returns no simulation at the settled target; during an ongoing swipe, requesting that tab can intentionally spring back to it. The existing routing therefore retains useful behavior. No unconditional same-index guard was added to the page-motion path.

## Explicit navigation-rail width validation

- The official [`WideNavigationRail`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/WideNavigationRail.composable) still owns the expanded layout and its width motion. The package's `expandedWidth` is a caller override for the Flutter host; a NaN or nonpositive override previously reached `AndroidM3EPlatformView` and failed later as malformed `BoxConstraints`. The bridge now rejects nonfinite and nonpositive overrides before creating any platform view, while leaving valid custom widths and the native auto-width path untouched. The new regression first failed on NaN constraints and passes for all invalid cases; all 16 focused rail tests and targeted Dart analysis pass. Actual width paint and animation remain device checks.

## Measured navigation-rail viewport continuity

- The pinned AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 [`WideNavigationRail.kt` source](https://dl.google.com/dl/android/maven2/androidx/compose/material3/material3/1.5.0-rc01/material3-1.5.0-rc01-sources.jar) measures expanded content width inside its 220–360 dp range and uses `DefaultSpatial` springs for the expanded/collapsed width limits. Compose reports the resulting width through the package's `onSizeChanged` bridge. Flutter previously discarded the last measured width whenever a destination label or another measurement input changed, exposing the full 360 dp platform view until the next native event. The Flutter clipping host now retains its last measured width while waiting for the current keyed Compose measurement; its existing build constraint still clamps that width if the available maximum shrinks. A focused test observed the old 220→360 dp jump before the change and now verifies 220 dp retention, stale-event rejection, and the subsequent 320 dp native measurement. Physical-frame continuity and RTL appearance still need an Android device check.

## Bottom-navigation selection after destination removal

- The official [Compose navigation-bar guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/navigation-bar) treats `selected` as the per-item visual state and delegates clicks to the caller. When an app shrank its destination list but retained a former `selectedIndex`, the native bridge serialized an index outside that list, leaving no Compose item selected. The Flutter Material fallback instead forwarded the same index to `NavigationBar`, whose constructor asserts that it lies within the current destination range. CupertinoTabBar has the same range assertion, and the iOS 26 native scaffold/UITabBar path also consumed the stale index. The scaffold now bounds any nonnull configured index once against the current list before all four routes use it; an intentional null remains null for the native Android bar. The Android and Flutter regressions failed before the initial change, and the Cupertino regression failed before the shared iOS follow-up. All 17 bottom-navigation tests, including both iOS 26 branches, and targeted Dart analysis pass. Flutter's fallback `NavigationBar` cannot display a null selection and continues to show its first item in that case; the public comment states this limit. Native selection motion and TalkBack still need device verification.

## Badge token and placement source audit

- The official [Compose badge guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/badges) uses `BadgedBox` as the anchor and the real `Badge` for both a dot and short text. In the pinned AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 `Badge.kt`, `Badge` supplies its own token shape, minimum size, color and text style; `BadgedBox` places a text badge 12 dp inward and 14 dp down from the anchor corner, or a dot 6 dp inward/down. The package's native badge calls that `Badge` directly and its Flutter host reproduces those placement offsets in both text directions without enlarging the decorated child's layout. The existing focused placement, resize, RTL and child-hit tests cover the bridge geometry; Compose paint and TalkBack still need a device.
- The initial Flutter host width for text is deliberately conservative: it estimates Compose's label width with Flutter text metrics plus 12 dp, while `Badge.kt` uses 4 dp of horizontal padding on each side. The actual Compose `onSizeChanged` measurement replaces that estimate. This can produce a transient first-frame width difference when the two engines' font metrics differ; changing it to the token padding alone could clip text before native measurement. Keep this as a device-render check rather than claiming exact first-frame token fidelity.
- `Badge.kt` resolves an omitted content color with `contentColorFor(containerColor)`. The package's native route follows that contract, including a caller-supplied background. The public `AdaptiveBadge.textColor` comment previously promised `onError` for every Android background; it now describes the theme-color match and surrounding-content fallback accurately.

## Native expansion child lifecycle

- The native Android expansion header remains the real clickable Compose [`ListItem`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ListItem.composable). Its arbitrary `children` are Flutter widgets and follow the public [`ExpansionTile.maintainState` contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ExpansionTile/maintainState.html): when false, they are removed from the tree after closing; when true, they remain mounted. The bridge previously mounted them even while initially collapsed with the default false value. A focused test failed before the fix. The Flutter host now mounts them on opening, retains them until the existing spatial spring settles on closing, and then unmounts them. Reduced-motion closure unmounts immediately.
- The retained child subtree initially remained exposed to semantics while closed. Matching Flutter's `Expansible`, the host now uses `Offstage` and pauses child tickers when the closing spring settles. [Flutter documents](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/Offstage-class.html) that `Offstage` alone can still accept focus, so the closed subtree also uses [`ExcludeFocus`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/widgets/ExcludeFocus-class.html). The semantics and programmatic-focus regressions failed before their respective fixes. On-device header ripple, focus transitions and TalkBack still need checking.

## Native expansion child alignment

- Flutter's [`ExpansionTile.expandedCrossAxisAlignment`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ExpansionTile/expandedCrossAxisAlignment.html) aligns children inside their content column; `expandedAlignment` positions that column within the tile, while `childrenPadding` surrounds it. The native Android header uses Compose `ListItem`, but its Flutter body previously ignored `expandedAlignment`, defaulted the column to `stretch`, and applied `childrenPadding` around an inner center-aligned column that discarded an explicit cross-axis setting. The body now follows Flutter's `Align > Padding > Column` structure and inherits the theme's alignment/padding when unspecified. A 300 px geometry test first found the child 120 px away from the requested right edge; explicit and themed alignment tests now pass. `expandedAlignment` now accepts Flutter's full `AlignmentGeometry`, so `AlignmentDirectional` resolves in RTL; the iOS body also applies this alignment. Fourteen focused expansion-tile tests pass. Device rendering under large text and RTL remains open.

## Expansion shape and clipping gap

- The official Flutter [`ExpansionTile` shape](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ExpansionTile/shape.html) and [clip contract](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ExpansionTile/clipBehavior.html) apply to the complete expanded tile. Compose [`ListItemShapes`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ListItemDefaults) affects only the native header, so it cannot draw a caller's `ShapeBorder` around arbitrary Flutter children. The Android route now keeps its real Compose `ListItem` header and wraps the mixed header/body stack in a Flutter `Material` only when the caller or `ExpansionTileTheme` supplies a shape. That wrapper paints the custom border in front, clips the complete stack with the explicit/themed/default clip mode, and interpolates the border with the existing spatial spring without adding a second implicit animation. `clipBehavior` is nullable like Flutter's API so theme inheritance works; iOS retains its former `Clip.none` default. Tests failed first for the absent whole-tile shape and ignored theme clip, then passed with an intermediate spring shape and stable native-view state. Android rendering of the clipped platform view, border paint, ripple at corners and large child content still needs device inspection.

## Expansion child-axis validation

- The official Flutter [`ExpansionTile` constructor](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ExpansionTile/ExpansionTile.html) rejects `CrossAxisAlignment.baseline`: its children are in a vertical `Column`, where a cross-axis baseline cannot be applied. `AdaptiveExpansionTile` previously accepted it on every platform and deferred the failure until layout. A constructor regression first returned a widget instead of asserting; the wrapper now validates the same invariant. All fifteen focused expansion-tile tests pass, with no change to the real Compose header or spring.

## Native switch-row state changes

- The official [Compose `Switch` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Switch.composable) receives controlled `checked` state and proposes a new Boolean through `onCheckedChange`. The package's real Compose switch row already separates the row action from the switch when both callbacks exist, but its Flutter bridge forwarded a `changed` event even when it repeated the currently controlled value. A focused test first saw `[false, true]` from a false switch and now sees only `[true]`; the separate row tap remains delivered. The standalone `AdaptiveSwitch` already had the same state-change guard.
- The public `activeColor` is documented as the on-state track color. Native Compose maps it to `SwitchDefaults.colors(checkedTrackColor = ...)` and Cupertino maps it to `activeTrackColor`. The Material fallback instead set Flutter [`Switch.activeThumbColor`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/Switch/activeThumbColor.html), leaving [`activeTrackColor`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/Switch-class.html) null. A custom-font fallback regression failed before the correction. The fallback now colors the track and leaves the native default thumb. All thirteen switch-list-tile tests pass; device drag, color contrast, focus and TalkBack remain to verify.

## Native tabs

- The official [Compose tabs guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tabs) distinguishes primary tabs for the top content level from secondary tabs within a content area. `Tab` supports optional text and icon slots. The pinned Compose Material 3 source exposes `PrimaryTabRow`, `PrimaryScrollableTabRow`, `SecondaryTabRow` and `SecondaryScrollableTabRow`, plus different color defaults for each hierarchy. The bridge now exposes the hierarchy through `AdaptiveTabRowStyle` and serializable `AndroidM3EIcon` values while preserving its primary text-only default. Icon-only tabs still require localized `tabs` labels and use those labels as native icon content descriptions. A real Android device must verify final layout, focus, TalkBack announcements and selection animation.

## Toolbar action descriptions

- The official [Compose `AppBarScope` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/AppBarScope) says `clickableItem` and `toggleableItem` use `label` in the overflow menu and provides `customItem` for separate app-bar and menu content. The pinned AndroidX implementation also uses that `label` for the icon's automatic tooltip. The package forwarded an independent `AdaptiveToolbarAction.tooltip`, but the real visible action still displayed `label` on hover/long press. Actions whose tooltip differs now use the official `customItem` path: a native `TooltipBox` around the native icon control and a native `DropdownMenuItem` retaining the short overflow label. Selection, disabled state and event IDs follow the same path. Kotlin compiled with one worker; actual tooltip placement, hover, keyboard focus and TalkBack still require a device.

## Search bar API migration constraint

- Kotlin compilation warns that the compact, no-suggestions search route uses a deprecated `SearchBar`/`InputField` overload. The [official Android search-bar guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/search-bar) still documents that overload. The pinned 1.5.0-rc01 state-based `SearchBarDefaults.InputField` automatically expands on touch focus or typing, while its `SearchBar(state, ...)` disables the soft keyboard in the collapsed host and expects an `ExpandedFullScreenSearchBar` or `ExpandedDockedSearchBar` partner. Replacing only the compact route would therefore break editable search without suggestions. The package already uses the state-based pair when `androidSuggestions` is provided; keep the compact route on the official legacy overload until a native compact-editing API or an explicit always-expanded product behavior is chosen. A device check remains necessary for focus/IME behavior in both routes.

## Determinate loading motion token

- The pinned AndroidX [Compose `ProgressIndicatorDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ProgressIndicatorDefaults) defines `ProgressAnimationSpec` with no-bounce damping, very low stiffness and a 0.001 visibility threshold. The package had copied those three numbers into a local `spring` when driving the real determinate `LoadingIndicator` morph. It now uses the official token directly, matching the library's current timing and following token updates without maintaining a duplicate. Capped offline Kotlin compilation passed; device animation and reduced-motion checks remain pending.

## Circular progress size contract

- The official [Compose circular progress API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/CircularProgressIndicator.composable) accepts a `Modifier` for sizing; the pinned AndroidX source applies its default 40 dp size only after caller modifiers, and the wavy variant similarly defaults to 48 dp. The public `AdaptiveProgressIndicator.androidSize` accepted any finite positive value, but the Flutter host silently clamped it to 24–240 dp before sending it to Compose. A regression first observed `androidSize: 16` rendered as 24 dp. The bridge now passes the exact requested positive size, including 16 and 320 dp, while retaining 40/48 dp native defaults. All 21 focused progress tests and targeted Dart analysis pass. Very small sizes may be visually cramped; device rendering remains to check.

## Button geometry validation

- The official [Compose `ButtonDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/ButtonDefaults) provides size-dependent content padding and icon/text tokens. The package already uses those real tokens but calculates its Flutter host from `minSize` and custom `padding` before mounting Compose. A `minSize.width` of NaN reached Flutter as invalid `BoxConstraints`; a negative left inset was forwarded without an error and could reach Compose `PaddingValues`. Android now rejects nonfinite or negative dimensions/insets with field-specific errors before building the native view. Both regressions failed before the guard; all 29 focused button tests and targeted Dart analysis pass. Standard valid sizes and native pressed-shape motion are unaffected; device layout checks remain.

## Native elliptical button outline

- The official [Compose `Button` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/Button.composable) accepts `ButtonShapes` and states that its press morph interpolates `CornerBasedShape` families. The pinned AndroidX implementation snaps between shapes outside that family. A Flutter `BorderRadius` with different horizontal and vertical corner radii had forced the entire Android button onto Flutter. The bridge now sends all eight physical axes and uses the same Compose `GenericShape`/`RoundRect` normalization already used by native cards. It remains the real Compose `Button`/`TextButton`/`OutlinedButton`/`FilledTonalButton` with native ripple, colors, focus and semantics. The explicit ellipse is used for both resting and pressed shapes to avoid a snap to an unrelated default outline; the normal circular-corner path retains official press morphing. A regression first found no Android platform view, then all 29 button tests and capped Kotlin compilation passed. On-device outline, hit area and ripple clipping remain unverified.

## Icon-button size token audit

- The official [Compose `IconButtonDefaults`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/IconButtonDefaults) exposes five heights and three width options. The pinned 1.5.0-rc01 source derives each width from its icon size plus the matching leading/trailing spacing tokens. The Flutter host's width table matches those fifteen values exactly: extra small 28/32/40 dp, small 32/40/52 dp, medium 48/56/72 dp, large 64/96/128 dp, and extra large 104/136/184 dp (narrow/uniform/wide). The native Kotlin route calls `IconButtonDefaults.*ContainerSize` for the same option. Existing bridge tests cover representative extremes, and the 877-test full suite passes. On-device visual/touch geometry remains pending.

## Carousel source-version audit

- The [official Compose carousel guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/carousel) uses the real `HorizontalMultiBrowseCarousel` and `HorizontalUncontainedCarousel` with `CarouselItemScope.maskClip(MaterialTheme.shapes.extraLarge)`. The pinned AndroidX Material 3 1.5.0-rc01 source gives `HorizontalCenteredHeroCarousel` an `itemSpacing` default of `CarouselDefaults.ItemSpacing` and `CarouselState.animateScrollToItem` a default `spring()` animation. The package already passes that spacing token and calls the default animation when the caller omits a duration. No spacing or motion replacement is warranted on the pinned dependency.
- [AndroidX's 1.5.0 release notes](https://developer.android.com/jetpack/androidx/releases/compose-material3) record the multi-aspect API's simplification to a global `carouselParallaxScrollEffect` modifier in alpha28. The pinned source JAR contains that modifier and no dedicated `MultiAspectCarousel` composable. The package's native multi-aspect route uses Compose `LazyRow`/`LazyColumn` with the official modifier, rather than a hand-drawn Flutter carousel. Keep this route while 1.5.0-rc01 is pinned; compare against the actual dependency source again if the library version changes. Device checks for mask, parallax, touch and TalkBack remain open.

## Native text decoration

- Compose's official [`TextDecoration`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/text/style/TextDecoration) provides none, underline, strikethrough and their combination. The pinned native `Text` and `TextStyle` APIs accept that decoration, so Android buttons and text fields can retain these Flutter styles in the real Compose controls. Overline and custom decoration color, style or thickness still take the Flutter route because those effects are not represented by the Compose decoration contract. The two native-route regressions failed before this bridge and all 86 button/field tests pass afterward. Targeted Dart analysis, formatting and capped offline Kotlin compilation also pass; visual checks remain open without a device.

## Wavy progress motion

- The official [`LinearWavyProgressIndicator`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/LinearWavyProgressIndicator.composable) recommends `WavyProgressIndicatorDefaults.ProgressAnimationSpec` for determinate updates, while [`CircularWavyProgressIndicator`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/CircularWavyProgressIndicator.composable) recommends the spring `ProgressIndicatorDefaults.ProgressAnimationSpec`. The pinned AndroidX 1.5.0-rc01 source confirms the former is a timed tween and the latter a spring. The native bridge previously selected the timed wavy spec for both shapes; it now uses the timed spec only for linear wavy progress and the Compose spring for circular wavy and flat progress. A capped one-worker offline Kotlin compilation passes. An Android device is still needed to inspect the motion and TalkBack announcement.

## Tooltip geometry boundary

- The official [`TooltipBox` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/TooltipBox.composable) delegates placement to `TooltipDefaults.rememberTooltipPositionProvider`; the pinned AndroidX implementation rounds caller spacing to pixels. The official [`Modifier.absolutePadding`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/layout/absolutePadding.modifier) rejects negative padding. The Android Flutter bridge previously forwarded nonfinite `verticalOffset`, `height`, `padding`, or `margin`, and negative padding or margin, into those real Compose APIs. It now rejects invalid geometry before opening the native overlay. A finite negative vertical offset remains valid for intentional overlap; the separate `androidSpacing` contract remains nonnegative. The regression failed on the old bridge, then all 27 focused tooltip tests and targeted Dart analysis passed. Android positioning and TalkBack still require a device.

## Badge source recheck

- The current [official Compose badge guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/badges) still uses `BadgedBox` with a real `Badge` for dot and text variants, and permits any composable anchor. The pinned AndroidX 1.5.0-rc01 `Badge.kt` places dot badges 6 dp inward/down and text badges 12 dp inward/14 dp down, with a 6 dp dot and 16 dp text minimum. The standalone package badge uses the real Compose `Badge` over an arbitrary Flutter child; its measured Flutter host reproduces those offsets. Existing navigation controls use native `BadgedBox` directly. No size, color or component substitution follows from this recheck. Mixed Flutter/Compose clipping, semantics and edge placement still need an Android device.

## Navigation bar system-inset reservation

- The current [Compose navigation-bar guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/navigation-bar) places the real `NavigationBar` in a scaffold bottom slot. Pinned AndroidX 1.5.0-rc01 applies `NavigationBarDefaults.windowInsets` and `ShortNavigationBarDefaults.windowInsets`, each including horizontal and bottom system bars, before its 80 dp tall or 64 dp short minimum height. The package's Flutter `Scaffold.bottomNavigationBar` host reserves the matching base height plus `MediaQuery.viewPadding.bottom`. A new layout test gives Flutter a 24 dp bottom system inset and confirms 104/88 dp native host heights and a body ending at the bar's top in both styles; all 18 bottom-navigation tests and targeted Dart analysis pass. Compose's actual inset delivery, IME changes and on-device edge geometry still require Android runtime checks.

## Tab color-token recheck

- The current [Compose tabs guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/tabs) uses real `Tab` controls inside `PrimaryTabRow` and `SecondaryTabRow`. The pinned `Tab` overload defaults its unselected content color to the selected color, but the pinned primary and secondary navigation-tab tokens separately specify `onSurfaceVariant` for inactive label and icon color. The package's native primary and secondary rows pass that inactive token to `Tab` while preserving caller overrides, matching the component tokens rather than inheriting a generic API default. No color replacement is warranted. Indicator motion, text scaling and TalkBack remain device checks.

## Blank native snackbar action

- The official [`SnackbarHostState.showSnackbar`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/SnackbarHostState) accepts an optional action label and defaults an actionable snackbar to indefinite duration. Passing whitespace through the package bridge previously created an indefinite snackbar with no readable action. The Android entry now trims the label and treats a blank result as absent before queuing or opening the real Compose snackbar. A focused regression reproduced the old payload and passes after the change. Real-device timing and TalkBack remain open.

## Search app-bar navigation logo name

- Google's [Compose accessibility guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults#graphic-elements) requires a localized description for an interactive image and explains that `IconButton` merges its child's semantics. The search app bar could replace its navigation glyph with a logo image whose separate brand description was null or unrelated to the navigation action. That image now carries the already resolved navigation action description, while a logo in the title area still uses its brand description. The capped offline Kotlin compilation passes; TalkBack wording requires a device.

## App-bar overflow icon semantics

- The same [Compose accessibility guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/accessibility/api-defaults#interactive-elements) explains that clickable controls merge their children's semantics. Each app-bar overflow row already renders its localized action name as visible text, so its leading icon is decorative there. The icon now passes a null description in that row, while the icon-only button shown in the app bar retains its action description and tooltip. This avoids supplying the same name from both menu text and icon to the merged row. Capped offline Kotlin compilation passes; actual TalkBack speech and focus order remain device checks.

## Native list-item padding boundary

- The official [Compose `Modifier.padding(PaddingValues)` API](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/foundation/layout/padding.modifier) rejects negative padding. `AdaptiveListTile` resolves caller padding into physical edges before forwarding it to the real Compose `ListItem` or `SegmentedListItem`, but previously accepted a negative edge. A nonfinite edge could also corrupt the Flutter host's initial height. The Android native path now checks that all resolved edges are finite and nonnegative before creating its platform view. A regression failed on the old negative edge; all 15 focused list-item tests pass after correction, including a nonfinite case. Actual Android layout and RTL still require a device.
- The same boundary also exists in the native expansion header's `ListItem` and the state-based native text field. Both now reject negative or nonfinite resolved `tilePadding`/`contentPadding` before sending `PaddingValues` to Compose. Their 76 focused tests passed serially at low priority while the other project's frontend server was idle and memory was 41% free; targeted Dart analysis and formatting also pass. The existing valid RTL padding paths are retained. Device geometry remains open.

## Physical popup anchors in RTL

- The official [Compose `Modifier.offset` reference](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/ui/Modifier) says positive horizontal offsets move left in RTL, while `absoluteOffset` keeps physical rightward coordinates. Flutter supplies physical global `anchorX`/`anchorY` for the long-press context menu and the custom-child anchored popup. Both real native `DropdownMenu` hosts previously applied those coordinates with `offset`, moving the one-pixel anchor to the opposite side under RTL. They now use `absoluteOffset`; the native popup still owns menu placement and transition motion. Source inspection confirms both callers supply physical coordinates. Capped offline Kotlin compilation passes; device RTL placement remains to verify.

## Expansion-header theme padding

- Flutter's official [`ExpansionTile.tilePadding`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ExpansionTile/tilePadding.html) contract uses `ExpansionTileThemeData.tilePadding` when the widget property is null. The native Android header previously resolved only the widget property, ignoring a theme-level inset and reserving the wrong initial height. It now resolves the widget override or theme value before its finite/nonnegative check and forwards the physical edges to the real Compose `ListItem`. A focused RTL test failed on the missing native payload and all 21 expansion tests pass afterward. Targeted Dart analysis and formatting pass; on-device first-frame and large-font geometry remain open.

## Expansion-tile theme colors and body surface

- Flutter's official [`ExpansionTile`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ExpansionTile-class.html) and [`ExpansionTileThemeData`](https://api.flutter.dev/flutter/material/ExpansionTileThemeData-class.html) contracts resolve collapsed and expanded background, title and icon colors from the theme when the widget does not override them. The native Android header now forwards these effective colors to the real Compose `ListItem`, including the title and subtitle fallback. A focused regression failed on the missing collapsed color payload and passes in both states.
- Flutter's own `ExpansionTile` paints its effective state background behind the whole tile, including children. The Compose header previously had that color while the Flutter child subtree remained transparent. The Android host now colors the complete mixed tile; when a custom shape exists, the containing `Material` supplies the same color and clipping. The new child-background regression failed before the fix, and the existing shape regression now checks both state colors. All 23 focused expansion tests pass. On-device color transitions, clipping and TalkBack still need inspection.

## Example app Kotlin toolchain compatibility

- Flutter's [built-in Kotlin migration guide](https://docs.flutter.dev/release/breaking-changes/migrate-to-built-in-kotlin/for-app-developers) recommends enabling AGP 9 built-in Kotlin after migrating the app and its plugins. This plugin's library module already uses AGP 9 built-in Kotlin, so the example was tried with `android.builtInKotlin=true`. The local Flutter 3.47.9 Gradle plugin rejected AGP 9.1.1's bundled Kotlin 2.2.10 as below its 2.2.20 minimum before task execution, reproducing [Flutter issue 192167](https://github.com/flutter/flutter/issues/192167). The example therefore retains `android.builtInKotlin=false` and its declared Kotlin 2.4.20 plugin version while this SDK still rejects the built-in version. `:app:compileDebugKotlin` passes offline with one worker and a 768 MiB heap in that configuration, including its dependency on the package's native module. The example's Dart/Flutter minimums now match the package's 3.12/3.47 contract. This Gradle compatibility choice does not change the real Compose widgets.

## Expansion-header effects spring

- The official [Material motion guide](https://m3.material.io/styles/motion/overview/how-it-works) assigns color changes to effects springs and small component changes to the fast token; Compose's [`MotionScheme.fastEffectsSpec`](https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/material3/MotionScheme) exposes that token. The real Compose `ListItem` expansion header already rotated its arrow with the fast spatial spring, but explicit theme/widget title, subtitle and icon colors changed immediately between collapsed and expanded states. Those three targets now use native `animateColorAsState` with `MaterialTheme.motionScheme.fastEffectsSpec<Color>()`, retaining `ListItem` and caller icon color overrides. Native Kotlin compilation passes with one worker and a 768 MiB heap. The shared background of the Compose header and Flutter children still changes as one step to avoid a temporary color seam; a device check is needed for the text/icon transition and the hybrid surface.

## Native progress corner boundary

- The [Compose shape guide](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/graphics/draw/shapes) uses valid corner geometry for clipping. The package's optional linear-progress `borderRadius` is forwarded as eight doubles and converted to Compose `CornerRadius` values inside a native `GenericShape`; unlike the other native progress dimensions, its edges had no finite/nonnegative guard. `AdaptiveProgressIndicator.linear` now checks each horizontal and vertical radius before creating the real Compose progress view. A widget regression passed NaN, infinity, a negative horizontal radius and a negative vertical radius; it failed before the guard and passes afterward. All 22 focused progress tests pass. This changes only malformed custom clipping input; the real flat/wavy indicators, their default tokens and motion remain unchanged.
